# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1991

## Introduction by Sureśvara dāsa

I open the new Vyāsa-Pūjā book and do what we all do first: look at the pictures of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Prabhupāda is praying, palms pressed. A golden watch ticks the time. A soft light bathes his head. No … the light is coming *from* his head. His eyes are closed, brows turned upward. He is utterly surrendered to Kṛṣṇa.

Prabhupāda is sitting cross-legged on a white sheet playing *mṛdaṅga. Gopī-candana* anoints his forehead. His eyes are slit in concentration, microphones crane nearby. He wears big bright earphones, conjuring up a moonman. His vibrations pierce the universe and Kṛṣṇa listens.

Magnificent Prabhupāda is dancing atop a Ratha-yātrā cart, rose garland flowing, arms upraised, inciting the crowd to chant. He is just about to leap, and so are the trees. The clouds too.

My thumb relaxes and the pages flip by: Prabhupāda on Juhu Beach, Prabhupāda in Red Square; Prabhupāda like thunder, Prabhupāda like a baby; Prabhupāda in a hut, Prabhupāda ten stories up; Prabhupāda fingering beads, initiating disciples, throwing flowers, giving a sweet, giving sauce, his shiny rings, gold tooth, *tilaka, surabhi* lips … what about his words?

Some look on Śrīla Prabhupāda as amazing, some describe him as amazing, and some hear of him as amazing, while others, even after hearing about him, cannot understand him at all.

Here is a picture: September 1972. Śrīla Prabhupāda is in Dallas to install Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandjī. I sit third base from His Divine Grace, who is at home on the *vyāsāsana* chanting verses from the *Brahma-saṁhitā.* Suddenly Prabhupāda needs something. His face turns round the room for help. I know he needs something, but all I can do is sit and grin. Then his glance falls upon me. It is the kindest glance in the world. It says: You’re an idiot. *Jaya,* I grin, I’m an idiot. Śrīla Prabhupāda loves me and I’m an idiot. *Jaya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I need to read this book. Much as I like the pictures, I can see Śrīla Prabhupāda only as far as I’ve followed him, and Lord knows, the devotees who wrote these offerings have followed him much further than I. If I lose myself in their words, I will find myself at his lotus feet. And love his pictures more, too! I’m excited. Let’s read.

Sureśvara dāsa  
Gītā-Nāgarī Village

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Īśvara Swami

“Hey! The king has no clothes!” An innocent child declared the plain truth. Respectable ladies and noble gentlemen, all subjects of the king, had flattered him, perpetuating a farce to win his favor and please their own egos. But when the young child bluntly revealed the truth, they all felt relieved at no longer having to carry the burden of lies. Turning to the naked king, they attacked him with arrowlike glances, as if he alone were guilty.

In today’s world there are many “naked kings” and “foolish subjects.” The performers in the drama may change, but the theme is always the same: a society of cheaters and cheated. We produce and worship some idol because our egos need to be nourished with the image of a superman or god to give us shelter. In addition, we build our public image upon the foundation of vanity and try to sell it in the marketplace of fools, who are all very eager to buy a new leader. Thus history repeats itself and the master becomes the servant, the servant the master.

In this fair of illusions, a genuine and trustworthy leader is hard to find. He must be steadfast and cannot be carried away by the flattery of fools. He must follow the dictates of a higher authority than his own ego or his followers’. In other words, a genuine leader must be a pure devotee of the Lord.

Since he acts according to the will of the Lord, the pure devotee’s commands are devoid of the desire to dominate his followers. He can deliver perfect knowledge because his knowledge comes from the source of all knowledge, the Supreme Being, and he delivers it undistorted. Such a pure devotee discharges his duty of leadership perfectly because he transmits perfect knowledge without personal motives. This is a great soul, a real representative of Kṛṣṇa. Association with him opens the treasure house of all secrets, for only one who is pure and perfect can disseminate real knowledge.

But who is this perfect being? Where can we find this rare soul? Can we find him among ourselves? Is he so cheap? How can we trust anyone if we see mistakes in everyone?

Certainly there must be a pure being in whom we can deposit our faith; otherwise, all the knowledge Kṛṣṇa spoke personally to Arjuna and others would be only theory, and the perfection He talks about simply a phantasmagoria.

But the instructions of Kṛṣṇa are not merely theoretical, nor is perfection a dream. Rather, one who perfectly follows His instructions embodies the perfection He describes and becomes a trustworthy spiritual guide who can transmit His perfect knowledge to his followers. In other words, the miracle of perfection is simply this: to carry out the instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa without deviation. What is required is to strictly follow in the line of the *guru-paramparā*. The *guru* is the messenger, the “postman” who delivers the “mail” without any adulteration. His obligation is magnificent and profound, for he gives us the unadulterated message of Godhead. He is our link with the Supreme. He is the representative of Vyāsa.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva is an empowered incarnation of Kṛṣṇa, and by the power of his transcendental intelligence he wrote down the *Vedas* to benefit future generations. Down to the present day, a bona fide *guru* is considered the representative of Vyāsa, and the anniversary of his appearance day is thus called Vyāsa-pūjā, or “worship of Vyāsa.” The representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva is a qualified guru because he has understood and realized the essence of Vedic knowledge and is strongly determined to deliver the fallen souls rotting in this material world. Therefore he is worthy of the same respect and adoration we accord the Lord Himself.

There is every reason to worship the guru in this way, for he is firmly fixed at the Lord’s lotus feet, far beyond the worldly fanfare of entangled souls. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (*11.17.27) Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself says that we should respect the *ācārya*, or *guru*, as we would Himself, for he is the sum total of all the demigods. Indeed, the *guru* is not a mundane personality. He is the manifestation of the Absolute Truth. Since the Absolute Truth is Kṛṣṇa Himself, the all-merciful Godhead, the *guru* is also an ocean of mercy, and he delivers his followers from the blazing forest fire of material existence.

Those who are truly fortunate recognize the exalted position of the bona fide spiritual master and duly worship him as they would the Lord Himself. Some may think that receiving so much worship and hearing so much adulation will cause the *guru* to deviate from his humble position as a servant of the Lord. But a real *guru* takes all the praise directed at him and delivers it to Kṛṣṇa, the ultimate object of all veneration. Just as the knowledge imparted by the guru descends from Kṛṣṇa via the *guru-paramparā* system, so all praise the guru receives in the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony or at other times goes all the way to Kṛṣṇa via the same disciplic chain. If we do not understand this fact, then we may envy the *ācārya* and think he has trained his disciples to be professional flatterers. No. The guru is a humble and menial servant of Kṛṣṇa. If he didn’t accept worship on behalf of the Lord, the disciplic succession would come to an end and he would be derelict in his duty.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the most empowered successor of Śrīla Vyāsadeva in modern times, showed us the real example of an *ācārya* by strictly following the scriptural injunctions himself, energetically preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and pushing his followers to preach. A true representative of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he encouraged everyone to become a Vaiṣṇava. He will be worshiped throughout the ages as the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, the institution that is saving the world from the most hellish calamity. Thus it is a great benediction to participate in his Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony on the anniversary of his appearance, the most blessed event.

Like the sun, which is always fixed in its own orbit and is never forced to “rise,” the real *ācārya* is never forced to appear in this world. He comes not by obligation, due to the laws of *karma*, but to fulfill Kṛṣṇa’s most cherished desire—to preach His message to the conditioned souls. Being a pure servitor of the Lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda came to this world by his own sweet will. If we follow his instructions and his example, we will receive the priceless benediction of going back home, back to Kṛṣṇa. We will never be cheated by following and worshiping Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā day is thus an event to be revered by all souls throughout the whole universe. Indeed, we are sure that this most auspicious day is celebrated even by Kṛṣṇa Himself in the spiritual world.

Īśvara Swami  
International BBT Trustee

## Homages from the GBC

## Agrāṇi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this ninety-fifth anniversary of your divine appearance within this world.

On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I want to once again thank you very much for kindly continuing to engage me in your precious devotional service, although I am most undeserving. Service to you through the wonderful world-wide society of devotees you have created, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, is to be situated in actual transcendental life, for it purifies one of the effects of unlimited sinful activities and subsequently gives one promise of liberation from material existence and returning home, back to Godhead. What greater benediction could I ever have hoped for or aspired to than simply to be engaged as a humble servant within your *saṅkīrtana* movement?

Frail and neophyte as I am, I need continuous engagement in devotional service. Only then will the deluding effect of *māyā* not overwhelm my mind and divest me of all good sense. Service to your *saṅkīrtana* movement is my only means of devotional service, and therefore it is my lifeline and link to the divine domain of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This I accept without question, because no other activity could please you more than service to your movement for propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Through pleasing you, I will undoubtedly please Kṛṣṇa, through whom all that I desire (and unlimitedly more) in spiritual perfection may be achieved. Due to oppressive conditioning under the modes of material nature, I am apparently taking very slowly to the devotional process, yet I am sure I can, by your grace, become a perfectly pure devotee, just by continued service to your splendid *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Because of you, tremendous good fortune has been evoked for the benefit of the whole world! Taking into account your outstanding service to the *saṅkīrtana* movement, who could possibly be more dear to Lord Caitanya and the *ācāryas* in disciplic succession than you? One can be sure that to whomever you show your mercy, Lord Caitanya’s mercy will quickly follow. And what sincere person could not receive your mercy? One need only chant 16 rounds, follow the four regulative principles, and dedicate the remainder of his life in service to the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Since your blessed appearance within this world, all good fortune has evolved as the benediction moon of your *saṅkīrtana* movement extends its auspicious rays to all directions of the universe. Thus what could be cause for lamentation or anxiety?

Unfortunately, I have every reason to lament and endure anxiety. I lament because I somehow fail to meet the mark of your expectation of me as your disciple of so many years. After such a long time I continue to be stuck in the muck of the bodily conception of life, even though you have given me all means to get out. And I’m in anxiety because I fear that you will one day grow impatient with my lagging indifference to surrender and sacrifice and cast me aside (to again become a nonentity in the terrible ocean of material misery). Certainly by now you must be growing weary of my many apologies and excuses for shortcomings and deviations. You taught that “disciple means discipline,” but I have fallen painfully short of that criterion. Naturally, a disciple should always feel himself a fool in the presence of his spiritual master, but to continuously behave in a foolish and immature way is not becoming of a sincere disciple.

In this sorrowful condition I can only petition you for your continued patience and mercy. Only if you agree may I be granted the strength and determination to continue in your devotional service and, one by one, overcome all the formidable obstacles on the path. Permit me to always feel myself a fool in relationship to you, and just as importantly, please help me to no longer behave like one. I promise to try very hard, but ultimately my hope lies solely on your mercy.

To you, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by whose kindness I have been lifted out of the ocean of material misery and have been maintained thus far within the protective womb of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, I pray for you to kindly keep me engaged all the remaining days of my life in your glorious preaching movement. For in that alone lies my means of deliverance from the bondage of this material world.

Your aspiring servant,  
Agrāṇi Swami

## Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

“My dear King Rahūgaṇa, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth simply by observing celibacy (*brahmacarya*), strictly following the rules and regulations of householder life, leaving home as a *vānaprastha,* accepting *sannyāsa,* or undergoing severe penances in winter by keeping oneself submerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer by fire and the scorching heat of the sun. There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.12.12)

“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.28)

Like the moth which propels itself into the flame,  
For our suffering we have only ourselves to blame.

We are by nature eternal, full of knowledge and bliss,  
Guru, śāstra, and sādhu all confirm this.

Repeatedly succumbing to material desire,  
Such weakness dampens the devotional fire.

Due to suffering, the conditioned souls are loudly crying;  
They can be delivered by your mission if we only keep trying.

Material memories fade with the passage of time,  
But remembrance of Your Divine Grace is something sublime.

Your advice and kind counsel ring in my ears,  
Like a torchlight in darkness, dispelling my doubts and my fears.

The boat is strong and steady, if I’ll only get on board.  
When I’m absorbed in your service, Māyā’s currents are easy to ford.

Māyā and Krsna are existing, side by side,  
But I keep reaching for what I have already tried.

In spite of her promise, all I’m left with is an empty, bitter taste.  
Without spreading Lord Caitanya’s movement, it is all such a waste.

You’ve taken the idle gourd, stick, and string,  
Combined them with Kṛṣṇa’s service and taught them to sing.

The more I try to serve, the more I realize my debt.  
Please bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I’ll never forget.

Trying to be your servant,  
Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet.

During the year, one of the most difficult things is for me to write your Vyāsa-pūjā offering. Every year my heart seems harder, and whatever I can think of seems superficial and of very little significance. All I know is that it was you who saved me from hellish life, and that it is your causeless mercy alone that I’m still allowed to serve you in the association of the devotees.

Even though in the beginning it was very difficult for me to accept, I see it now as your special mercy that I am on the GBC body. Although I feel most unqualified to be on this body, by associating with your senior leaders I am more clearly understanding what you expect from me. I hardly ever had the opportunity to deal with you directly, as many of my Godbrothers did. You always had me work under your senior men, and I was satisfied with this. I’m grateful that this system is still there for me in the form of the GBC, and I pray that you never let me become “independent.”

You have many times explained that we show our love for you as much as we follow your instructions. In making an inventory of my last year’s service, I admit that I have done very poorly. So much more could have been done if I had been more concerned with your desires. So many more books could have been produced and distributed. So much more preaching could have been done to inspire the devotees and to attract new ones. And much more could have been done to develop your many other projects. Instead I have wasted so much time and energy with my personal *anarthas*. I know that I’m falling short of your expectations, but I can only beg you to please give me another chance to improve myself. Please do not lose patience with me, as my service to you is the only thing I have.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your lowly servant,  
Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

## Bhakti Charu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. I wish I could become a speck of dust and remain there forever. Will I ever be able to go back to you and render personal service to you once again?

Although I cannot see you, I know that you are watching me at every moment. You want me to become qualified to go back to the spiritual sky, where you are playing with Kṛṣṇa in His eternal pastimes.

Just as Kṛṣṇa appears as a Deity in order to enable us to see Him with our mundane eyes, you also appeared before us in order to enable us to experience the beauty of the spiritual sky. Through your association, you made us realize the joy of being in the spiritual sky. Allowing us to drink that nectar, you aroused an eternal craving in us to experience that joy endlessly. Now our hearts throb with only one desire: We want to be with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But just some wishful desire will not be enough to gain something so precious. We have to become qualified; we have to learn to make you our life and soul and to follow your instructions with all our existence. It used to be so easy when you were here. You squeezed out whatever little love there was in my stonelike heart and forced me to become obsessed with your thought all the time, especially during those last few months of your final pastime here. By your all-encompassing love, you compelled all of us to desire nothing but your lotus feet.

I remember one of my first meetings with you, in Śrīdhām Māyāpur. You were having a massage on the roof, and I sat at your lotus feet watching your golden form shining more brilliantly than the morning sun. Then you started to instruct me on the futility of material existence. You told me, “Don’t get involved with any woman. This material nature is a prison house of sex desire. Everyone here is tied up with the desire for opposite sex. You never run after that illusion. Just offer this life to Kṛṣṇa.”

I responded, “Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, this life has been offered exclusively to you.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was burning in the blazing fire of material miseries, but you saved me by pouring down the nectar from the ocean of your causeless mercy and extinguishing that fire.

How shall I ever pay back my eternal debt to you? All I can offer is myself. That is my only possession. But that also cannot actually be a payment because by doing that I gain myself. That makes me realize that in the spiritual sky there is nothing to lose but all to gain. I wanted to offer myself to you, thinking that that is how one begins one’s spiritual life, but in return I gained your Divine Grace. I was a beggar without any possession whatsoever, but out of your causeless mercy you made me one of the richest persons just by engaging me in the service of your lotus feet. In spite of all my defects, you allowed me to serve you personally. In spite of all my disqualifications, you allowed me to be in the midst of your most advanced disciples. What good deeds must I have done in the past that endowed me with such good fortune? Some used to say that because I am your youngest son you were therefore showering so much mercy upon me. But I know that I am the most fallen; therefore you showered such kindness upon me.

Now you have made me a leader in ISKCON. I was happy being your irresponsible and carefree youngest son, but all of a sudden I had to become responsible and mature to bear the pleasant burden of your ISKCON. If Kṛṣṇa wants, He can make an ant carry the entire universe; as Kṛṣṇa’s pure representative, you proved that fact to the whole world.

We learned from you that ISKCON is nondifferent from you and that by serving ISKCON we serve you. You were always so concerned about the welfare of ISKCON and asked all your disciples to protect and maintain ISKCON. You warned us that it is only from within that ISKCON can be destroyed—no one will be able to do anything to ISKCON from outside. You saw how your spiritual master’s organization broke down due to some mistake. But still, recognizing ISKCON as his greatest enemy, Kali started his attacks soon after your disappearance. Knowing well that he would not be able to enter ISKCON from the outside, he made his way into the hearts of some of the leading devotees and convinced them that since you were gone they should take over your position. One of them even told me once, “What is so special about Prabhupāda’s spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world? I am also preaching around the world.” Thus Kali provoked them to compete with you and set them up for the most dangerous competition among themselves. Soon the fire of their conflict would have engulfed the entire ISKCON, but their contamination became exposed in the light of ISKCON’s purity. Although saved from the greatest danger, ISKCON was completely unprepared for this attack, and enormous damage was done. Indeed, your ISKCON was about to crumble, but the spirit of unity among your loyal devotees saved ISKCON from that disaster.

Frustrated, Kali then prepared for another attack. He possessed some motivated souls and propagated the theory that no one is qualified to become a *guru,* and that therefore you are the only *guru* in ISKCON and everyone should become initiated by you.

This theory gained a lot of popularity because the faith of most of the devotees had become weak due to the falldown of so many *gurus,* and they swallowed this tempting bait of Kali. Your ISKCON started to rock again in the turbulent ocean of doubt. But your loyal followers remained united and brought the situation under control. Once again Kali was defeated.

I know that in spite of being defeated repeatedly, Kali will keep on launching his attacks against his archenemy, ISKCON. But your valiant soldiers will defeat him without difficulty, as long as they remain together and fight with a spirit of unity. Through these attacks their faith will be tested, and after each victory their conviction in you will become stronger. This is also your divine arrangement to help us make spiritual progress.

Fourteen years ago in Vṛndāvana I thought that you had left us, but now I am convinced that you are still with us, guiding us toward our spiritual goal. Although I cannot see you, I can hear your voice: “Have faith in me and I will take you back to Kṛṣṇa.”

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In a troubled and confused world, you appeared with the light of the B*hāgavata* to enlighten those who are sincere. Most fortunate were those who took shelter of your divine lotus feet. As a *mahā-bhāgavata,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, you poured your divine oceanic compassion upon everyone who came in touch with you. Who can describe your divine glories?

“Prove that God is a person; prove that life is a person,” Your Divine Grace ordered. “Darwin’s forefathers were monkeys; my forefathers were kings and emperors. Prove that Darwin’s theory of evolution is totally wrong,” you said. As your centennial celebration approached, this most unworthy servant is beginning to feel great nervousness because my insignificant attempt to please you may not be very worthwhile. Everything depends on your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, kindly listen to my silent prayer. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

**A Prayer for Forgiveness**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the day of your appearance we can all feel very fortunate that you allowed us some very precious moments of your association through you *vāṇī* and *vapuḥ* manifestations. As year after year passes by we can understand more emphatically how merciful, tolerant, pure, and potent you are. In contrast, we can see how we are often insensitive, erratic, and immature. Please forgive us all for our immaturities and for sometimes going from one extreme to another extreme. Please forgive us also for sometimes minimizing the essence and therefore putting ourselves and others in anxiety. Please help us to increase our ability to remain united as God-brothers, -sisters, -nieces, and -nephews in your family even though we may differ in the propaganda or administrative efforts. Please help us to remain faithful to our vows and to steadily increase our love of all Vaiṣṇavas and our compassion for all fallen souls.

I beg you to forgive me for being such a poor devotee. Sometimes I feel that you and Kṛṣṇa are so far away. If we keep your instructions foremost in our minds, we will never feel that you are absent. Our feeling that Kṛṣṇa is far away is a sign that we do not have deep love for all persons around us, for if we did we would feel Kṛṣṇa’s presence coming to us in so many forms. Please forgive me for trying to be a great man. Sometimes we forget that an actual great man is one who does great things for others. You gave your life showing us how to do great things for others. Shall we follow that example, or shall we exploit it, or shall we keep interpreting it? Both exploiting your mercy and interpreting it are signs of a lack of faith in you and the Vedic autocratic system you transmitted.

You are watching us all. You know what we do in the public and what we do in private. You know our intentions as well as our ulterior motives. You know where our *bhakti* ends and our sense gratification begins. So no one has any secrets to hide; the only secret is that as we surrender, you and Kṛṣṇa reward us accordingly, and as we surrender, we acquire causeless knowledge and detachment. When we act against our *paramparā* system, we remove ourselves from its certain protective shelter, and therefore we once again become the slaves of our senses.

I also ask you to forgive me for being a coward, as I have no desire to be too close to the karmic fallout. Forgive me for being too impotent, as I have no power to help the situation. And most important, forgive me for being so puffed-up that I can even think I have some sense of healthy perception. As this may be the last time the administrative board will allow me to submit an offering to you, I want to thank you for allowing me to make submissions in the past, and I want to thank them for tolerating me as long as they did. Let us all continue to agitate our minds in trying to find ways to serve you, but please keep us all from sabotaging each other—making serious Vaiṣṇava *aparādhas—*and please force us on, despite ourselves, so that when we have to meet up with you again you will be proud of how we represented you, gave shelter to others, kept our vows, distributed your books, and remained faithful to the Vedic conclusion.

Never have I been more happy in my Kṛṣṇa consciousness, never before have I had so much service, never before have I felt such love for the Vaiṣṇava community, and never before have I realized how much of a selfish neophyte I am. However, on this day of the anniversary of your appearance day I beg forgiveness for my weakness and those of my Godbrothers, and I pray that you force us all on to offer a pleasing offering to you, as our time has almost run out.

Begging to become a worthy son,  
Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You mystically appeared in New York, and of those souls looking for magical experiences, you caught the fortunate. And to see you chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa on Hippy Hill in San Francisco—eyes closed, deep, unfathomable face, brow furrowed—was to see a visitor from a different dimension.

Yet you promised us that your process wasn’t high-handed mysticism. It was, rather, practical and scientific. Yet you personally defied your own words.

You didn’t sleep. You traveled across the world to save me by late-night strong words. Yet you arose early, hours before the next morning’s dawn, to translate from the *Madhya-līlā* of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

Nor was your patience of this world. How many incompetent servants did you carry simply out of your compassion? You again and again and again dealt with our doubts and our selfish materialism. You declared that purifying us was like cleaning coal. Yet you continued.

I see this as your devotional compassion. You told me one day in your Manila Intercontinental Hotel room that it was your desire that every living entity within this world should become Kṛṣṇa conscious. And you gave your time and energy and life and heart and privacy and final days—everything—to set the groundwork and example so this would occur.

And your determination. Kṛṣṇa spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā* on the battlefield—of all places—to a warrior—of all people—so that no one in the future could neglect his own duty, claiming it more difficult than Arjuna’s. Similarly you, an “elderly” “Indian” “gentleman,” began ISKCON at seventy and served *guru* and Gaurāṅga with all creativity, fearlessness, boldness, humility, and conviction until your last moment. Now, after knowing of your life, what can we claim as a suitable justification to ever leave our own battlefield?

You also exhibited humanity. We sat in Japan as your secretary read you a letter from a fallen disciple who was acting peculiarly. The secretary read for a few moments and then stopped. “It’s hard to make sense of this part, Prabhupāda. It sounds sort of crazy. Should I read any more?” Your answer: “Yes. Let us enjoy.”

You—with your humanity, determination, patience, compassion, austerity, tolerance, kindness, strictness, fearlessness, faith, humility, boldness, preaching spirit, practicality, and devotion—amaze me! My awe only increases at your mystical prowess. You exhibited devotion and devotional qualities that require one’s being within the spiritual world, at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Yet you did this before our eyes, as we watched and saw you preaching—as you wished us to preach, living as you wished us to live—very much within this world.

I find myself unable to fathom the surface, what to speak of the depth, of your most mystical Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I simply offer you my most humble obeisances and beg you to continue showering this unworthy soul with your blessings. In awe and reverence, but with a soft, loving heart required when approaching one’s father, I pray to always follow behind you, using your footsteps as my guideposts.

To evoke your pleasure, and as an acknowledgement of your mystic success, I would like to offer you—as one offers palmfuls of water to the Ganges—your own words that hint at your secret:

\[Translation:\] The wonderful festival and installation of Śrī Gopālajī was arranged in one day. Certainly all this was accomplished by the potency of Gopāla. No one but a devotee can understand this.

\[Purport:\] If the preachers in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement are sincere devotees of Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa will always be with them because He is very kind and favorable to all His devotees. Just as Arjuna and Kṛṣṇa were victorious in the Battle of Kurukṣetra, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will surely emerge victorious if we but remain sincere devotees of the Lord and serve the Lord according to the advice of predecessors (the six Gosvāmīs and other devotees of the Lord). As Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has stated: *tāṅdera caraṇa sevi bhakta-sane vāsa, janame janame haya ei abhilāṣa*. The Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees must always desire to remain in the society of devotees. *Bhakta-sane vāsa:* they cannot go outside the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society or the movement. Within the society, we must try to serve the predecessors by preaching Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s cult and spreading His name and fame all over the world. If we attempt this seriously within the society, it will be successfully done. There is no question of estimating how this will happen in the mundane sense. But without a doubt, it happens by the grace of Kṛṣṇa.

Your servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

I must thank you for bringing me back to India once again, where your glorious presence and merciful benedictions are revealed more and more progressively. And especially I must thank you for the association of devotees, through whom you reveal yourself and your transcendental gifts.

You said that Bombay is your office. Bombay is also my *prabhu-datta-deśa,* the place given by you to me to serve you. We have to keep your office in order, and we also have to preach. “Similarly I am building this temple for you.” Actually you are the real preacher. We are serving in Bombay only to facilitate your preaching. So we pray to you to give us the vision of how you want to preach there and the intelligence and strength required to realize it.

You said that Māyāpur is your place of worship. Here we worship Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu by the performance of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña.* Here the senior devotees gather to execute the order of the spiritual master. Seeing how seriously the GBC’s and other devotees have taken your instructions and how cooperatively they are endeavoring to execute them is inspiring. I try to serve them as you would want, but I need your guidance. Please reveal your plans for me.

You said that Vṛndāvana is your home. Certainly you are an eternal resident of Vṛndāvana. And we are meant to live there with you. I feel more at home in India than anywhere else, and more at home in Vṛndāvana than elsewhere in India. But I feel completely satisfied at Govardhana. If you have any service for me there, please let me know.

You reveal how one must understand the intention of the spiritual master through your translations and purports:

When Rūpa Gosvāmī fell down like a rod, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu got up and gave him a slap. Then, taking him on His lap, He began to speak to him. “No one knows the purport of My verse,” Caitanya Mahāprabhu said. “How could you understand My intention?” Saying this, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu bestowed various benedictions upon Rūpa Gosvāmī, and taking the verse, He later showed it to Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī with great wonder, Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked him how Rūpa Gosvāmī could understand the intentions of His mind.

\[Purport:\] We had the opportunity to receive a similar blessing from Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī when we presented an essay at his birthday ceremony. He was so pleased with that essay that he used to call some of his confidential devotees and show it to them. How could we have understood the intentions of Śrīla Prabhupāda?

\[Text:\] Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī replied to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “If Rūpa Gosvāmī can understand Your mind and intentions, he must have Your Lordship’s special benediction.” The Lord said, “I was so pleased with Rūpa Gosvāmī that I embraced him and bestowed upon him all necessary potencies for preaching the *bhakti* cult.” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Madhya 1.68-73\]

Those who are blessed to understand the heart of the spiritual master or the Supreme Personality of Godhead can similarly bless others. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī blesses us that we may also understand Lord Caitanya’s mission:

*anarpita-carīṁ cirāt karuṇayāvatīrṇaḥ kalau*  
*samarpayitum unnatojjvala-rasāṁ sva-bhakti-śriyam*  
*hariḥ puraṭa-sundara-dyuti-kadamba-sandīpitaḥ*  
*sadā hṛdaya-kandare sphuratu vaḥ śacī-nandanaḥ*

**“May that Lord who is know as the son of Śrīmatī Śacīdevī be transcendentally situated in the innermost core of your heart. Resplendent with the radiance of molten gold, He has descended in the Age of Kali by His causeless mercy to bestow what no incarnation has ever offered before: the most elevated mellow of devotional service, the mellow of conjugal love” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*** **Ādi 3.4)**

In the same way we can understand you. By association with those who know your intentions, we can also come to know your heart better, and thus please you more fully in our efforts in devotional service. And as you become more and more pleased, you will reveal more and more to us—about yourself, about your service, about your place of residence—everything.

“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who reveals the holy name, the devotes, Lord Caitanya, Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, Rūpa Gosvāmī, Sanātana Gosvāmī, the holy *dhāma,* the residents of the *dhāma,* Girirāja Govardhana, Rādhā-kuṇḍa, Śyāma-kuṇḍa, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and Their associates and service.”

All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your causeless mercy upon all the fallen souls!

Your fallen servant,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

My dear spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of Your Divine Grace’s Vyāsa-pūjā, I beg to offer my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. As I try to properly glorify you by this offering on this most auspicious of all days—the anniversary of your appearance on this planet—our words seem inadequate. So I stop—and try again—and ultimately go on with the understanding that in all my activities, all I can do is try to please you by following your instructions. As soon as I forget your instructions or try to minimize them or deviate from them, I most certainly will encounter a myriad of difficulties.

The success in preaching that you accomplished has never been achieved by any other devotee in history. Within a brief span of eleven years, you opened Kṛṣṇa consciousness centers in every corner of the world and made the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* a household word. Due to your simplicity and purity, everone who met you was attracted to the philosophy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You never resorted to the display of mystic power or magic to fool the mass of people. You never concocted any cheap method of approaching God. You broke down all the barriers and freely distributed the timeless knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone. You showed that regardless of place of birth, everyone can chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You offered the whole world the opportunity to rise from the *mleccha-dharma* platform and enter upon the path of *bhāgavata-dharma.*

Since your departure from this planet, your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has continued to expand, not just maintain. You were the force that started this movement, and you are the force that is giving your disciples and granddisciples courage, strength, and enthusiasm to expand your divine movement. Except for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, there is no hope for suffering humanity. More and more centers are opening, especially in countries that were once under Communist regimes; more and more books are being translated into newer languages; so many diversified preaching activities have started—all due to your spiritual potency.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 14.1) it is said,

*kathāñcana smṛte yasmin duṣkaraṁ sukaraṁ bhavet*  
*vismṛte viparītaṁ syāt śrī-caitanyaṁ namāmi tam*

**“Things that are very difficult to do become easy to execute if one somehow or other simply remembers Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. But if one does not remember Him, even easy things become very difficult. To this Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu I offer my respectful obeisances.”**

These words also fully apply to remembrance of your divine self. When we remember your instructions, everything becomes clear and simple. Therefore I have to dedicate myself to your instructions and help expand the preaching effort. By distributing your books, we are giving the people your association and an opportunity to appreciate your greatness.

You often told us that our love for you will be shown by how we cooperate together to push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. In the Fourth Canto (30.8) of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the Supreme Lord says the following about the Pracetas:

*varaṁ vṛṇīdhvaṁ bhadraṁ vo*  
*yūyaṁ me nṛpa-nandanāḥ*  
*sauhārdenāpṛthag-dharmās*  
*tuṣṭo ’haṁ sauhṛdena vaḥ*

**“My dear princes, I am very pleased by the friendly relationships among you. All of you are engaged in one occupation—devotional service. I am so pleased with your mutual friendship that I wish you all good fortune. Now you may ask a benediction of Me.”**

So on this auspicious day, I, your most fallen disciple, pray that you give me strength to realize that you are always with us and that you are intently watching all our actions. Please give me strength to take shelter of your *vāṇī.* Please make me your fully surrendered puppet and make me an instrument in helping expand your glories.

Your most fallen servant,  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On the occasion of the ninety-fifth anniversary of your appearance in this world, I offer my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are Lord Caitanya’s instrument for delivering the fallen souls from the material energy, which envelops us. This *māyā-sukhāya* so absorbs our attention, giving us the brief illusion of happiness before we die, that no one but a pure devotee like yourself is able to break its spell.

When asked by his father, Yamarāja, what was the most wonderful thing in creation, Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira replied:

*ahany ahani bhūtāni*  
*gacchantīha yamālayam*  
*śeṣāḥ sthāvaram icchanti*  
*kim āścaryam ataḥ param*

**“Every day millions of living entities go to the kingdom of death. Still, those who are remaining aspire for a permanent situation here. What could be more wonderful than this?” (*****Mahābhārata, Vana-parva*** **313.116).**

This means that we foolishly believe that our bubble of material happiness will never burst. The subtle conviction that we shall go on in this body, in this world, forever is very hard to root out.

As Lord Caitanya’s personal representative, you have appeared just for the same purpose as He: to wake up the sleeping conditioned souls. To this end you have brought Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* not only to the shores of America but all over the world. Your transcendental translations and purports of timeless Vedic knowledge have made a true understanding of spiritual life possible. And above all, the memory of your sweet personal association remains this fallen soul’s greatest impetus to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let me continue to serve you and your legacy. Although I am a poor excuse for a servant, this is the only “job title” to which I aspire. Please keep me engaged in your devotional service forever.

Your aspiring servant,  
Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Guru-prasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your Vyāsa-pūjā day is an opportunity to meditate on how you mercifully gave Kṛṣṇa to us. Certainly our humble effort to express our gratitude is insufficient. You sacrificed your entire life to allow us to initiate, cultivate, and ultimately perfect spiritual life, something which we could not even conceive of previously.

Under your perfect guidance your ISKCON society has flourished. After your departure it appeared to the external vision of some that everything would gradually deteriorate. But to one who has complete faith in the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, all is revealed. Now those who have chosen to adhere to your desire to cooperate, even though sometimes difficult, see that your mercy is more readily available. It requires more surrender to follow the orders of the spiritual master in separation. But that separation is felt by those who don’t see how your example continues to polish into rough stones the coallike hearts of persons like myself. You are present as the incessant rainfall of infinite mercy—*trāṇāya kāruṇya ghanāghanatvam*—which is gradually eliminating all the remaining flaws, or *anarthas.* Your instructions, your books, and your pristine example are more meaningful and necessary as the obstacles in devotional life become more apparent.

An unfortunate fool like myself is always avoiding this inundation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy that you have brought. But just as Kṛṣṇa allowed the envious demoness Pūtanā to feed Him, and because of the position she occupied she received His causeless mercy, so you are acting the same way with me. I am disguising myself as a Vaiṣṇava, only looking for name and fame. But you are mercifully surrounding me with your sincere devotees, injecting the *mahā-mantra* into my dull brain, allowing me to be exposed to the most perfect revelation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* etc. Thus I am being dragged along by your causeless mercy. Although I don’t deserve to ask anything, I beg you to continue to give me these opportunities day after day, life after life, and hopefully someday I’ll wake up and try to repay you for one small fraction of what you have given to me. All glories to the shade of your lotus feet.

Your insignificant servant,  
Guru-prasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, the savior of the fallen souls and the inaugurator of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement throughout the world.

Your worldwide vision for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness is perfect. You can see all aspects of the preaching mission and adjust all difficulties to create the perfect situation for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When we analyze your plan for spreading this movement throughout the world, we can see your most wonderful transcendental vision.

The first phase of your plan was to produce your books in all the languages of the world. Book production is going on due to the sincerity of some of your disciples and grand-disciples who have taken this work as their life and soul. By your mercy we have completed producing your books in many languages, and many more are soon to come. We find great bliss in producing your books, and we wish to perform this service for the rest of our lives. Now we are concentrating on the different languages of the Soviet Union. We think it a great mercy that there are many languages there. It is a challenge, but a blissful one. Let the book production go on more and more, and please bless the book producers with your causeless mercy.

After book production, the next stage of your plan is book distribution. What is the use of producing so many books if they are not distributed far and wide with great determination? By your mercy that work is also going on due to the sincerity of your determined book distributors throughout the world. Now your disciples and grand-disciples are distributing these books throughout the Eastern Bloc with great enthusiasm, although just a few short years ago we thought it would never happen. There seems to be no limit to the amount of books that can be distributed in the world. And this mission satisfies the heart and soul and makes us want to serve you more and more by distributing more and more without limit. Please bless the book distributors with your causeless mercy.

Now the third stage of your plan is to produce a transcendental city in Māyāpur based around a wonderful temple. This project is now going on, and it is sure to gain momentum as the planning becomes more intense and complete. Although we are at the first stages of planning, by your blessings we shall be able to complete these plans and begin the actual construction work of the temple and the city. Now we are gradually building up the housing and guest hotels, and this makes us more eager to begin work on the main temples. Please bless us to accomplish this stage of your work within our lifetimes so that we will have your great legacy to hand down to the generations of devotees to come.

I want to be part of these historic efforts to accomplish your mission. You have one great mission, the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, with many parts, each perfect in itself. My fervent desire is to be part of this mission. Please bless me to be able to serve you throughout my life in this way. Please always grant me in this life constant and uninterrupted devotional service. I just want to serve you more and more, and I never want to be idle. Please engage me in your loving devotional service eternally.

Thank you for establishing your worldwide mission and engaging me in it.

Your eternal servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Harivilāsa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the Preface to your *Nectar of Devotion* you have written that every living entity has a natural propensity to love. “No one can live without loving someone else.… Even an animal like a tiger has this loving propensity, at least in a dormant stage, and it is certainly present in the human beings. The missing point, however, is where to repose our love so that everyone can become happy.”

You go on to explain that when the living entity is born he begins to love his mother and father, then his brothers and sisters. Gradually he loves his uncles, aunties, cousins, and other members of his extended family. This loving propensity can expand to the society, race, nation, and even to all human beings. But the person remains unfulfilled until he knows who is the supreme beloved. Our love can only be fully satisfied when it is directed to Kṛṣṇa.

There is a nice example. If a stone is thrown into a pond, a circle is formed that gradually expands. Similarly, every person naturally tries to expand his loving propensity. Without information of Kṛṣṇa, however, the loving propensity remains limited within a certain circle. Therefore, we are never satisfied.

In this age of hypocrisy it is not even certain that our natural propensity for love can develop. In the material world we love someone for getting something in return. We call this love, but it is really selfishness. There are many people who are selfish. They frustrate sincere persons in their loving relations. Sincere seekers become embittered by failed love, and their soul’s natural loving propensity is thwarted. Sometimes people keep cats and dogs because they must love someone.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taught us who the supreme beloved is, what real love is, and how to awaken our natural dormant love for Kṛṣṇa. I do not see anyone as merciful as you within the three worlds because you have freely distributed the secret of fulfillment of our yearning for love and happiness. You have very simply stated this most precious secret. If we learn from the *ācāryas* how to love Kṛṣṇa, then it is very easy to immediately and simultaneously love every living being. It is like drawing a circle with a compass. To do so one must begin by placing the pointed stem on a central point. Then many concentric circles can be drawn. Similarly, you have taught us that if we make Kṛṣṇa the center, then all people can harmoniously develop their circle of love without conflict or frustration.

By developing our natural love for Kṛṣṇa, we become fulfilled persons because our love has reached the supreme beloved. *Premā pum-artha mahān:* “Pure love of God is the ultimate goal of human life.” Yet there is something further to be said. Lord Śiva tells Goddess Durgā, “My dear Devī, although the *Vedas* mention worship of the demigods, the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is topmost and is ultimately recommended. However, above the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the rendering of service to Vaiṣṇavas, who are related to Lord Viṣṇu.” Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your most holy Vyāsa-pūjā day is the best opportunity for everyone to fulfill his heart’s yearning for love. *Sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstraiḥ:* “The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord.” By expressing our love for you, we come closer to Kṛṣṇa than is conceivably possible because you are so dear to Him.

Today especially we come together to glorify your priceless achievements. You have delivered the message of Lord Caitanya exactly according to the order of your spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda. You have written on this point: “It is a fact that by serving the spiritual master one becomes free from material life and makes spiritual advancement. I have not done anything personally very wonderful. I am simply serving my spiritual master, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati Goswami Maharaja, and all the *ācāryas* in the disciplic succession. If I have done anything of credit it is that I have not changed their teachings, I have not added anything of my own interpretation” (Letter to Bhīma dāsa, May 16, 1974).

Simply carrying the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is, without adulteration, entitles one to be a bona fide spiritual master. This is the test for determining who is a real spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have said, “He \[the bona fide spiritual master\] is not playing with the message of Kṛṣṇa in order to introduce himself, his person. He is simply presenting the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is. Then he’s spiritual master. Nobody else” (Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* Vṛndāvana, Oct. 31, 1972).

I feel very satisfied and happy when I hear your transcendental words. By following your instructions, my anxieties of life go away. My appreciation and love for you is born from this relief. There is a burning sensation inherent in material life. As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sings in his *Gītāvalī:*

*e-viṣaya-halāhale divā-niśi hiyā jvale*  
*mana kabhu sukha nāhi pāya*

**“Day and night I feel a burning sensation from the poison of sense gratification, and thus my mind cannot find any happiness or satisfaction.”**

Your mercy is so powerful that it has extinguished the blazing fire of anxiety within my heart. I feel happy and secure with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I want to dedicate all my energies to your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world for the benefit of all living entities.

Once when I accompanied you to the airport in Paris for your departure, I was fortunate to sit next to you and speak personally with you. I wanted to be very close to you, so I foolishly sat next to you on an airport bench and asked a question. “Why did the Pāṇḍavas experience so much trouble? Kṛṣṇa was their personal friend and relative. Why did they suffer so much?” You replied that Kṛṣṇa’s devotees are always satisfied simply by serving Him. Whether in happiness or distress, Kṛṣṇa’s devotees are simply satisfied by devotional service because through service the devotee always sees Kṛṣṇa. Then you gave me your garland and said, “Distribute the flowers.” I was somewhat perplexed. Then another devotee, Yogeśvara Prabhu, said, “Come on, Śrīla Prabhupāda wants us to distribute his garland to the people.” Under your merciful glance, we distributed the flowers to the people in the airport. “Hello, sir, please take a flower. It is so nice. It’s a gift from my spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He is sitting over there. Please take it, it’s his mercy.”

“Oh, thank you, it smells very good.”

The people were smiling, you were smiling, I was smiling. Yes, I understand that the devotee is simply satisfied by devotional service because through the service he can experience your loving mercy and the presence of Kṛṣṇa.

*Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi:* “By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master one cannot make any advancement.”

By always trying to distribute your merciful garland of books and instructions, I will retain the vision of your smiling face in my heart. I am fondly remembering your sitting on the airport bench and nodding approvingly of the distribution of your mercy. I pray that I may always distribute your mercy in the company of the multitudes of devotees who are sincerely serving your mission.

Viśāla Prabhu once related a very important exchange that he had with Your Divine Grace. He went into your room in the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple compound in Vṛndavana, desiring that you name his newborn daughter. After offering obeisances he said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have heard from my Godbrothers and gathered from reading your books that you are going to leave \[the planet\] soon. Before you leave this world, could you please give my daughter a spiritual name.”

You replied, “Oh, my spiritual master has never left me!” Viśāla Prabhu was impressed by your answer. He realizes more and more the deep meaning of your words as each day passes. You are always standing next to your devotees, ready to guide them, especially if they are actively serving your mission. You have written, “If a disciple is constantly engaged in carrying out the instructions of the Spiritual Master, he is … constantly in company with his spiritual master. This is called *vaniseva,*… executing the instructions.… So in the absence of the physical presentation of the Spiritual Master, the *vaniseva* is more important. My Spiritual Master, Sarasvati Gosvami Thakur, may appear to be physically not present, but still because I try to serve His instruction I never feel separated from him” (Letter to Karandhar dāsa, August 22, 1970).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your presence evokes the feeling and vision of the spiritual world. Whenever Kṛṣṇa or Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee is present, Goloka Vṛndāvana is manifested. Wherever a pure devotee lives, he creates the atmosphere of Vṛndāvana by his devotional service. Śrī Advaita Prabhu said to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “Wherever You are, O Lord, there is Vṛndāvana.” On a tape recording I heard you say, “I am sitting in your New York City, but I am not here. I am always thinking of Vṛndāvana. I do not take your American culture.”

When I was on a morning walk with you in Paris, I personally had a glimpse of your Vṛndāvana vision. We were walking up the sidewalk of a slow-rising hill in a Paris suburb. The morning air was fresh, and the streets were beginning to buzz with commuter cars. You were striding briskly in front of me when suddenly you stopped. I almost ran into you. Your cane was touching the ground and your head was slightly raised and shaking gently. Your eyes seemed to be closed, and you were in deep contemplation. I was surprised and expectant. My mind was trying to grasp what was happening. As the seconds passed and seemed to get longer and longer, I felt the air electrifying. I was wondering why the commuters were rushing to work with so much determination when Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee was standing in their midst. I felt so fortunate to be able to appreciate your transcendental presence and be so intensely waiting to understand what you were contemplating. Then you began to speak slowly: “Oh, this breeze is so nice. It is just like Vṛndāvana. So nice.” Your head was shaking softly, and your words were deep and slow. Time stopped; my breathing also. I was no longer aware of anything else but Your Divine Grace and the soft wafting of a pleasant breeze. I realized that you are not in this world or of this world. You are perpetually in Vṛndāvana with Kṛṣṇa.

By your mercy I was glimpsing the spiritual world in a Paris suburb. I never was so conscious before of a pleasant breeze or how it was related to Kṛṣṇa. My senses were so gross and my mind so materialistic that I could never be reminded of Kṛṣṇa and Vṛndāvana by a gentle breeze. But your presence revealed the spiritual world even to a gross fool like myself. That moment and the feeling I experienced are forever in my mind, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I can only try to thank you over and over again.

cakhu-dān dilo yei janme janme prabhu sei  
divya-jñān hṛde prokāśito

“He who has given me the gift of transcendental vision is my lord, birth after birth. By his mercy divine knowledge is revealed within the heart.”

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, the friend of the poor and the savior of the devotees!

Your humble servant,  
Harivilāsa dāsa

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

This year, O master, I bring you a song and a prayer. The bright song is of your own making, for it is the glories of your divine mission, growing and reaching into the depths of many millions of hearts. The prayer is a fervent wish that this fallen place called America be allowed by Your Grace to train hundreds and thousands of its brightest children and send them forth in a great second coming of *saṅkīrtana,* chanting the holy names, and especially distributing the true touchstone of the age, your divine books. My prayer ends with a humble plea, that you bless me to serve your servants and their servants, all those who sport in the waters of this second wave.

Falling again and again at your lotus feet, I remain,  
Your hopeful servant,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Īśvara Swami

All glories, all glories to the beautiful *ārati* ceremony of Śrīla Prabhupāda. This *guru-pūjā* is taking place in each and every ISKCON center of the whole world and is attracting the minds of all the devotees.

On Śrīla Prabhupāda’s right is a devotee fanning him with a *cāmara,* and on his left is someone with a peacock fan. Down the hall, hundreds of fortunate souls are dancing, jumping up and down, and loudly singing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has sat down on a beautiful *vyāsāsana,* and all the demigods are coming to pay their homage.

Many, many devotees are present, and each of them is engaged in a particular service during this wonderful worship. One is doing the *pūjā,* another is fanning, another is applying *candana,* someone else is offering a garland, another is leading the sweet *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma* prayers, while many others are responding to the *kīrtana*. One after another, all the devotees offer some flowers at Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet and then bow down in complete humility before him, the personification of magnanimity.

Conchshells, bells, and *karatālas* resound, and the *mṛdaṅgas* play very sweetly and rhythmically. This *kīrtana* is supremely blissful and relishable to hear.

The golden brilliance of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s face conquers millions upon millions of suns, and the garland of fresh jasmine, roses, and gardenias around his neck shines.

All his faithful disciples are there, and their voices are choked with the ecstasy of transcendental love.

This is the vision of the most blissful event of the day: Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *guru-pūjā*! And this is taking place every day in hundreds of cities around the world.

This *pūjā* is eternal, full of *rasa,* full of bliss, and full of *śakti.* All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s daily *guru-pūjā,* which will be performed in this world as long as there is a fortunate soul who can understand his glories. This ceremony should go on millennium after millennium. It is the most relishable event, and it transcends past, present, and future.

And all glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s yearly *vyāsa-pūjā* ceremony. We see devotees coming from all over to join in this most blessed event. They are all ecstatic, and they dance blissfully for the pleasure of His Divine Grace.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s movement, his instructions, and his desires. Like an expert maestro, he is making everyone sing and dance according to his will.

I pray that I will always be able to offer not only flowers but also my body, mind, and words at the feet of the highest personality the world has ever seen—His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda! I wish I may participate in his daily *guru-pūjā* for many millennia to come. I hope I can be of some service for the pleasure of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Īśvara Swami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**The Greatest Alchemist**

Alchemy: working on changing the gross,  
Transmuting it to something refined,  
Iron to gold? Or darkness to light!  
Rarely achieved but vital to life.

Greatest of mysteries unveiled by you,  
Something that never before could ensue.  
Giving la chiave to all who approach,  
One such as I can even come through.

Slighting the treasure laid at its door,  
Still mankind lingers in rapt delusion.  
But soon, by your mercy and that of the Lord,  
The world will ignite in transformation.

Your eternal servant,  
Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

Who can appreciate enough the causeless mercy Your Divine Grace has bestowed on the conditioned souls of the Western world? Without your causeless mercy most of the world wouldn’t have known the sublime existence of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, and His most merciful form as Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your Divine Grace has given the way of life by which any human being can become perfect, realizing the summum bonum of life.

The Vedic authorities declare that the debt a disciple owes the spiritual master is so great that even for being taught one syllable of a transcendental *mantra,* the disciple can never fully repay the *guru* even in millions of births. Your Divine Grace has given us so much—then how much greater is our debt! Our every thought, word, and deed for the rest of our existence belong to Your Divine Grace.

When Your Divine Grace allowed us to serve your *vapuḥ,* we didn’t know how to take care of or serve the spiritual master. Like small children offering insignificant presents to the loving father, we offered many facilities to Your Divine Grace that were actually substandard and grossly inadequate for facilitating your devotional service. Still, as our loving spiritual father you accepted the humble offerings we made. If only we could have facilitated your service better. If only we had been expert to fulfill your desires quickly and sufficiently.

Your Divine Grace was offered two cramped rooms in Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir’s Lotus Building—rooms where Your Divine Grace preached to government leaders, dignitaries, spiritual heads, and countless disciples and spiritual aspirants. The facility was soon outgrown and inadequate for the great works Your Divine Grace was undertaking. The snores of your secretaries and slamming doors would disturb Your Divine Grace. Finally Your Divine Grace sanctioned the plan to build you a personal residence near the *pukur* in Māyāpur, but even after years it was never started—what to speak of completed. Out of Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy you gave us another chance to fulfill this desire by instructing us to build your *puṣpa-samādhi* in Māyāpur at the site of your approved residential quarters. The *samādhi* is Your Divine Grace’s permanent memorial residence at Śrīdhām Māyāpur!

Yet we are such worthless servants that even after so many years we haven’t completed your *samādhi* in Māyāpur. Lack of foresight and planning catch us unprepared as new obstacles present themselves at every juncture. When will we be able to offer Your Divine Grace the facility you desired? The worship that Your Divine Grace deserves?

Your Divine Grace wanted a road to the River Ganges so that you could bathe every day—you said it would extend your life span. What we couldn’t do, the Ganges has made possible by coming closer—now we have your road to the Ganges. Śrīla Prabhupāda, if only we could accompany Your Divine Grace now for a daily bath in the sacred Ganges!

Your Divine Grace established the foundation stone and Ananta Śeṣa Deity for the Pañca-tattva Temple and Vedic Planetarium at Śrīdhām Māyāpur. Now all the GBC’s, ISKCON managers, and devotees around the world are united cooperatively to complete this temple as quickly as possible, and to develop the spiritual city of Śrī Māyāpur as per Your Divine Grace’s and the previous *ācāryas’* desires.

Left to ourselves, we would toddle along at our usual pace. Your Divine Grace made us realize that with your transcendental mercy we could achieve, and should achieve, far more than we had ever dreamed we were capable of. Your pure love for Lord Kṛṣṇa is the inspiration for the whole world to enthusiastically serve the Lord. Your Divine Grace is the driving force compelling us, out of love, to achieve new heights and realize greater goals than we ever conceived possible.

Now we need Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy more than ever to serve your *vāṇī* and fulfill the mission you have given us. As Your Divine Grace would constantly remind us of the goals you wanted us to achieve, we need your mercy to responsibly remind ourselves of every aspect of the mission you want us to fulfill. We need the *antaryāmī* to remind us—to maintain our concentration and focus on the priorities and objectives that Your Divine Grace has given us. Your Divine Grace would constantly evaluate our progress and praise us for work well done, and admonish us for works behind schedule or imperfectly done. Kindly bless us to always remember your divine will.

When, by your divine grace, will we be empowered to evaluate and to push ourselves to fully achieve the results you want? When will we be able to fully inspire countless others to also take up your divine mission and expand the *saṅkīrtana* movement unlimitedly? This year Your Divine Grace’s ISKCON Governing Body Commission has embarked upon the ambitious program of making their meetings and activities “goal-oriented.” Everything is possible with Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy. In addition, we simply need to enthusiastically and faithfully serve your transcendental *vāṇī*!

This year the twenty-fifth anniversary of ISKCON (the International Society for Krishna Consciousness) is being observed, and in 1996 Your Divine Grace’s centenary appearance celebration will also be observed. Let us take this opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace! When will every resident of Bhāratavarṣa know your name and recognize your form? When will every house be adorned with your transcendental *mūrti*? When will the leaders of the world accept Your Divine Grace as the savior of the world?

On this sacred Vyāsa-pūjā day we offer our prostrated obeisances at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet again and again. We reconfirm our surrender to your lotus feet. Please forgive us for any offenses committed knowingly or unknowingly. Please don’t allow us to wander off from the shelter of your lotus feet, but keep us always under their cooling shade! Please always keep us in the shelter of your transcendental ISKCON body, and in the association of pure devotees dedicated to Your Divine Grace’s service. All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servitor,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Every year the evidence of your greatness is manifested more and more, and more devotees are writing you homages, so the task becomes more difficult and yet more sublime.

You have revealed the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* which gives us the unlimited ocean of spiritual *rasas*. The first chapter explains the spiritual masters. This Vyāsa-pūjā is meant for remembering and glorifying the spiritual master, and by this we can hope to gain further realization into the transcendental nature and character of the spiritual master.

*yadyapi āmāra guru—caitanyera dāsa*  
*tathāpi jāniye āmi tāṅhāra prakāśa*

**“Although I know that my spiritual master is a servitor of Śrī Caitanya, I know Him also as a plenary manifestation of the Lord.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*** **Ādi 1.44)**

“Every living entity is essentially a servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and the spiritual master is also His servant. Still the spiritual master is a direct manifestation of the Lord. With this conviction, a disciple can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa because he is a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa …”

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāśtrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is non-different from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi*** **1.45).**

“The relationship of a disciple with his spiritual master is as good as his relationship with the Supreme Lord. A spiritual master always represents himself as the humblest servitor of the Personality of Godhead, but the disciples must look upon him as the manifested representation of Godhead.”

By your causeless mercy we can understand the philosophy of *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva,* simultaneously one and different. The Māyāvādīs think that the *guru* is God, and the *guru* foolishly believes also that he is God. But you never fall into such illusion, and you are teaching everyone that we are all servants of God.

The phenomenal growth of your International Society of Krishna Consciousness shows that your relationship with your spiritual master is most perfect. You perfectly demonstrated the potency of following the order of the spiritual master. As your disciples we must learn this art from you. Many are following your orders, especially in the matter of printing and distributing your books. The world is changing due to the ecstatic effulgence of your books. In more and more languages they are being received and read by people all over the world. I cannot understand fully, but anyone can see that this is good for the world: *niḥśreyasāya lokasya dhanyaṁ svasty-ayanaṁ mahat:* “It is meant for the ultimate good of all people, and it is all-successful, all-blissful and all perfect.”

Your Bhaktivedanta purports are your “emotional ecstasies,” and no one can estimate the good fortune of anyone who reads them. And even greater is the fortune of those who distribute them. You write, “Lord Caitanya’s private secretary, Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, advised all intending visitors who came to see the Lord at Purī to make a study of the self-realized bona fide spiritual master, and through him only can one understand the lessons of *Bhāgavatam* in order to receive the desired result. One can derive from the study of the *Bhāgavatam* all benefits that are possible to be derived from the personal presence of the Lord. It carries with it all the transcendental blessing of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa that we can expect from His personal contact.”

Your have made this sublime opportunity available to everyone. Your book distribution is the perfect manifestation of your genius. Please let me become absorbed in this and thus establish a relationship with you.

Your servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Kīrtirāja dāsa

Śrīla Prabhupāda is our master, father, and friend,” Brahmānanda Prabhu once told me. In one person we can repose all our feelings, and with that one most spiritual personality we can perfect these three relationships, without any contradiction.

**R**ight Śrīla Prabhupāda always was, no matter what he was talking about. His knowledge knew no bounds, for he is a direct representative of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa.

**I**SKCON is my body,” Śrīla Prabhupāda said. To serve ISKCON and the devotees in ISKCON is thus nondifferent from rendering personal service to Śrīla Prabhupāda. ISKCON is a manifestation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotion and dedication to the instructions of his Guru Mahārāja.

**L**ove for Kṛṣṇa is the only real love, Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us. That which is thought to be love in this material world is only lust.

**A**bsolute is sentient, thou has proved, Impersonal calamity thou hast removed,” wrote Śrīla Prabhupāda in his Vyāsa-pūjā offering to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda.

**P**ure love of Godhead was what Śrīla Prabhupāda made available to us all, through constant practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**R**ādhārāṇī’s lotus feet are cherished by great devotees like Śrīla Prabhupāda; therefore he remains sheltered at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

**A**ssociation with Śrīla Prabhupāda is always available. One can feel his presence as long as one remains absorbed in remembering Prabhupāda and as long as one is surrounded by Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotees.

**B**alarāma and Kṛṣṇa played and let Their cows and calves eat the soft grasses in Ramaṇ Reti. In Ramaṇ Reti Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us a temple, where he allowed us to engage in Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma’s service.

**H**eavy is a quality of a *guru,* and Śrīla Prabhupāda was certainly heavy when he needed to be. When we deviated, he corrected us in a heavy way, but we always knew that there was love and concern for us when Śrīla Prabhupāda was heavy.

**U**nder Māyā’s grip we would have remained, had it not been for Śrīla Prabhupāda. But if we do not stick to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda at every moment, Māyā can get us under her spell once again.

**P**raying to Śrīla Prabhupāda, I ask him to please forgive me for all the offenses I’ve committed in my feeble attempt to serve him.
