**A**nd I ask all those sincere followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda to for-give me for all the offenses I’ve committed to them, so that I can go on with my service to our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda.

**D**esiring Śrīla Prabhupāda’s company, his smile, his razor-sharp words, and his transcendental form, I pray to be fixed at his lotus feet.

**A**lways longing to be somewhere near him, to see his swanlike form, and to hear the nectar emanating from his lotus mouth once again, I pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda that someday, through his causeless mercy, I may live with him once again, but this time eternally.

His worthless servant,  
Kīrtirāja dāsa

## Loka-sākṣī dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It is said that the Vaiṣṇava’s only business is delivering the fallen souls. Very unhappy to see the suffering of the poor souls, the Vaiṣṇava, not caring for his own well-being, tries to deliver them. This you did for us when you left your *bhajana* in the heaven of the Rādhā-Dāmadora temple just to relieve us of our suffering brought on by our callous pride and ignorance.

As you poured your mercy upon us fallen brutes, your transcendental qualities were manifested:

*nārāyaṇa-parāḥ sarve na kutaścana bibhyati*  
*svargāpavarga-narakeṣv api tulyārtha-darśinaḥ*

**“Devotees solely engaged in serving the Supreme Lord, Nārāyaṇa, never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation, and the hellish planets are all the same, for such devotees are interested only in the service of the Lord.”**

In the service for Lord Nityānanda, you always showed your *abhaya* nature. Going to preach in any and every place, never fearing any condition of life, you never minded if it was the hell of New York or the heavenly planets. For you, all places were the same. You had Kṛṣṇa and you saw that everyone was suffering without Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And today you continue to give Kṛṣṇa to everyone, because you have Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa is yours.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please save this wretched rascal. Make him pure and fearless enough to help you in your mission of love.

Your useless but eternal servant,  
Loka-sākṣī dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Twenty-five years ago you kindly initiated me, but in terms of spiritual development I feel like a small child, even though I’m sometimes referred to as a “senior devotee.” My spiritual life, it seems, is always just beginning.

The quarter century of attempting to serve you has no meaning unless I can become a responsible, surrendered servant, displaying at least some of the qualities of a *sādhu*. I pray to become worthy to engage in the service of your servants to fulfill your transcendental ambition of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

I’m continually amazed and sometimes overwhelmed by realizing how much I have to learn, how much further I have to go, to become worthy of your grace. Day by day, it seems, I discover more impurities.

Sometimes I fantasize, “If only I could turn back the clock and retain what I know now. My devotional career could be more successful. With what I’ve learned from all my experience, I could make a fresh start and serve you with real devotion.” But then I realize that I’d only make as many new mistakes as I made old ones, still trying to shed, layer by layer, the thick curtain of *māyā* that surrounds me.

Serving your lotus feet is a happy but long course. The goal, to become pure, seems far away. Only if I can cling tightly to your lotus feet is there hope.

As your movement grows and matures, great responsibilities fall upon many of your early disciples as we try to keep pace. Greater demands seem to come from all sides, and we tend to demand a lot of your newer, younger servants. But perhaps this is as it should be—as long as we always demand more from ourselves—as the living entity’s essential *dharma* is to constantly serve Lord Kṛṣṇa.

I see many great Vaiṣṇava souls among ISKCON’s new devotees, whether in the temple or living outside the temples. How difficult it is to judge the merits of a Vaiṣṇava by outward appearance!

I remember when Gaurasundara and Govinda dāsī became your personal servants immediately after they joined the San Francisco temple in 1967. “Raw recruits … Texans,” we thought to ourselves. One devotee questioned why these newcomers had been given such an exalted position at your lotus feet. Wasn’t it premature, inappropriate? You answered, “They have undergone many penances and austerities in their past lives to have earned this position. You shouldn’t criticize.”

Your movement draws Vaiṣṇavas out of every part of every culture of human society. And you constantly drive us out into the field to find more. You once told us Vaiṣṇavas are standing on every street corner, and that it was our duty simply to find them. It is your energy only, fully supported by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desires, which has created a worldwide community of devotees. One day I hope to be worthy to represent that mission.

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Srila Prabhupada,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Another year has gone by, and I find myself once again sitting in front of the computer wondering how this incapable fool will ever be able to express, with some realization, the glories of your transcendental personality. I know that I lack the surrender necessary to expand your glories, and that’s why each year I sit, sometimes even for hours, searching for a way to express what a disciple should be able to express if he is actually surrendered.

I confronted this same problem yesterday, when I was about to sit down for my first attempt to write this homage to you. Since only two days remained before the deadline for this offering, I was feeling pressured by time, and therefore I wanted to take full advantage of the available hour Kṛṣṇa provided me to start collecting my thoughts. Just then, the temple president of Montreal came into my room and asked if I would take a few minutes to meet a guest who had come to the temple for a brief visit. He said that this guest was a retired police commissioner from Montreal, and it would be good if he could meet an older devotee. I was hoping that Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami would meet with him instead of myself, since I considered him far more qualified than I, but he had another commitment elsewhere and was just preparing to leave. With so little time left to write my offering, I was reluctant, but since it seemed that he was not an ordinary guest, I relented after being assured that it would take only a few minutes.

All three of us walked to the front room of the temple, and the gentleman stood to receive both Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami (who was on his way out) and myself. They spoke briefly in French, and then Mahārāja had to excuse himself to make his appointment.

The moment that Mahārāja left, this gentleman started talking to me, and very soon it became apparent that he was going to continue to do most of the talking. It was a little unexpected, since I was under the impression that he wanted to ask me a few questions. But since he was speaking favorably, I didn’t intrude. He didn’t take long to reveal his disposition, though. He wanted to talk about you.

He said that he had read your *Bhagavad-gītā* thirty-two times, and then he went on to describe why he felt your translation and purports were superior to all others. He said that he never goes anywhere without his little black *Bhagavad-gītā*. “As soon as there’s a spare moment, I pull it out and start reading from it,” he said. “Sometimes when my wife’s doing errands, she gets carried away and comes out of the store hours later, with so many apologies for taking so long. ‘Don’t worry,’ I tell her, ‘I’ve got my *Bhagavad-gītā* here. You can take all the time you want.’”

He went on to describe how he would also read your *Bhagavad-gītā* every night. “Someday I’ll probably stop counting how many times I’ve read it,” he said, “because I’ll just lose track. I don’t think I’ll ever stop reading Prabhupāda’s *Gītā*. The philosophy totally enlivens me. There’s no book like it in the world.”

Then he started telling me a little bit about himself. “When I think back to what I was doing when Prabhupāda was here in Montreal…” he said. “If I only I had taken the time to come to the other side of the city to meet him, things probably would have been a lot different for me.” He described some of the things he was doing with the police force, but soon he refocused on you and said how you had influenced his life anyway. “If I had only met Prabhupāda personally,” he said, “I think he would have fully converted me right then and there.” Then on he went to describe some of your pastimes from the *Līlāmṛta,* his eyes sparkling with delight as he described each pastime in full detail. This man seemed to be totally captivated by you. I stood motionless in the hallway for at least thirty minutes, listening attentively to everything he had to say. Then suddenly he realized that he had to go pick up his wife, so we moved closer to the front door. “You know, my wife knows a lot about Prabhupāda, too,” he said. “She always hears about him from me.” Then on he went again, telling me how he and his wife sit and read *Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* together and how they discuss your pastimes.

Another ten or fifteen minutes went by, and then he shook my hand once again, thanking me for my time, and wished me all success in my spiritual pursuits. I looked at my watch and realized that my available hour was practically consumed. I didn’t regret it, though. It was an enlivening experience. I began to think how fortunate this man was to have such a taste for talking about you. Undoubtedly, you have already begun clearing his path back to Godhead. If he doesn’t become “fully converted” in this life, I can’t see how it can be very long before he is.

Although I didn’t realize it at the time, I can now understand that Kṛṣṇa was trying to help me. Lacking the realization necessary to put something in writing, He took me away from the computer and tried to give me a glimpse of your glories so that I wouldn’t waste that hour just staring into the screen. It’s inconceivable, but today it seems as though this man practically composed this whole Vyāsa-pūjā homage for me. Although I’m still lacking in realization, somehow I, a blind fool, have had my eyes pulled open again for another glimpse of your exalted position. Just yesterday this man was expounding your glories because he had so much of your transcendental association by reading your books. I can understand that your books must be changing the destiny of an incalculable number of souls all over this planet. If one gets one moment’s association with you, he can start on his way back home, back to Godhead.

On this most auspicious day of the anniversary of your appearance in this world, I humbly beg you to please allow me to continue to serve you eternally. As long as I’m here in this material world, please don’t ever let me forget what is most pleasing to you—to preach and distribute your books. Please help me to be pure and to be instrumental in giving your association to others who are forgetful of their eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Although I’m not worthy or even capable of performing any of these activities, I beg you to please let me continue to try. Only by your divine mercy can I have even the slightest success in this endeavor.

Your eternal servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your transcendental glories are spreading,  
At the helm of ISKCON, ever blissfully expanding,  
The world the holy name is swiftly overflooding,  
All souls the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is uniting.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books in Kali-yuga are shining,  
Just as the bright sun rays are darkness dispelling,  
The fallen souls to Kṛṣṇa they’re perfectly guiding,  
Let’s distribute more and more—to you ‘t will be pleasing.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your temples are steadily increasing,  
As years go by, the BTG more addresses is listing,  
Like oases in the desert for conditioned souls thirsting,  
Where they can hear the nectar of Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your restaurants are always distributing  
Prasādam, Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, so deliciously tasting.  
First control the tongue, then other senses will be following,  
Thus fix the mind on Kṛṣṇa, all illusion overcoming.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your festivals are one and all attracting,  
Especially Ratha-yātrā, everywhere we’re celebrating,  
Lord Jagannātha’s transcendental form is so amazing  
As He rides atop his chariot, with giant wheels rolling.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your gurukulas are young Vaiṣṇavas training  
To carry forth the mission of your worldwide preaching.  
We must set the example, which for them will be inspiring,  
To give their lives to Kṛṣṇa, ISKCON’s future securing.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your farms are made for simple living,  
And following varṇāśrama-dharma that’s based on Kṛṣṇa serving.  
These spiritual communities are meant for higher thinking,  
To elevate our consciousness in natural surroundings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your Deities around the world are smiling,  
Balarāma, Lord Kṛṣṇa, Rādhārāṇī so enchanting,  
Jagannātha, Gaura-Nitāi, and Sītā-Rāma are calling  
Everyone to serve Them, all our energy devoting.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your holy dhāmas are one and all inviting  
To come and take shelter in association pleasing,

To purify our hearts by extra chanting and hearing,  
Then return to fight the battle, with firm resolve increasing.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your devotees are like miracles living,  
Empowered to spread Lord Caitanya’s saṅkīrtana chanting,  
In every town and village they’re ecstatically dancing,  
Pray let me serve their lotus feet, offenses avoiding,

Thus let me serve your lotus feet, I’m eternally begging.

Your servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet.

Force me, if it should come to it, to become wholly your instrument. For that has become my single goal. All other aims being put aside or gathered into this one, my life has become simple and organized economically toward a single point.

To me you are the most vivid exemplar of a successful devotee. Indeed, your achievement is breathtaking, unparalleled. (And your achievement has only begun to unfold.) You have revealed the secret of this success to all of us in plain and simple language: “The secret of success in spiritual life,” you wrote, “is to take the order of the spiritual master as one’s life and soul.” From that completed act of surrender to the spiritual master everything else exfoliates. You have revealed this priceless secret and demonstrated its truth by your actions, and I gratefully accept the gift of your revelation without reservation.

But I must confess that it is one thing to accept in principle, another in practice. A poet once wrote:

Between the desire and the act Falls the shadow.

The shadow of hesitation falls across my heart. I have no doubt about what I must do, yet still some obscure and irrational reluctance benights me. I cannot discern its origin. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, lead me out of this umbra of unwillingness.

Whenever I hesitate to execute your order, *force* me. Make me do it; give me no choice; remove all alternatives. If you request this, I’m sure Kṛṣṇa will oblige. If I protest and complain, please ignore it. Keep on forcing. For however painful it may be to my body or mind, it will be unending joy to the soul.

Lord Caitanya’s plan is unfolding inexorably, step by step, and you, His empowered agent, enlisted me as one of your instruments. That is all I want to be, and that is what I attain simply by following your order. When I willingly become part of the Lord’s plan, an instrument in His hands through you, a veil is lifted from my eyes, and I see the Lord move, I see Him infallibly accomplishing His ends through those who are both willing and unwilling agents. This vision is my life, and I want more than anything to be a willing and conscious part of this wonderful mission of Lord Caitanya.

I realize all too acutely that I am not qualified. Many great souls populate this movement, but I am not among them. I feel like a party-crasher. But somehow by your mercy I am here. And now that I’ve had a taste of real life, I fear leaving. Although I lack credentials, I hope if I can make myself useful, I’ll be allowed to stay.

I am watching with awe how the seeds you planted grow. I see the future because it is in a way already present: I see the materialistic civilization fall and authentic spiritual culture rise up. I read it in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* I see it happening. As I witness your plan proceed, see the unmanifest become manifest, I am aware more and more of your presence at work among us.

Do not let me become separate from this process, from the unfolding of your will. Therefore I am pleading with you: You brought me here; now please keep me here. When I hesitate to surrender to your order, *force* me. Begging this mercy from you, falling at your feet, I am

Your unworthy servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet, along with this insignificant offering as a feeble attempt at your glorification on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

1\. How was it that I …

… toiling under the insurmountable burden of the banyan tree of illusion, decomposing in an insignificant, far-flung planet called home, interned in a cage of bones produced of an infinite maze of unforgivable sins, welded by insatiable lust, fermented of an incredulous ego, whipped by merciless witches, poisoned by pride, blinded by avarice, tied to the vampire of my fate, ever came to hear your transcendental message, which severed the abysmal knots of misery, leading me to a new land …

… fresh new hope …!

… where, wandering the jeweled islands, born of Śrī Rādhā’s separation from Her dark-complexioned friend, Their love eternally engraved in the places of Lord Gaura’s transcendental pastimes, decorated by divine groves of incomparable beauty, among palaces of jewels situated on the banks of the celestial Ganges, where great festivals of Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* are always manifest, while great devotees, demigods, and sages are rapt in the pastimes of the Lord, do I constantly meditate on the lotus feet of the dearest of Lord Gaura Hari, who alone delivered me …

… how did this come to pass.…?

… only by the causeless mercy of the atomic rays reflected from the lotus feet of my eternal spiritual master, who entered the gates of Candradoya Mandira, saying, “Back home, back to Godhead,” relishing the “Vaikuṇṭha breezes” in his “place of worship,” Śrīla Prabhupāda.

2\. How was it …

… that in the abysmal mud of a social machine conceived in the evil minds of faithless demons, designed in the blood of innocent fools, built in speculative factories of greedy ministers, protected by lasers, computers, harlots, and drugs, operated in cement jungles of skyscrapers and sewers, fueled by the sweat of lusty *śūdras,* producing violence, anxiety, and fear in the guise of progress … a real society of pure Vaiṣṇavas, full of love and truth, blossoms like a golden lotus …

… whose corolla …

… resides in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, wherein the transcendental authority spreads from the will of Lord Gaurahari through the medium of His Divine Grace, represented through his governing commissioners, disciples, followers, to the thousands of petals that encompass the globe, residence of their own presiding Deities, manifest through Their Divine Grace, served reverentially by countless attendants under the guidance of Vaiṣṇava *paramparā,* where intersecting petals define the character of unity in diversity and the stem is fixed in the instructions of Śrīla Prabhupāda . . .

… he who is an ocean of mercy …

… well-wisher of all, founder of ISKCON, *varṇāśrama* visionary, greater-than-any-king Śrīla Prabhupāda … whose name—when we were on *parikrama* of Śrī Vṛndāvana and were asked by a bearded old Vaiṣṇava “Who is your spiritual master?” and we gave our proud answer—elicited the wide-eyed response “You are the most fortunate people in the world.”

3\. Typeset trash …

… newspaper propaganda, lying cheating in the guise of truth, weeklies, monthlies, full-color rags, proud banners of a more arrogant decadence, bulletins and billboards peddling sex, “education” branding malleable hearts with greed, radio and T.V. poisoning the ether, movies and video opiate of the mass, rock and punk the dalliance of Kali, the living gods doctors, scientists, technology’s finest, social vampires draining the blood of anemic humanity … all lies, all ignorance, all madness and illusion, no relief, no hope, none for the damned … only Śrīla Prabhupāda …

… who could glorify…

… the Supreme Lord in countless nectarean verses, saturated with the sweet-thick honey of his spontaneous love for the dear consort of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, concealed in Lord Gaurāṅga’s unfathomable will, hidden from the view of brother Gauḍīyas, transparent to the desire of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda’s empowered son, manifest to the world under the full moon of His desire, vanquishing the scorching sun of Western materialism, voidist dogma, universal impersonal-ism, through the wisdom of the *Gītā, siddhānta* of *Bhāgavatam, rasa* of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and other pure songs of devotion … while the world stagnates, glorifying monsters, turtles, muppets, and dogs …

… only Vyāsadeva’s representative …

… empowered pen of the *ācāryas,* servant of Śrī Rūpa, true son of his own *guru,* greatest author, poet, philosopher of this age, who toiled through all hardships, heart attacks, headaches, conspiracies, “walked through fire,” to whom Śrī Kṛṣṇa “comes daily and dictates every word … every word,” about whom Mann Gahr, a well-spoken old Bābājī, in a mountain *āśrama* at Varṣāṇā, asked us, “Do you have any of his books? I read them and give them to my friends. We take great guidance and faith. Do you have any?” my great fortune, only pride, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

4\. How is it that even now …

… I struggle with drowsiness, inattention, and inability while delighting in the waves of material facility, while anger and envy marshal Mongol hordes, countless *anarthas* haunting me at every turn, every moment, every place, day and night, a serpentine tongue, acrid and vile, licking stale news, offenses to Vaiṣṇavas, how I delight in such exquisite suicide, dressed in *dhotī,* decorated with *tilaka,* freshly shaven *sannyāsi-guru,* Kali’s *chelā,* seeking higher posts, acquiring more, tasting less, superficial success, spiritual failure …

… while he …

… the perfect example, pride of the *paramparā,* unfathomable intellect and unsurpassed preaching spirit, pragmatic “world” *ācārya,* unfailing inspiration of his followers, the witness within his pictures, continuing support of ISKCON, always eager to reciprocate with preachers, his word heartbeat of book distributors, pure, faultless, humble, renounced, ideal for all, inimitable Śrīla Prabhupāda …

… who said. …

… “Caitanya Mahaprabhu says, By My order you become a spiritual master.’ So one may be very illiterate, no education, no scholarship, may not be born in a *brāhmaṇa* family or may not be a *sannyāsī*—there are so many qualifications. But one may not have all these qualifications. He may be rascal number one. But still he can become spiritual master … How?. … if you follow, then you become *guru*. One may be rascal number one … simply follow … become *guru*,” and then left, leaving us to reconcile his instructions and example, both perfect, the hearer not.

5\. By his grace, eternal father …

… what is impossible for those who follow? … cannot the lost find shelter in the midst of obscurity, cannot the dumb recite Sanskrit eloquent poetry, are not the lame scaling the mountain of a once-impregnable hope, does social disharmony not find its long-lost chord, are obscure euphemisms not dispersed in the sun of Truth, impersonal dogma giving way to a cowherd’s wistful smile, monkeys and bears not becoming the new intelligentsia? what has not happened yet is yet to come, what appears impossible is his followers’ challenge …

… after the testimony of such chivalrous deeds …

… is it not right to hope … that despite innumerable sins, unfathomable obstacles, subtle and gross, the invaluable treasure of pure devotion, gift of him who is glorified herein, reflected in the hearts of great devotees, shining brighter by the fire of ordeal, fanned by the pangs of separation from him, who vowed “Chant sixteen rounds, follow the regulative principles, and you will go back home, back to Godhead, in this life,” stoked by the works of the *ācārya,* fueled by the dust of Vṛndāvana, forever looming like an inevitable reality, always distant, will by his grace, in some precious time to be worshiped by millions of offerings, inundate this fallen soul, miserable and insignificant, with the nectarean waves of spontaneous attraction to the lotus feet of Lord Gaura Hari? …

… is it not possible?

Your insignificant servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, who are the most intimate servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda!

In India you referred to us as your dancing white elephants. The Indians were always curious to see the dancing foreign *sādhus*. We were unqualified in so many ways to serve you, but because people would come to see us, they would also sit and listen to your preaching. Perhaps in this insignificant way we were useful.

On other occasions you called us *mūḍhas,* donkeys, because we were very often foolish in carrying out your instructions. Sometimes you talked about the swanlike men who know that the purpose of human life is God realization and the crowlike men who are only interested in material life.

You quoted the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which compares men in Kali-yuga to dogs, hogs, camels, and asses. Devoid of any *ātma-tattva-jñāna,* or spiritual knowledge, they blindly follow blind leaders, obstinately pursuing sense gratification unrestrictedly.

I learned from you how Lord Rāma engaged so many monkeys in conquering the great demon Rāvaṇa. He also engaged an insignificant squirrel, saying that the squirrel was serving to his capacity and was thus equal to Hanumānji.

I remember a story you once told me of a man in Calcutta who sold dead rats to the British government. It was a very difficult period, and to check the outbreak of disease in the city the government was offering a reward for exterminating rodents. You said that this man, because of his utilizing everything, eventually became very wealthy.

While thinking of you in separation on this most auspicious day of your ninety-fifth birth anniversary, I am trying to imagine how I can be of some service to you in your preaching. I am realizing more and more that my salvation is completely dependent on your unlimited mercy, and yet I fear that due to my negligence and offenses I may be left out of your plan.

In my anxiety I find a little hope thinking of the rats in Calcutta. Please kill the ratlike *anarthas* hiding in the recesses of my heart and use me in any insignificant way. In any insignificant way. Please don’t neglect me.

Your servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering to you from China at a time when America and her allies are hard at war with Iraq. I am traveling on a train from Guilin to Nanning, but as we pass through the mystical mountains of Guilin into the fertile fields of Guangxi, it is not the gunfire of the Gulf which deafens my ears. The silent cry of one billion conditioned souls is as sobering as any horror of war could ever be. One imagines how confident Kali must be, laughing disdainfully at our very small band of devotees here. But we are equally confident, engaged in our “mission impossible,” for we know that by our efforts we can earn your unlimited blessings, no matter the outcome.

I am reminded of how Śrī Nārada Muni traveled through the various tracts of land of Bhāratavarṣa. Referring to this in your Bhaktivedanta purports, you have written, “It is the duty of the mendicant (*parivrājakācārya*) to experience all varieties of God’s creations by traveling alone through the forest, hills, towns, villages, etc., to gain faith in God and strength of mind as well as to enlighten the inhabitants with the message of God. A *sannyāsī* is duty-bound to take all these risks without fear.” You go on to say that Lord Caitanya is the most typical *sannyāsī* of the present age, but I instantly think of Your Divine Grace as no less typical, for you too have traveled to even more dangerous jungles to deliver mankind from their animalistic existence.

Though you never entered the mainland of China, you stopped twice at its gateway, Hong Kong. By doing so, you showed your concern for the more than one billion members of the Khasa race. It was always your desire that they become Kṛṣṇa conscious, as you wrote to me in 1969: “So if we can overflood Japan with the *saṅkīrtana* movement, it will be a great, great achievement in the Eastern countries, and if we move a little further more into China and Russia, then we shall build up a strong belt all around the world of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.”

The only hope for persons fallen so far from the Vedic culture is to receive the mercy of the Lord’s pure devotee. Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī states, “Kirāta, Hūṇa, Āndhra, Pulinda, Pulkaśa, Ābhira, Śumbha, Yavana, members of the Khasa races, and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.” Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī, perfect knower of the past, present, and future, must surely have spoken these words with Your Divine Grace in mind. Otherwise, how could persons such as those of the Khasa race ever be delivered? Therefore they desperately require Your Divine Grace.

You asked that we profusely distribute your books to create the field for preaching Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message to them. Unfortunately, their government seems to have quickly realized the threat your books pose and has embarked on a policy of confiscating all literatures that propagate “superstition,” their code word for religion. With a morbid enthusiasm they have seized and burned our *Bhagavad-gītās* to “protect the population from becoming contaminated by foreign ideas.”

We remain undaunted, however, for in the midst of Kali-yuga’s increasingly dismal atmosphere, a ray of hope illuminates our path. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* states, “Simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom.” You have always encouraged *harināma saṅkīrtana,* especially for those unable to read (either due to disinclination or, as in this case, due to repression). Following your order, we have urged them to chant Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, only to find that in the Chinese language the sound *ri* does not exist. You can imagine our dilemma: each of the *mahā-mantra’s* sixteen names requires this letter be sounded, yet there is no equivalent Chinese character to express it. The closest approximation to the *mahā-mantra* would translate as: Hale Kesina, Hale Kesina, Kesina Kesina, Hale Hale, Hale Lama, Hale Lama, Lama Lama, Hale Hale. We actually met persons faithfully chanting sixteen rounds of this *nāmābhāsa* hybrid. When we saw that their *anarthas* persisted, we concluded that accurately reproducing the holy names was more important and decided in favor of the Romanized version whenever the *mahā-mantra* appears in print.

*Prasādam,* you have stated, is our secret weapon in the battle with Kali’s forces. In a society where rice and vegetables are the staples of its diet, we had every right to be hopeful. But as we soon discovered, such tame fare holds little allurement compared to the more lively entities that walk, crawl, squirm, swim, or fly. When recently a Peking hot-bun shop was exposed for stuffing their pastries with the flesh of human corpses, only the families of the deceased—not the restaurant-goers—expressed outrage! One would not imagine such tongues would find any taste strange. Yet when it comes to Vedic spicing they are surprisingly hesitant, preferring their own familiar tastes. So we have had to adjust to eating “Chinese” (*prasādam,* of course). It is an acquired taste one slowly develops—but then, there is no question of rushing when one uses chopsticks.

Though there have been numerous setbacks, and though difficulties remain, every small triumph provides us sufficient strength to continue. When we see a devotee like Nāmācārya dāsa chant eighty rounds a day (and on Ekādaśīs one hundred and twenty), we know that all our efforts are worthwhile and that seeds of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are firmly planted. But victory is yet far away. Though you have trained us well and armed us with the transcendental weapons of your books, Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, and His *prasādam,* it seems that in the land of the Khasas, something more is required. On this battlefront such conventional spiritual weapons are simply not enough.

In the war with Iraq, the Allies have their “smart weapons.” Though launched from very great distances, their computerized memories guide them unfailingly over hilltops, down valleys, even around skyscrapers. If Kali is to be defeated in Khasadeśa we need an equally “smart” spiritual weapon. The one billion plus spirit souls awaiting liberation are being held captive, guarded carefully in prisons walled by ancient traditions and encircled by the barbed wire of modern propaganda. If these lines of defenses can be penetrated, victory shall be ours.

Your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that special weapon we desire. If you agree to trust us, then we shall aim and release your mercy, and it will unfailingly navigate on its own through all the labyrinthian conditions until it reaches the most secret of all hidden places where those souls lie captive. And the Khasa race shall never again be the same.

Your ever-faithful soldier in the field,  
Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vāsudeva dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

lease accept my *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet again and again.

Lately, devotional service appears to me to be getting more and more difficult. But I know this is a test. As you write in a *Bhagavad-gītā* purport, “One must be able to pass the test of the spiritual master, and when he sees the genuine desire of the disciple, he automatically blesses the disciple with genuine spiritual understanding” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34)

Sometimes I wonder how you preached all over the world at the age of seventy. My age is sixty-five, and I find I am not able to do much. I am running a restaurant, as you wanted. It’s very successful, just as you said: “*Prasādam* distribution will make Fiji successful.”

Somehow, I established this restaurant exactly according to the instructions you once gave to a devotee. The name of the restaurant is “Hare Krishna Restaurant,” as you wanted. We serve *prasādam* in *thālīs,* as well as giving the guests a selection of individual items. We also play light devotional music, as you wanted. Our restaurants maintain a very high Vaiṣṇava standard of cleanliness. If one follows your instructions, he will certainly be successful.

I still regret not having met you in your last days while your physical health was failing. You had requested all GBC’s and other devotees to come and meet you in Vṛndāvana. At that time the Kṛṣṇa-Kāliya Temple here had just opened. Being new and less mature in understanding you, our spiritual master, I could not make the correct decision—whether to go and visit you or see to the running of the new temple. I chose the latter, and thus I did not see you in your last days. Sometimes I become very angry with myself. I, along with my elder brother Bhuvana-mohana Prabhu and my wife Bhāgavatī, could have easily visited you at that time. Kṛṣṇa had given me all the facilities, yet I did not understand your urgent request for meeting all the devotees. There is one consolation: that in my regret, I do remember you in separation. But nevertheless, I feel upset with myself.

Despite the fact that many difficulties may come, I still feel very, very fortunate to have met you and taken part in devotional service under the shelter of your lotus feet. Now your granddisciples, who are increasing in number, are assisting me in your service.

Once, before departing from the Nandi airport in Fiji, you told me that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread when local devotees take charge of distributing books and preaching. By your mercy, book distribution and preaching work are going on well. There is good cooperation among the devotees.

I am sincerely trying to establish a temple in Suva, the capital city, where most of Fiji’s population is concentrated. Before I become physically weaker, I pray for your mercy so that I may be able to offer this new temple at your lotus feet. Always remembering you, our dear spiritual master, especially on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day of 1991, I remain

Your servant,  
Vāsudeva dāsa

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Prostrated at your lotus feet, paying my humble obeisances, I have come to beg for your mercy and request that you accept this attempt to chant your glories. It is an offering from one of your innumerable fortunate children.

Oh, yes, I am most fortunate. By your mercy. It is said by Śrī Uddhava in *Śrīmad-bhāgavatam* (3.2.23),

*aho bakī yaṁ stana-kāla-kūṭaṁ*  
*jighāṁsayāpāyayad apy asādhvī*  
*lebhe gatiṁ dhātry-ucitāṁ tato ’nyaṁ*  
*kaṁ vā dayāluṁ śaraṇam vrajema*

**“Alas, how shall I take shelter of one more merciful than Him who granted the position of mother to a she-demon \[Pūtanā\] although she was unfaithful and she prepared deadly poison to be sucked from her breast?”**

In your purport we find our good luck intertwined with your indescribable mercy: “Here is an example of the extreme mercy of the Lord, even to His enemy. It is said that a noble man accepts the good qualities of a person of doubtful character, just as one accepts nectar from a stock of poison.”

Certainly, you are that noble man, and our character is most doubtful.

“The Lord accepts the least qualification of the living entity and awards him the highest reward. That is the standard of His character.”

Our great fortune has been that that is also the standard of *your* character. Quoting Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, you have described Lord Caitanya in the same way. He has given the highest thing—*kṛṣṇa-prema*—to the lowest, not asking for anything in exchange. Therefore he is Kṛṣṇa’s most munificent incarnation. But what are we to say of yourself? This magnanimity of Kṛṣṇa’s that descended in the form of Lord Caitanya has descended for a second time in Kali-yuga in the form of Your Divine Grace, His most intimate associate. Uddhava makes this very clear to us in his next statement:

*manye ’surān bhāgavatāṁs tryadhīśe*  
*saṁrambha-mārgābhiniviṣṭa-cittān*  
*ye saṁyuge ‘cakṣata tārkṣya-putram*  
*aṁse sunābhāyudham āpatantam*

**“I consider the demons, who are inimical toward the Lord, to be more than the devotees, because while fighting with the Lord, absorbed in thoughts of enmity, they are able to see the Lord carried on the shoulder of Garuḍa, the son of Tārkṣya \[Kaśyapa\], and carrying the wheel in His hand.”**

But without your words we would have not understood it: “This salvation of the demons is not due to their being devotees of the Lord; it is because of the Lord’s causeless mercy. Anyone who is slightly in touch with the Lord, somehow or other, is greatly benefited, even to the point of salvation, due to the excellence of the Lord. He is so kind that He awards salvation even to His enemies because they come in contact with Him and are indirectly absorbed in Him by their inimical thoughts.”

Sometimes we may think so many things as to how and why we have come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In my case, the actual reason is clear from the above purport. There is nothing good in me except for your blessings. How can I take shelter of one more merciful than you?

Oh, yes, I am most fortunate. By your mercy. It was told by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to Rūpa Gosvāmī (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 19.151):

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

Please keep me always in the shade of your lotus feet, protected from my greatest enemy—myself. May I always remain there as one of your innumerable and fortunate servants.

Eternally grateful and happy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I go on chanting your glories, which have made me fortunate.

Your lowly servant,  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Ānanda Svarūpa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glory to your lotus feet, which are the only sure means to cross over the vast ocean of material suffering.

Vyāsa-pūjā day is an important opportunity for formal exchange with you. The entire year I may evade contact, but today there is no escape. Naturally, I wish the exchange to be upbeat, positive; report progress and optimism. At the same time I cannot but be honest. To be otherwise, I feel, is bordering on the suicidal. It is thus futile to take shelter of euphemism and pretension, because, frankly, you know exactly what’s going on better than anyone.

The body of the Society is unwell. The ills many, the symptoms complex, diagnosis difficult, and proper prognosis evades even the most experienced devotees. That apart, there’s deep reluctance from some to take any medicine, while others lunatically insist there’s no problem.

How I wish things were different! How I long for the Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere you so wonderfully created by your mere presence on this fortunate planet.

Transcendental authority (with the notable exception of Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī) uniformly decries the material universe as the abode of abject misery. But, to me, when you were here, *my* entire universe was full of nectar: *viśvaṁ-pūrṇa-sukhāyate.*

I, and legion others, disfranchised, marginalized members of your movement, look skeptically at the present-day reality—spiced by historical repetition—as it unfolds before us. How I wish things were different. How I wish someone had the power to do something constructive. Perhaps it’s all part of the plan to put you in the center factually rather than rhetorically. Perhaps it’s to teach us that you and only you are the *ācārya* and that you and only you can guide us back home. Since I sincerely feel *that* to be the case, I come to you today with a prayer, and since you are that transcendental personality who can deliver beyond anyone’s capacity to ask, I submit the following wish list, implicit in which is my unbounded faith in your compassion and expertise.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how I wish:

• There were no more personality cults. In ISKCON let there be one personality: Śrīla Prabhupāda. One Vyāsa-pūjā book: Śrīla Prabhupāda’s. One Vyāsa-pūjā festival: Śrīla Prabhupāda’s.  
• There was a system through which we could help each other in the process of purification, *anartha-nivṛtti,* instead of empty offerings of reciprocal obeisances overlaid with diplomatic etiquette without something constructive being done about the attachment to a process of purification which has become hackneyed and stereotyped and that obviously doesn’t deliver.  
• The vision to *really* see each other as spirit souls separate from whatever false ego or *māyā* may be covering us, and thus not be subject to condemnation or rejection for either, but rather to find positive encouragement to deal with such basic problems.  
• Freedom from fanaticism, from rigid sectarian views which have really nothing to do with the teaching of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.  
• For an end to our artificial isolation from the society in which we live, through encouraging each other to develop our talents and the understanding that everything is connected with Kṛṣṇa and thus everything can be used in His service on the principle of *yukta-vairāgya.*  
• To renounce once and for all the demoniac practice of squeezing the devotees for everything they’re worth in the name of devotional service, when the real reason has been shown time and again to be to fulfill short-term economic goals at best or, at worst, to feed someone’s false conception of greatness and/or purity.  
• Finally have everyone in ISKCON understand that the reason people join our movement is to become spiritually transformed, to reestablish their eternal relationship with God, and that the price asked for this transformation is high (total surrender of energy, time, opinions, other interests, and so on), and that with proper training, counseling, guidance, and love these goals are certainly possible, but that we, as a movement, are not delivering on our promise while expertly cashing in on the price for the course.  
• That ISKCON ceases to be a cult but transforms itself into an open, spiritual institution wherein people come first and attention is given to the development of real, honest, and loving relationships free from fear or guilt. And please, please, no elitist clubs.  
• That women be encouraged to take part in decision-making, give classes, and generally be awarded credit for being spirit souls too and for doing at least half the work.  
• That we collectively move away from the *kaniṣṭha* mentality that Kṛṣṇa is only in ISKCON and that everyone and everything else is useless and demoniac. To face that, even if Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy is being channeled through us, that’s only all the more reason to become humble and compassionate, not proud and condemning. Please, Prabhupāda, help us get off the neophyte platform.  
• That our leaders accept meaningful responsibility for the ills of the Society and for the vote of no confidence by an overwhelming number of Godbrothers delivered with their lotus feet (as they walked out).  
• That the *gurus* and *sannyāsīs* do the job of *guru* by enlivening their students in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, making certain of their spiritual progress by systematic training, education, and counseling as an ongoing part of their charges’ daily life.  
• That I develop a modicum of humility and patience.

Ānanda Svarūpa Swami

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Reading your matchless books, I discover your treasures, have a glimpse of your greatness, and realize my littleness.

Thanks again and again for the privilege of serving you, and please forgive my innumerable offenses.

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says in his *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that those who strictly obey the orders of the spiritual master are useful for executing the will of the Supreme, whereas those who do not follow them are useless:

*ācāryera mata yei, sei mata sāra*  
*tānra ājñā laṅghi’ cale, sei ta’ asāra*

**“The order of the spiritual master is the active principle in spiritual life. Anyone who disobeys the order of the spiritual master immediately becomes useless” (*****Ādi-līlā*** **12.10).**

The spiritual master is like a father, without whose care the child cannot develop in a proper way. Without the competent guidance of the spiritual master, it is not possible for anyone to progress spiritually to reach the transcendental level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The perplexities of material life keep the living entity confused and misguided within his own intellectual limits. The delimitations given by the false ego diminish the already reduced space of his mind. Anyone desiring to get rid of the conditioning effects of the illusory material energy needs the efficient and unquestionable help of the spiritual master.

The process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is real and sublime, producing amazing and bona fide results.

The *sublime* is the extraordinary reciprocation that the devotee experiences in the personal form of the Absolute Truth, whom he serves—that is to say, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This reciprocation is manifested in many and factual revelations within his heart.

The *real* is found in the very possibility of maintaining a direct and personal relationship with that Truth, his worshipable Deity, whereas other people are wasting their lives searching after it with preconceptions and abstract ideas in their heads.

Knowledge is like light that destroys the darkness of ignorance, and the spiritual master is holding the torch emanating such light. At the time of initiation the bona fide representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva—the spiritual master—transmits that knowledge to the heart of the disciple, together with something even more valuable—the devotional feeling. This enables the disciple to *taste* the highest taste.

This ability to taste is an understanding beyond his own intelligence.

Transcending matter, understanding Kṛṣṇa, rendering service unto Him, and absorbing oneself in His wonderful pastimes are the highest goals for any human being interested in perfection, and they can be easily obtained by those who have full faith in the spiritual master. The order of the spiritual master empowers the disciple to carry on his devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, to a limit beyond his own conscious capacity.

Persons engaged in devotional service achieve the supreme abode of Kṛṣṇa, where there is no disease, old age, or death.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura advises, “My friend, you are being washed away in material nature’s waves of time. Please try to understand that you are the eternal servant of the Lord. Then everything will stop, and you will be eternally happy.”

The spiritual strength coming from the degree of purity of the devotee is easily achieved by faithfully following the order of the spiritual master, which is the purifying factor par excellence, because by his grace we can put our senses in contact with Kṛṣṇa, the supreme purifier, through devotional service.

By following the order of the spiritual master with determination, the disciple not only saves his life but also becomes an instrument for Kṛṣṇa’s mercy to save others.

The relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple is based in service and trust. Faith in the spiritual master is all-important. With it we will be able to overcome the times of trial and to understand the scriptures better.

If the disciple commits the blunder of not giving importance to the instructions of the spiritual master, naturally he will become vulnerable to the influence of the illusory material energy.

To spiritually survive and maintain our original Kṛṣṇa conscious state within the material world, we have to be isolated, beyond the touch by matter. But since we are living within a material body, we are unavoidably infested with many false identifications. Therefore we urgently need a remedy to protect ourselves from falling victim to the disease of materialism.

Only the spiritual master can guarantee to cure this disease, if we take his instructions as our life and soul.

A person in illusion is usually proud and therefore cannot properly use his intelligence. But if he is submissive enough to let himself be guided by the spiritual master, he will be able to act in a perfect way.

Anyone who reads Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books is situated in transcendence, overcomes his doubts, and gets free from fear. Fear is due to separation from Kṛṣṇa.

The pure devotee is free from duplicity. The pure devotee, or *uttama-adhikārī,* is the most qualified to act as spiritual master, and Śrīla Prabhupāda is the best example in this regard.

One may follow the four principles, chant sixteen rounds daily, and be a responsible devotee, but it will be very difficult to be free from duplicity. He who is free from duplicity will understand Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The spiritual master is like Kṛṣṇa Himself, but because of his purity and genuine humility he sees everyone as a more advanced devotee than himself.

Without any poetical show, let us render a permanent homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda by simply following his instructions and explaining to others everything we have learned from him. Let this be our renewed and increasing mood of worship, or eternal Vyāsa-pūjā, to him who has so generously saved us from material bondage and given us the opportunity to go back to Kṛṣṇa forever.

Personally, I am eternally grateful for such undeserved mercy, and thus I offer my respectful obeisances millions of times to the lotus feet of my real father, savior, and master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, who I fully trust to be able to cure my otherwise incurable, useless condition.

Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti-mārga Swami

**The Vyāsa-pūjā**

The Vyāsa-pūjā,  
Most blessed event,  
Casts on all  
That radiant moment

When zealous admirers  
Converge at his base  
And outpour their feelings  
Of shame and disgrace.

An outlet for truth,  
A time of reflection,  
With rewarding returns  
Of fatherly affection.

His Divine Grace  
Eased on his chair  
Displays his peace,  
Gentleness, and care.

Our guru and savior  
Makes patience his theme,  
While we squander our time  
In materialistic scheme.

His Divine Grace  
Illumes through his lessons,  
As a lantern at night  
Emits its essence.

The envoy of Vyāsa,  
The agent of God,  
The center of our pūjā,  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti-rāghava Swami

**The Most Blessed Event “Surrendering at Your Lotus Feet”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance in this troubled world of Kali, please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who forever continues to shower inconceivable mercy upon all those who take full shelter and solace in your divine instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am here in Patna on a short visit. I have written letters to various Godbrothers of mine regarding preaching matters. Writing to my Godbrothers I find quite easy, but writing to you is not at all an easy or spontaneous affair. That is my great misfortune. I have not learned to properly surrender, and therefore I cannot so easily and spontaneously approach and glorify you.

Please know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I am feeling immense pleasure in reading your nectarean instructions as given in all the wonderful letters you sent to your inquisitive and surrendered disciples. Alas! I was so dull and ignorant that I did not take the opportunity to write to you even once. The thought of writing to you never even entered my crippled brain. Yet today, after seventeen years in the movement, I find that many of these instructions you gave to so many disciples are also meant for me, and I thank you for the time and effort you gave to replying so meticulously to all these letters.

In the past few years I do not feel I have done much for you. I would like to contribute to your ISKCON Society, and I pray that you kindly bestow your mercy on this fallen conditioned soul to help render some little service to you. You are wonderful, Śrīla Prabhupāda, simply wonderful, because even after having physically left us fourteen years ago, by your inconceivable and transcendental potency you continue to attract many sincere and pure souls to your movement. I have personally seen and experienced this causeless mercy of yours, and I frankly do not know how some of this mercy is also coming my way.

Now your one-hundredth anniversary is fast approaching. I, along with many of my Godbrothers, would like to simply see you glorified like anything, all over the world. Let everyone know how you single-handedly brought the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to every major city of the world, let everyone appreciate how you so methodically and intelligently structured this Society of ISKCON to accommodate all the residents of the world. Let everyone witness the exquisite beauty of the Deities in your temples. Let everyone come and take part in the purifying process of honoring Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. Let all become enthralled by the captivating sunshinelike purports given in all the books you wrote. Let the misguided and illusioned conditioned souls of the world catch hold of your divine lotus feet and thus become immersed in the ocean of ever-increasing transcendental bliss by understanding and realizing their eternal constitutional position as servants of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the ALL-ATTRACTIVE and SUPREME Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although I have no qualification, I do have a desire to assist you, to be with you. Please somehow continue to shower your merciful glance upon me. You have many, many wonderful, childlike disciples. Please help me to become one of them, fully surrendered at your divine lotus feet.

Your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti-rāghava Swami

## Bhaktivaibhāva Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

**“I offer my respectful and humble obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful and humble obeisances are unto you, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, servant of Sarsavatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you write in one of your purports, “One who has faith in his spiritual master actually receives transcendental instruction, and as his attachment for material life slackens, he is able to advance on the spiritual path.”

The process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so wonderful because it does not depend on any material qualification. Many times you referred to your own example of personally having no qualifications. These are not only words of your incomparable humbleness, but a deep, meaningful instruction to your followers and disciples—a statement teaching us the process of *guru-paramparā.* A sincere disciple does not have to invent his own mission; he simply has to develop faith in the words of his spiritual master. In March 1975 you explained during the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, referring to the story of the illiterate *brāhmaṇa* from South India: “I am simply turning the pages. My Guru Mahā-rāja ordered me, so I have to read.” This is called *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā.* This is called firm faith in *guru*. Because he had firm faith in *guru,* Caitanya Mahāprabhu came to see him. Just see how this line is important. *Ār* means anything more you don’t try to understand—whatever your *guru* says, try to take it. Next line, *śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei sei uttama-gati.* Don’t try to make very good advancement by manufacturing your own way. *Vede gāya …* The *Vedas* say, *yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau: ‘*To understand Vedic literature, one must have firm faith in Kṛṣṇa and *guru*.’ Not that ‘My *guru* is not so learned; let me capture Kṛṣṇa directly.’ This is useless.”

Understanding Kṛṣṇa through the transcendental medium of the spiritual master is the perfection of the sincere disciple. He does not want to understand Kṛṣṇa in a different way. He is only accepting that Kṛṣṇa which is being delivered to him by his spiritual master. As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays, “Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. I am simply running behind you, shouting, ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!’ “

You further write in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* “In Vaiṣṇava philosophy there are three ways for perfection—namely, *sādhana-siddhi,* perfection attained by executing devotional service according to the rules and regulations; *nitya-siddhi,* eternal perfection attained by never forgetting Kṛṣṇa at any time; and *kṛpā-siddhi,* perfection attained by the mercy of the spiritual master or a Vaiṣṇava. Kavirāja Gosvāmī here stresses *kṛpā-siddhi,* perfection by the mercy of superior authorities. This mercy does not depend on the qualifications of a devotee.”

Endowed with firm faith in the perfection of his spiritual master’s teachings, the sincere disciple tries earnestly to render service, and that service attitude invokes the mercy of his spiritual master. Again, that service might be any menial service—it does not depend on any material qualification. Such service can be rendered with any small capacity one might possess. Receiving the mercy of the spiritual master, such a sincere disciple develops strong affection for the association of other devotees. Śrīla Bhakti-siddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura explains in his *Anubhāṣya:* “A person who has attracted the attention of the spiritual master by his sincere service likes to dance and chant with similarly developed Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees. The spiritual master authorizes such a devotee to deliver fallen souls in all parts of the world. Those who are not advanced prefer to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* in a solitary place.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, following in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas,* you designed your ISKCON movement in such a wonderful way that there is always ample facility for dancing, chanting, and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the association of devotees. The cultivation of our association with the devotees might be the biggest challenge in our Kṛṣṇa conscious career, because it is like a mirror for our actual advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How to be compassionate to and tolerant with those who are less advanced, how to make friendship with and take great inspiration from those who are on an equal level, and how to render service to and assist more advanced devotees—this is the proper way to associate with devotees. In the post graduate study, *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* we find unlimited examples of dealings between Vaiṣṇavas. You continue to write in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* “Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, in his thesis *Bhakti-sandarbha* (202), has stated that uncontaminated devotional service is the objective of pure Vaiṣṇavas and that one has to execute such service in the association of other devotees. By associating with devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa, one develops a sense of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and thus becomes inclined toward the loving service of the Lord. This is the process of approaching the Supreme Lord by gradual appreciation in devotional service. If one desires unalloyed devotional service, one must associate with devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for by such association only can a conditioned soul achieve a taste for transcendental love and thus revive his eternal relation with Godhead in a specific manifestation and in terms of the specific transcendental mellow (*rasa*) that one has eternally inherent in him.”

Gradually, by your causeless mercy, I am learning how to appreciate every single devotee in your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and I am praying for your blessings to gain entrance into the innermost circle of their association. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī writes that there are innumerable devotees of the Lord, and that he offers his respectful obeisances thousands of times unto their lotus feet. Through the intimate association of the devotees one is constantly being reminded of the unique position of your movement. Because you followed strictly in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas* and because you had full faith in the instructions of your Guru Mahārāja, your ISKCON movement is being placed in the highest position of *vaiṣṇava-saṅga,* provided its members adhere strictly to the principles you have laid down so carefully. In comparison with other exalted Vaiṣṇavas, most of your ISKCON devotees might come up short in every respect, but as long as they cling to your *saṅkīrtana* movement, they are even exalted among exalted Vaiṣṇavas. Many times you demonstrated this secret truth in defending any criticism against your disciples. Your Divine Grace’s movement will always remain unique and outstanding, as long as we follow your instructions. Here I would like to quote another passage from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* “It is very difficult to understand the secret of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but one who advances by the instruction of the previous *ācāryas* and follows in the footsteps of his predecessors in the line of disciplic succession will have success. Others will not.”

This secret is an open secret, but it remains a mystery for those who are not following cent percent in your footsteps by sincerely trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every nook and cranny of this planet. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you remind your sincere disciples and followers in describing the significance of Vyāsa-pūjā: “One cannot become a *guru* if he does not know what the Personality of Godhead Kṛṣṇa or His incarnation wants. The mission of the *guru* is the mission of the Supreme Personality of Godhead: to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.24.48).

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving to us everything that could possibly be given, and let me take the opportunity on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā-tithi to worship you as my beloved spiritual master, the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, the dearmost disciple of your spiritual master, and the dearmost servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhaktivaibhāva Swami

## Bhaktivikāśa Swami

**Śrī Guru Caraṇe Rati**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are our worshipable deity (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.2.37, 11.3.22). ISKCON is your body, BBT is your heart, and *lakṣmī* collections are your blood. Only you cared so much for Kṛṣṇa that you “kicked on his face with boot” His imitators and detractors! … Thus not denying the dust of your triumphant lotus feet even to the demons.

*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama gati*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

The topmost achievement in spiritual life is devotion to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. By his mercy all spiritual aspirations are fulfilled.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of Lord Caitanya’s causeless mercy. Lord Caitanya wanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness spread all over the world, and you did it.

I was one of many who you picked up. I was suffering. Not a little, but a lot. Not briefly, but eternally. Being a demon, envious and mischievous, in the “lowest of men” class, I was perpetually being cast into the ocean of material existence, in various demoniac species of life. Sin and selfishness were all I knew. There was no possibility of my attaining Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Who was Kṛṣṇa? I had no knowledge of or inclination for Him. How was my wretched existence redeemed, being washed to the shore of your lotus feet? The only answer is: “causeless mercy.”

You saved me. You saved all of us. It was you, no one else. You are our only hope—hope for the hopeless.

I was a street dog; you, a Vaiṣṇava aristocrat. What did you have to do with me? But you picked me up. Indeed, you did not hesitate to pick me up. Being still filled with an astonishing repertoire of ghastly material desires, I am still so far from Kṛṣṇa. I know that my lack of advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness cannot be very pleasing to you. But still I am not despondent, because I have got you. Now I am a dog with a master (*sanātha-jīvitam*). A dog is kickable, but somehow you haven’t kicked me away yet. So I shamelessly beg you now to give me the mercy of the severe kicks of rectification, which I so badly need.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, unless you kick me into shape, how am I ever going to control my mind? Although I am a nonsense and continue to be, still whatever I am, I have surrendered to you. Now please kindly do whatever is necessary to make me Kṛṣṇa conscious. My own struggling attempts are all useless.

I don’t have much scriptural or any other knowledge, I’m not austere, I’m always losing in the struggle to control my mind, I’m insensitive to others; like this, I have so many disqualifications. Hopeless case. But (in one sense) I don’t even care too much because I’m relying on your causeless mercy. I know that you are very merciful and that if I can just stick to the principle of trying to please you, then I won’t need to do anything else; my whole life will be perfect. *Tomāra karuṇā sāra:* your mercy is all that I am made of. I know that if I just stick to your lotus feet you will gradually set all my wrongs right, by your causeless mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your causeless mercy is manifested as service to you. There is no possibility for my advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness outside the scope of your service. Attachment to your lotus feet means service to your lotus feet. I pray to Kṛṣṇa to bless me with such attachment. Service to your lotus feet is the greatest opportunity, the greatest challenge, the source of all bliss, and the life of all your disciples. Without it, we would certainly drop back very, very deeply into material existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always stressed service. Your eternal occupation is to expand the service of the Lord by training disciples in a service attitude (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Adi* 1.44 purport). Scholars translate the word *bhakti* as “devotion,” but you translated and lived it as “devotional service.” What made you unique among your Godbrothers was your attitude of service to your spiritual master. You only wanted to serve. You didn’t want to waste even a moment. You didn’t aspire for material comforts, facility, name, position, glory, or anything else. When they came as a by-product of service, you matter-of-factly accepted them, without giving them much importance. What was important to you was service to your spiritual master.

You once explained how you had wondered how it was that you, among all your Godbrothers, had become successful. Several of your Godbrothers were deeply learned scholars, pleasing orators, and renowned *sannyāsīs*. Unlike you, many had extensive personal association with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. How, then, among all of them had you alone been successful? “When I analyze,” you explained, “I see that of all my Godbrothers only I had full faith in the order of our spiritual master.” You quoted: “*Guru-mukha-padma-vākya cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā: ‘*My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from the lotus mouth of my spiritual master.’ “ Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being a real *ācārya,* not just teaching by your words but by your very life.

Let us have faith in your words, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And let us serve you single-mindedly. This is our *paramparā*. We need not waste time debating what is or is not *paramparā;* we have only to hear from and serve the *paramparā*. If we can collapse at the end of every day exhausted from a full day’s service, only to get energy to serve you again—then that is causeless mercy. May our days and our lives be like that. Life without service to your lotus feet is not life at all; it is living death. If we can’t serve you, then what is the use of sustaining our lives?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us *guru-bhakti*. Not a ritual, not something that had to be done just because the *śāstras* say so, not something to be legislated or speculated on, but alive, powerful. You were attached to your *guru,* you showered down mercy on us, and we actually loved you.

*Love* is a very inadequate term. That very simple, natural, and deep relationship of the soul, a spontaneous but continuous outflowing from the heart that continues to increase even in the physical absence of the object of love (in this case, yourself)—such love, though long forgotten, buried deep in our hearts, is the very essence of our existence; such love, which even in the first stage of development easily defeats the petty so-called love of this material world, even though unimaginably wonderful in the beginning, continues increasing even more unimaginably up to the point of actual love, love of Kṛṣṇa—and even then goes on increasing; such love you ignited in our hearts, then kindled with your words, activities, gestures, encouragement, and chastisement, until our hearts blazed with the desire to serve you and your beloved Kṛṣṇa.

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement spread all over the world because of your love. Your love was for *guru* and Kṛṣṇa; you desired to satisfy them in all respects. And we only desired to serve you and your Kṛṣṇa. You sacrificed everything to please your *guru,* and you instilled the same spirit in us; otherwise, how could the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement spread as it did? It was materially impossible.

But you did not stick to the material. You were from the world of the unlimited. You did not think that service to *guru* meant giving a few lectures, initiating some widows, and indulging in theoretical conversations about advanced levels of devotion. As you wrote, “A great heritage brings responsibility in the matter of proper discharge of duties” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.14, purport). Being the disciple of none other than Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, the incomparable, transcendentally mighty, sunlike effulgent *ācārya,* you always felt that you had to do the unlimited, and Kṛṣṇa responded unlimitedly. You mercifully gave unlimited souls a glimpse of the unlimited. Despite being on the platform of empowered unlimitedness, you always considered yourself a humble servant of your spiritual master and felt keenly your bond of relationship with him. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being a real *ācārya,* not just teaching by words but by your very life. You are “the *ācārya* in the true sense of the term, who is authorized to deliver Kṛṣṇa” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta Adi* 1.47, purport). You are undoubtedly such a self-realized spiritual master.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have written in your purports to *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is:* “Satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34, purport), and, “One should accept the instruction of the bona fide spiritual master as one’s mission in life” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41, purport). When you first read these instructions in the Sanskrit *Gītā* commentary of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, they entered deep into your heart and shaped your whole life’s mission. You trained your disciples like that also, so that when I was mercifully allowed to join this movement, the devotees kindly instructed me: “In everything you do, always think how to please Śrīla Prabhupāda.” The whole movement was powering ahead on this simple but infallible principle.

Despite being inducted into and nurtured in such Prabhupāda consciousness, in course of time I became deluded by imaginary notions of being spiritually advanced. I gradually deteriorated into subtly looking for ways to try to enjoy Kṛṣṇa consciousness in self-centered ways. By your mercy, however, you did not reject me, and the self-introspection which followed my inevitable dissatisfaction led me once again to realize my dependence on the dust of your lotus feet.

So this is my conviction: that the only way to understand the Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is to follow you completely, without any deviation. I don’t have to read a million books, grow a beard, stay up all night or artificially try to become a *gopī*. I just have to follow your simple and straightforward instructions. That’s all. It’s really as easy as that. Others may say or think or do what they like (and even say, think, or do so many things in your name), but for me at least, as far as this tiny one soul is concerned, my path is very clear: follow your instructions (without introducing half-chicken logic), read your books with full faith that this is one hundred percent truth (*sarvam etad ṛtaṁ manye yan māṁ vadasi*), and pray to you continuously for guidance to follow you correctly. Step by step, I entrust my life to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m such a long way from Kṛṣṇa, but I know that if you are pleased with me, then, as and when you wish, you will give me Kṛṣṇa. By your causeless mercy you picked us up from *māyā,* by your causeless mercy you keep us going, and by your causeless mercy you will one day introduce us to Kṛṣṇa, face to face. *Kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite paro, tomāra śakati āche:* “Kṛṣṇa is yours, you have the power to give us Kṛṣṇa.”

As you write (in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.1.10): “By the blessings of a Vaiṣṇava, everything is possible.” This statement gives me hope. Indeed, I have based my whole life on it.

You also write (in your “Prayer to the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa”):

*tomāra se śakti pele guru-sevāya bastu mile*  
*jīvana sārthaka jadi hoy*  
*sei se sevā pāile tāhale sukhī hale*  
*tara saṅga bhāgyate miloy*

**“If You bestow Your power, by serving the spiritual master one attains the Absolute Truth—one’s life becomes successful. If that service is obtained, then one becomes happy and gets Your association due to good fortune.”**

Thus, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa one gets *guru,* and by the mercy of *guru* one gets Kṛṣṇa. Of course, to get direct service to Kṛṣṇa is a very great and rare achievement. I don’t have such a bold ambition as of now.

This is my desire, though it may be selfish: I just want to see you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, maybe it is a foolish desire. You said that personal association is for fools. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am a fool. I want to see your lotus feet and place them on my head at least once every lifetime. I want to leave this body meditating very intensely on your lotus feet.
