And then, although I cannot ever deserve it, I request that I may be brought into your presence wherever you are and that you will kindly place your lotus feet on my head and cast your merciful glance upon me. This is my ambition and desire.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray to you for the strength to check my madness. I don’t want that when the time comes to quit this body, I will have to lament a wasted life. I cannot expect to achieve much, but I want to be able to honestly present myself to you and say, “I tried.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you identified yourself as a soldier of Kṛṣṇa. You prayed to Kṛṣṇa for the benediction to fight for Him up to the last breath, as Arjuna did. So please induct me as a soldier in your army. Please push me into the preaching fight, despite my unwillingness—”With Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa on the battlefield of life.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have many disciples. All have their realizations of you and their relationship with you. In the future, more and more souls will come to the umbrella shade of your lotus feet. I am just one of many, many “*duḥkhīs*” who by your causeless mercy have become “*sukhī.*” Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep a place for me also in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your still fallen but ever hopeful servant,  
Bhaktivikāśa Swami

## Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In attempting to relive the “good old days” of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, it is important to remember what the “good old days” were based on: complete dedication to you, complete dedication to your instructions, and absolute faith that by simply following your instructions we would be successful.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja gives the following definition of faith in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Mādhya-līlā* 22.62):

*‘śraddhā’-śabde viśvāsa kahe sudṛdha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

**“By rendering transcendental loving service to Kṛṣṇa, one automatically performs all subsidiary activities. This confident, firm faith, favorable to the discharge of devotional service, is called** ***śraddhā*****.”**

All problems on a personal level or movement-wide level can be traced to a lack of faith in your instructions.

We tend toward sophistication, which inevitably means change, which you cautioned us about. We want to change the format of your books, change your instructions, change your morning program, change your *Back to Godhead* magazine, institute new improved methods of fund-raising, being coached by expert demons, and a variety of other radical reforms.

We are thinking that we will hit upon the magic formula that will enable us to immediately spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

That magic formula is already there. We must get back to your basics: books are the basis, purity is the force, preaching is the essence, and utility is the principle.

Following these instructions would constitute a revolution. This revolution must start from the top. The leaders must set a perfect example of dedication to you and all your instructions—large and small. All instructions must be followed. No long hair. No *karmī* schools for our children. Attendance at the morning program. Grass roots preaching. Renunciation of opulent lifestyles and salaries. No *karmī* food. To mention a few.

In other words, we have to live in your *gurukula* as *brahmacārīs*. Even your householder disciples are meant to be *brahmacārīs* in a larger sense; you coined the phrase “*brahmacāri-gṛhasthas*.” This is the real meaning of what it means to be part of ISKCON. Being in ISKCON has nothing to do with legalities or geographical locations. Nārada Muni instructs us on this subject matter:

*brahmacārī guru-kule*  
*vasan dānto guror hitam*  
*ācaran dāsa-van nīco*  
*gurau su-dṛdha-sauhṛdaḥ*

**“A student should practice completely controlling his senses. He should be submissive and should have an attitude of firm friendship for the spiritual master. With a great vow, the** ***brahmacārī*** **should live at the** ***gurukula*** **only for the benefit of the** ***guru*****” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.12.1).**

A child is meant to be trained up in this manner so that he will internalize the “*gurukula* consciousness.” So he can in fact remain eternally in the *gurukula*.

We can study this verse to learn about the responsibility of a true disciple. Unfortunately, most of us did not have the opportunity to imbibe these teachings at an early age, but hopefully we can change.

I pray that you allow me to remain eternally disciplined by you in your *gurukula*.

Your servant,  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet, along with this humble offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

**Prema for the Simple and Sane**

I am not a writer,  
as a poet I’m worse,  
So only by your mercy  
may I write some verse.

The ways of God’s Māyā,  
so subtle and strong  
That even your śiṣya  
may say something wrong.

He said you had taught  
for the masses who came,  
By giving just law  
and not Kṛṣṇa-prema.

I looked at him aghast  
and cut his false notion,  
Read chapter nineteen of  
the Nectar of Devotion.

“The best process of prema  
to relieve our hunger,  
Ādau śraddhā  
tataḥ sādhu-saṅgaḥ.”

If faith, though, is first  
to attain our NEW goal,  
Your life and your talk  
give us trust in the soul.

We thus take your words  
as true to the letter.  
Ordered to hell,  
we’d think nothing better

If sādhu-saṅga’s next  
on the bhakti ladder,  
To deny our fortune,  
nothing can be madder.

For our guru’s dear men  
are a mine of jewels;  
To serve just one  
is the highest of rules.

To bring out devotion,  
we also need practice;  
To follow our minds  
will not really correct us.

Five potent practices,  
by your careful design,  
Are thus followed by us  
from four until nine.

If our heart’s a fire  
where lust has been lit,  
Lord Kṛṣṇa’s form therein  
will ne’er sit.

To cut anarthas  
and material vision,  
You bestowed upon us  
your preaching mission.

Your causeless service,  
without a moment’s waste,  
Awakens for Kṛṣṇa  
the sweetest taste.

Our addiction for Vraja  
grows day by day,  
And by that strong greed  
will come the first ray.\*

As Caitanya preached,  
you spread the name.  
As Śrī Rūpa wrote,  
you did the same

Effulgent ācārya  
of universal fame,  
How is it possible  
you did not give us prema?

By hearing this preaching  
direct from your book,  
This disciple felt badly,  
just like a crook.

With tears in his eyes  
and sad face in his hand,  
He began to retract  
his previous command.

“From the start of devotion  
to the topmost plane,  
Prabhupāda gave all  
for the simple and sane.”

Your servant,  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering on Gaura-Pūrṇimā day. The temple is celebrating. Devotees move all around, taking care of the last details for the ceremonies, while guests arrive and are received with attention and hospitality.

ISKCON’s great festival is taking place at Śrīdhām Māyāpur, but those who can’t be here—the thousands of devotees in in the hundreds of temples scattered throughout the world—are also celebrating Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s appearance day, following your instructions.

To Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitai, new clothes are being offered, along with new ornaments, *abhiśeka,* beautiful, perfumed garlands, and many tasty preparations.

Sounds of *kīrtanas, bhajanas,* readings from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and, every now and then, ecstatic shouts of “Nitai-Gaura *haribol*!*”* and “Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu *ki jaya*!” are heard along each temple’s corridors, revealing Navadvīpa-dhāma’s atmosphere to be present in every devotee’s consciousness on this day. It’s a transcendental party throughout ISKCON!

I’m a fan of your society. I talk about it with pride. And because I’m a leader, , I could even be seen as a demagogue. But what can I do? You are its founder-*ācārya*. This position has been explained elaborately by your erudite disciples, and I have understood at least a little of it. In this way I feel myself close to you and become glad, preaching the advantages of a society guided by a perfect being, a pure devotee who engages everyone in Kṛṣṇa’s devotional service with the skill of one who knows the art and science of rescuing the conditioned souls from the ocean of nescience.

You are so expert in distributing pure love of God, which flows from the reservoir broken open by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that all over the world people sing and dance in ecstasy without considering their material conditions.

The material world is destroying itself. Everyone should be in a panic because of the wars, crises, fights, hate, revenge, etc. But what we see is that, in the midst of the greatest calamities, many people approach us and become sincere devotees, surrendered to your instructions.

It’s so wonderful to contemplate and preach the glories of your society on this day, the day on which we celebrate the most blessed among all events, your appearance in this world! In spite of your physical absence, we feel the potency of your preaching by the vibrations of the ecstatic cries scattered through the corridors of the ISKCON world:

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

These cries reveal that the ISKCON *dhāma’s* atmosphere is already impressed upon the consciousness in general. It’s a festival in the world!

Your eternal servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Dyutidhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances and small attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace.

Like sunlight’s shining splendor,  
You entered the forest’s floor  
And awakened an unbeliever  
Who had always dreamed of more.

“Rise up and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa!”  
Came your bold appeal.  
“You’re living an illusion;  
Nothing here is real.”

Snapping chains of doubt and fear—  
The bondage suddenly ceased.  
And one who had toiled fruitlessly  
Was magically released.

“What is this mercy medicine  
You’ve so sublimely brought?  
Is this the Lord’s compassion?”  
Came the revolutionary thought.

How can there be a person  
So loving and so true?  
Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
To you this tribute’s due.

You’ve given all, you’ve taken naught,  
And pure devotion is your fame.  
Please let us always reside with you  
And chant Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Gaṇapati Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet, where the most intelligent *mahātmas* aspire to reside as an atom of dust. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

By your mercy we have had the good fortune to attract many sincere seekers to the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness this spring. This activity of instrumentally assisting your mission is the panacea for a heart full of envy and faultfinding. Unless one tries to follow in your footsteps and enthusiastically preach, it becomes all too easy to analyze and minimize your amazing accomplishments as the World Saṅkīrtana Leader. Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s antagonists thought Him to be just a little more opulent, powerful, or lucky than they, my arrogant mind supposes you to be a little special: “Any of Prabhupāda’s Godbrothers could have done it.” But as the demons, despite their criticisms, could not lift Govardhana Hill, neither can I even begin to imitate your achievements.

Although I understand that to envy the spiritual master or consider him a common, ordinary man is the pathway to hell (*nārakī saḥ*), I am still not surprised by my tendency to do so. After all, it is due to my envy of the Supreme Lord (*icchā-dveṣa-samutthena …* ) that I have submerged my consciousness in His inferior potency. And since the spiritual master is the direct manifestation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, it is “natural” that my deep-rooted jealousy be directed toward yourself.

I realize that this envy is not a new phenomenon. My entire life epitomizes asuric behavior toward others:

1\) To those more qualified—envious.  
2\) To those equally qualified—proud of my activities.  
3\) To those less qualified—oppressive and derisive.

As a young boy I envied my cousin, who was older, bigger, stronger, and more opulent than I. When he died of a brain hemorrhage, I cried false tears. My spite found new objects, however, in my teenage years—those who were more popular with the girls or had better grades. After a few years of college, drugs, and “Eastern” philosophy, my malice overtly manifested itself as *tat tvam asi*. Fortunately, after a couple of decades, my beleaguered heart took refuge under the cooling rays of your lotus feet, and I joined the local ISKCON center. For a brief period I found solace and freedom in the attitude of *dāsa-dāsānudāsa.* Being a sincere sweeper of the stool room was better than being a charlatan meditator or a “frog in the well.”

Gradually my old comrade (envy) announced his continued presence. He had just been waiting on the shore for the initial waves of bliss to subside. “Why do I have to do the menial services?” my mind asked. “If they only knew how qualified I was.” So I set out to do the most notable service—book distribution. “The temple should quickly recognize me as the most fired-up preacher.” But then, “Why not become the TSKP leader? The current leader is a fallen *gṛhastha* anyway.”

As time passed, Your Divine Grace officially accepted me as your disciple and agreed to free me from the reactions to my previous sinful acts. My old nature, however, still persisted. I wanted to become a *brāhmaṇa:* “Surely that will satisfy me.” You mercifully gave me second initiation, but the sound of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s flute was drowned by the shrill screams of my material ambitions. “I need more authority. Why not become a Rādhā-Dāmodara TSKP bus leader?” That wasn’t enough. “If I take *sannyāsa,* others will look up to me (while bowing down, of course).” But what I really needed was more responsibility: “How about GBC? Prabhupāda said they were his best men.” Something was still missing. “If I just had a few disciples, I could be absolute.”

So you allowed me to “serve” you in this way, too, Śrīla Prabhupāda, though I finally realized that all along I was only serving my false ego. The envy was still there: “Others have more disciples. They give better lectures. Their disciples are more qualified.” And so on … What’s next? I could try for “avatārship.” But isn’t that the original reason I chose to come to this miserable material world? There is one alternative—surrender. I hope I’m ready for it. You are definitely demanding it more and more. You say that an envious person cannot advance, cannot be happy, and cannot enter the spiritual world. Still I’m envious. What can be done? The *mantra* can work on the serpent, but it doesn’t work on me. Please help me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to be a simple, sincere, uninhibited, and unassuming stool cleaner again. Maybe this time I can start with my own heart.

Your aspiring servant,  
Gaṇapati Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

With my limited realization of who you are, I dredge my barren heart to compose some adequate words in glorification of your divine qualities and pastimes. But as a conditioned soul, I cannot even fully understand your fathomless glories, much less try to properly describe them. My only hope is that you accept my meager attempt at trying to glorify you, just as a father is sometimes pleased by hearing his small son childishly (but lovingly) try to describe the attributes of his father to others.

In the past year you have given me two essential realizations: first, that you are the most compassionate personality in all the three worlds; and second, that the only way I can please you is by sharing your unlimited compassion with others by preaching forcefully, as you did.

On numerous occasions you stated that a Vaiṣṇava preacher must embody the quality of compassion if he expects to successfully deliver the message of Lord Caitanya to the fallen conditioned souls. Of all the twenty-six devotional qualities you embody, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your quality of compassion appears to me most prominent.

When others may have thought it an “overendeavor” to cross the sea at such an advanced age and deliver Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy to the Western world, you boarded the *Jaladuta* at age sixty-nine with hardly more than a few clothes, some books, and eight dollars’ worth of rupees. Many thought that it was “too dangerous” to live in the Bowery; you thought, “This is my home.” Your moving to the Lower East Side and preaching to the hippies may have been viewed by some as “a disturbance to the consciousness”; you felt it to be the fulfillment of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī’s order. Most would have thought it “too austere” to rise shortly after midnight to write your books; you said, “This is my life’s work—my devotional ecstasy.” After incorporating ISKCON, having *kīrtana* at Tompkins Square Park, giving initiations, opening new centers, inaugurating book distribution, forming the GBC, and so much more … many may have thought that so much traveling and management was a “big botheration”; you thought, “This is *arcana*.” And during the final days of your manifest pastimes, when others would have felt “inconvenienced” being surrounded by so many disciples, all requiring your attention, guidance, and encouragement, you thought, “This is my duty.”

As a perfect *ācārya,* you worked so hard and sacrificed so much—more than countless volumes of Vyāsa-pūjā books could describe. And you did it all out of your unlimited compassion to relieve the suffering of the fallen conditioned souls of the Kali-yuga by giving them Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I aspire to be your real disciple—one who follows your instructions perfectly and works tirelessly to serve your mission. I realize this may take many lifetimes, considering the fact that I am still honeycombed with unlimited *anarthas*. But in the interim I am fully convinced that if somehow or other my life can be used by you in preaching work, this will in some small way please you.

Therefore, on this all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, I pray with all sincerity that you bless me to develop a fraction of the compassion that you have for the fallen conditioned souls. With these blessings of yours, I will never again refer to the trials and tribulations of preaching work as “overendeavor,” “too dangerous,” “a disturbance to my consciousness,” “too austere,” “a big botheration,” or an “inconvenience.” At all times and in all places I will simply feel the greatest ecstasy knowing that my body, mind, and words are being used to introduce you and your teachings to others.

Aspiring to become your sincere disciple, I remain  
Your eternal servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Guṇa-grāhī dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvati deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Prayer of Longing**

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while offering you homage, prostrated at your feet, will I, who am expert at making only a show of devotion, express genuine appreciation for your limitless mercy upon me?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while preaching on your behalf, will I, who always retreat at the slightest hint of adversity or dishonor, take strength from your bold and fearless demeanor?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while feeling plagued by the miseries of the body, will I, meditating on your undaunted determination in the face of old age, disease, and death, throw off my lame excuses for inertia?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while being dragged by the crow of my mind through the refuse heap of material desires, will I cry out to be rescued by the swan of attachment to your lotus feet?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while upon hearing your words, which strike like sharp arrows into the raging beast of foolish and destructive thought, will I, who am the keeper of this beast, no longer give it shelter?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while fleeing into the darkness of forgetfulness of Krsna, will I allow you, who have never for a moment forgotten Him, to capture me and illuminate the sky of my heart?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when, while singing words of praise to you each morning, will I allow the desert of my mind, parched by the heat of preoccupation, to be irrigated by the life-giving waters of your transcendental glories?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will I, who in secret alliance with the foes of envy and profit calculation have been opposing your dear servants, confront those foes and smash them with your pillar of love and trust?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will your order—”If you can’t increase, at least maintain”—which, because we failed to keep it, stands over us like an admonishing parent, be fulfilled?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will we expand on that order so that this land, saturated with sin, can be saved from the great calamities that await it?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will I, the embodiment of miserliness, cease being an impediment to the fulfillment of that order?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will I, whose eyes you are forcing open, stop forcing them closed again and yield to your loving guidance?

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will I, as the days turn to weeks and the months to years, wake up from my slumber of frivolity and heed your innumerable warnings that this life is quickly passing?

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, this lowly servant begs to offer this humble prayer of longing at your lotus feet.

Guṇa-grāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this year I would like to offer you a poem, a true account of how your mercy touched the heart of a conditioned soul, and was never forgotten.

**You Will Never Die**

The other day I noticed a man,  
During my japa session,  
Who stared at me in disbelief  
As I walked in contemplation.

He studied me for quite some time,  
Yet showed some hesitation,  
But finally coming to my side,  
He began a conversation.

“I met your master,” he said quite fondly,  
“Many years ago.  
“I know him well! He spoke to me!  
And gave my life great hope!

“Since then I’ve followed the instructions  
He gave to us that night,  
I chant God’s name without cessation  
And do all the things he said are right.

“I neither smoke, nor drink,  
Nor take intoxication,  
I’ve given up meat and sex as well,  
And all types of speculation!

“Every day I think of him  
And pray to him as well.  
For he has saved me from material life;  
As I would have surely gone to hell.”

With that he smiled and took my hand  
And asked to meet you once again;  
But when I told him you’d since departed,  
he said now he’d lost his dearest friend.

For since that day he heard from you,  
many years ago,  
His only goal in life had been  
To practice what you’d told.

But just before he started to leave  
My curiosity got the best of me,  
And I inquired, “Dear sir, please tell now,  
Where you met my master and how this came to be.”

“Oh, yes,” he said, “fifteen years ago on this very day,  
I walked the streets in desperation, looking for a drink,  
When suddenly I heard his voice within the hall that night,  
And looking through the door, I tried to take a peek.

“It was late at night and his program had finished,  
Except for his few last words:  
‘So take this Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously  
And you’ll leave this material world!

“ ‘But you must give up meat, sex, and drugs,  
And please no mental speculation!  
And every moment that you have,  
Chant God’s name without cessation:

“ ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa!  
Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare!  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma!  
Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare!  
… Thank you very much!’

“Suddenly the program was over,  
And he left the stage amidst the roar  
Of those very names that he had given,  
The names of our beloved Lord!

“So now you see why I feel bereft  
Of his divine association,  
For I saw him briefly—for just a moment—  
But he showed me true perfection.

“I came into his presence  
For a fraction of his time,  
But his instructions entered into my heart  
And made my life sublime.

“But now you tell me that he’s gone,  
So where will I turn for shelter?  
Where is that person now,  
Whom I want to serve forever?”

“Fear not, my friend,” as I took his hand,  
and led him toward the temple,  
“For Prabhupāda lives forever,  
And his followers live with him.

“A moment’s association with a pure devotee  
Can show us the path of perfection,  
And once we’ve started, if we persevere  
We’ll surely have his association.

“So come with me now and see his mūrti,  
And read the books he’s written,  
And live with his devotees and share their joy  
Of the eternal life he’s given.

“And remember the words of a great Ṭhākur  
Who gave counsel wise and just,  
By giving us the reassurance  
That saints will never leave us:

“ ‘He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!’ “

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I’m excited about your *Back to Godhead*. And I’m going to write about it here because BTG is a manifestation, an extension, an opulence, of Your Divine Grace. As you once said, “Actually I am writing all the articles. They should just be divided up under different names.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we’ve seen time and again that your plans are the best plans. Each of your plans is like a seed. We just have to plant it, water it, care for it, stick with it, and soon it will grow into a big, sturdy tree, yielding fruits profusely, sweet as anything.

*Back to Godhead* is one of those plans—perhaps your first plan. As you told us, “*Back to Godhead* is the beginning of my spiritual life.” You personally started it and cared for it in the beginning and then handed it over to us, your disciples.This tree of *Back to Godhead* is still small. And two years ago we even trimmed it back. But now it’s growing again, and we pray for your mercy so that it may grow dramatically, incredibly, supernaturally.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu planted a mango seed, and within no time it grew into a complete tree, full of mangoes—with no seeds and no skins, just nectar. The Lord did it because His devotees were performing *saṅkīrtana* and mangoes is what they wanted. And the devotees went on relishing the mangoes, day after day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m convinced that the BTG tree you’ve given us is a *kalpa-vṛkṣa*. So I look forward to similar wonders.

Lord Kṛṣṇa is the root of the tree, and Your Divine Grace is the trunk. The writers, artists, photographers, and readers are the branches, leaves, and flowers. And Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the fruit.

I don’t want to oppress the analogy. So let me get back to things literal.Your movement is growing, but where? In the temples, yes. But mostly outside. Many devotees live dispersed here and there, in clusters or by themselves. Some are weak, some strong, some in between.

They’re attached to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And many who are detached hanker to be connected once again.

And new people are out there—we don’t even know how many. Thousands have read your books and told themselves, “This is interesting. This makes sense. This sounds like what I want to be into.”

And still more people are yet to read your books and find out that what you say makes sense to them too.

And that’s not even counting all the Indian people who have the culture, training, and piety to recognize that Your Divine Grace is spreading *sanātana-dharma* authentically, as it is.

All these people can be united, connected, enlivened, inspired through the medium of your *Back to Godhead*. Whatever darkness covers them, the light of *Back to Godhead* can drive it away. Through the message of *Back to Godhead,* the weak can become strong, and the strong still stronger.

Pulled together through *Back to Godhead,* they can all take part—and take pleasure—in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the foremost transcendental *saṅkīrtana* leader. And BTG is one of your big *mṛdaṅga* parties. It’s a party anyone can easily join in. And it can come to everyone’s home, again and again, periodically throughout the year.

The more your big *mṛdaṅga* keeps beating, the more the *saṅkīrtana* movement keeps moving. From 2,000 subscribers, we’ve now gone to 7,000. And by your mercy we can go to 14,000, then 40,000, then on to 100,000 and more.

If even one reader goes back to Godhead, the magazine will be a success. But by your mercy I expect waves of readers to go.

Your servant,  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Jayadhvaja Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Vyāsa-pūjā’s day one year has passed,  
still serving you in ISKCON camp,  
pulled by your mercy, Māyā’s trick didn’t act.

In the beginning Brahmā heard “tapa.”  
Veda came to him with good signs—  
Nārada, Vyāsa, then divide,  
Śukadeva gave to Parīkṣit on the Ganges bank,  
Nitāi-Gaurāṅga appeared in Bengal,  
Bhaktisiddhānta instructed you in the dhāma,  
historical distributor Prabhupāda glorified.

In Bhārata-bhūmi you have taken birth  
to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world.

Madhu and Kaiṭabha the Vedas stole;  
Māyāvādīs come in Kali-yuga to play this role;  
Prabhupāda, you rescued the Vedas from the demons’ hold.

Ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt.  
Demigods you surpass.  
Brahmā, Nārada, and Vyāsa join you in Ratha-yātrā  
due to your ecstatic leading of harināma-saṅkīrtana.

Śiva, Pārvatī, and Gaṇeśa join you as your guests.  
Candra, Varuṇadeva you engage in the service of the  
Lord; nobody you spare.

Kṛṣṇa’s Arjuna at Kurukṣetra fought.  
Gītā as it is you presented,  
as Kṛṣṇa spoke it.  
Kṛṣṇa’s teachings not watered down.  
You give the light of guru-paramparā.

The great saint Śukadeva on the shore of the Ganges spoke,  
and this transcendental knowledge Prabhupāda  
brought to New York.  
Vedavyāsa has divided the Vedas into four;  
Prabhupāda is distributing as his guru told.

In the early morning, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam you translate,  
to let Kṛṣṇa speak for the first time in the West.

To so-called swamis, gurus, and saints  
your chopping technique makes them alert;  
their so-called commentaries have led everyone astray.

Kali-yuga enters by the brāhmaṇa’s curse,  
disguised as a king to cause turmoil;  
you have given us four principles to stop his plot.

In Naimiṣāraṇya the meeting took place;  
you are the representative of great sages and saints.

Rāmānuja, Madhva, and Śrīdhara Svāmī descend—  
all their conclusions you present.

Nitai-Gaurāṅga follow you;  
dear devotee mercy is due.  
Prabhupāda, dancing and chanting, delivers the fools.

Bhaktivinoda’s dream has come true:  
the city you built fulfills the due.

In guru-pūjā we are getting inspired  
by seeing your worship, Vedavyāsa’s smile.

Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, let me serve you now  
and distribute books with you in harināma-saṅkīrtana.

I beg to remain your eternal servant,  
Jayadhvaja Swami

## Kṛṣṇa dāsa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

lease accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you on this, the most auspicious anniversary of your appearance day to save the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy you are using me as a pawn in your great mission to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement throughout the world, even though I have no personal qualifications to do anything of any value for you. I was extremely privileged last year to attend the opening of another temple in your movement’s history, the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple in Mauritius. While there I witnessed an amazing development in that *yātrā,* which I would now like to relate to you for your pleasure and the pleasure of all the devotees. The more I see how that *yātrā* is developing, the more I realize the potency of your prediction that Mauritius will one day become the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world.

Of all the Indians who left their holy land to go to other countries, it is the people of Mauritius who have held on to their culture more or less intact. When their forefathers left India hundreds of years ago, they were poor and simple people. They thought that they would work hard and earn enough money to one day return to India, not realizing that neither they, their children, nor their children’s children would ever be able to go back. Many of them dreamt of going back to Bhārata-varṣa and performing pilgrimages to the holy *dhāmas* just as their elders had done before them, but they dreamt in vain.

But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, took pity on these people—not only upon them but upon the whole world. You realized that for many people it would be impossible to come to the land of Kṛṣṇa. So instead, in 1966, you left India to give Kṛṣṇa to the whole world. You gave the world the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa; the *mahā-mantra; harināma-saṅkīrtana;* your books; *prasādam; ratha-yātrās;* restaurants; farming communities; and beautiful temples where devotees engage in the service of Their Lordships Śrī-Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. It is for this reason that I was overjoyed to attend the opening of the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple in Mauritius. Now, out of Their causeless mercy, Their Lordships have come to give Their *darśana* to these villagers, most of whom might not have had the opportunity to see Them otherwise.

One evening I found myself unable to sleep. I was very restless thinking about the name of the village in which we had opened our new temple. The name of this village was Bud Bhuva, which means “bad smell.” I was very disturbed at the prospect of Their transcendental Lordships Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma staying in a place whose name meant “bad smell.” Therefore I prayed to you and Their Lordships for strength and guidance. After some deliberation I decided to see the representatives of the village council the following day.

Prior to the opening of our temple, the village council had already approved changing the name of one road leading into the village to “Hare Krishna Road.” So my first request to them was that they should change the name of the other road on the other side of the village to “Hare Krishna Road” also. To my surprise they immediately agreed, and not only that, but they were actually pleased with the idea. Feeling encouraged, I proceeded to explain what the name of their village meant. I explained how, now that Their Lordships Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma had come to their village, this name was very inappropriate. I suggested that since Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma enjoyed many of Their childhood pastimes in Vṛndāvana, they should rename their village Vṛndāvana. To my amazement they became excited at the suggestion and immediately agreed.

The word spread, and soon there was an atmosphere of excitement all around the temple and the village. Actually, the mood in the temple was so ecstatic that devotees took this incident as a sign of your desire to realize your prediction sooner than expected. Therefore, over the next few days, while touring and preaching in the area, the devotees approached various other village councils about name changes. By your mercy they were so successful that now there are over twenty villages, hills, and lakes that have or soon will have spiritual names.

So now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as we have a Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple in India, by your mercy we now have a Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple in Mauritius. Just as there is the holy *dhāma* of Vṛndāvana in India, by your mercy we now have a Vṛndāvana in Mauritius. Just as the places around Vṛndāvana all have spiritual names invoking remembrance of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, so by your mercy similar spiritual names are now appearing around the village of Vṛndāvana in Mauritius.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are simply fools, but by your mercy you are making us do incredible things. Some people may wrongly credit such successes to us, but the truth is that you are actually performing all these miracles and that we are just puppets in your hands. All we have to do is simply surrender at your lotus feet and follow your every instruction.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know that I am completely unqualified to serve you. But please don’t abandon this useless soul; please always act as my light-bearing guide on the long road back home, back to Godhead, back to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Your completely dependent child,  
Kṛṣṇa dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This ninety-fifth Vyāsa-pūjā marks the beginning of the five-year centennial celebration. Beginning today, we would like a celebration every day leading up to the grand celebration on September 6, 1996, in Calcutta.

*Ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana.* Your glories are spread all over the three worlds.

During your lifetime you never cared for your personal name or fame. You dedicated your life to spread Kṛṣṇa’s name. That Kṛṣṇa, whose name you popularized, now inspires us to spread your glories all over the world.

For the last several years Padayātrā devotees have been talking about organizing Padayātrā in a hundred countries to celebrate your hundredth anniversary of your appearance. The word has spread around, and the hundredth anniversary celebration is becoming the talk of ISKCON. Especially at the last Māyāpur festival, there were lots of discussions among your senior men. The spirit now seems to be encompassing all aspects of our devotional service.

Your senior men have prepared a tentative list of things that ISKCON should accomplish by the hundredth anniversary of your appearance day in 1996:

• 1 billion pieces of literature printed and distributed  
• 1 billion persons having honored *prasādam*.  
• 100 *sannyāsīs* recognized by the international community as bona fide teachers of Vedic philosophy  
• 100 countries hosting Padayātrā  
• 100 scientists supporting the Bhaktivedanta Institute  
• 100 scholars glorifying your books.  
• 100,000 subscribers to *Back to Godhead* magazine  
• 100 restaurants worldwide  
• 100 languages that your books have been published in  
• 100,000 congregational members internationally  
• 10 memorials (libraries, museums, hospitals, roads, etc.)  
• 100% of initiated devotees sheltered and regularly connected with ISKCON

There are similar goals for Ratha-yātrā, life membership, university preaching, cow protection, Food for Life—the list goes on and on.

Your ISKCON, founded in 1966, is now twenty-five years old. That makes 1991 ISKCON’s silver jubilee. ISKCON has come through the best of times and the worst of times. There is certainly a lot to learn from ISKCON’s successes and failures. To err is human, but one who learns from his mistakes is recognized as a wise man.

One of our many failures is that we have lost many of your spiritual children and grandchildren.

Last year in my offering I mentioned my involvement in *dharma-yuddha*. This is our fight for our religious rights in North America. This involved the famous *Robin George* vs. *ISKCON* legal battle. We prayed to the Deities to keep the temples open for the devotees. While offering those prayers, I wondered to myself, “For which devotees should the Lord keep the temples open?” Where are the devotees? Maybe the Deities are lonely. In some big temples the number of Deities is greater than the number of devotees. On some temple walls, big photos are hanging showing that same temple flooded with devotees during your times. And now to compare the same temple with just a handful of devotees is just a heart-breaking experience.

The above-mentioned challenging five-year goals should pull each one of the devotees to higher levels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, maturity, expertise, etc. Keeping you in the forefront of our mind as we become fully Prabhupāda conscious, we want to go all out to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness for your pleasure.

There is so much to do, we have many promises to keep and many miles to go before we sleep.

*Prabhupāda-śakti vinā nahi tāra pravartana:* “Without being empowered by you, we have no hope of accomplishing anything at all.”

A beggar at your lotus feet,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering to a picture of you that sits in your *bhajana kuṭira* in Vṛndāvana. Every day since March 1977, when you personally instituted the service, your disciples have offered your daily lunch *prasādam* to this picture, which is nondifferent from you.

Taken in November 1972, this timeless photograph shows you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, honoring *prasādam* in the kitchen of your “eternal home,” the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Sevā-kuñja, Vṛndāvana. In color photos your effulgent, beautiful golden form adorned with bright saffron cloth immediately captures our attention and satisfies our spiritual senses. But this photo is black and white. Your colorful s*annyāsa* garments and brilliant body are covered with dark tones and highlights. This photo is unique in that it’s saturated with the intensity of your mood, Śrīla Prabhupāda: determined, daring, fixed, and empowered with Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s message.

How would a magazine editor correctly caption this photograph? “Śrīla Prabhupāda taking p*rasādam* at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple.” Obviously, this caption doesn’t sufficiently portray the deeper, inner meaning that powerfully resonates here. Resting part of your weight on your left hand, you sit humbly on the floor as you deftly and delicately grasp a portion of pr*asādam* with your right hand. You appear relaxed but poised for action.

Your lotus face is fully illuminated by the brilliant sunshine penetrating through the sandstone latticework window overlooking Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi*. The *Brahma-saṁhitā* explains that during creation the Supreme Lord, Mahā-Viṣṇu, impregnates the living entities into the womb of material nature by His glance. Similarly, in this photo the sunlight beaming through the window appears like the merciful glance of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī that is falling directly upon you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. With that glance from his *samādhi,* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī infused you with the spiritual potency to preach pure devotional service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa throughout the world.

*Sevā* (service) begins with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, who eternally serves Śrīmatī Rādhāraṇī in Sevā-kuñja (the service grove) by massaging Her lotus feet and decorating Her hair and Her splendidly beautiful transcendental form. Five hundred years ago Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī sat in Sevā-kuñja (the present site of the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple and your *bhajana kuṭira*) and served Śrīmatī Rādhāraṇī in the form of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu by executing His order to preach and write about the science of devotional service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is called the *abhidheya ācārya,* the spiritual master of serving Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī himself was a perfect servant. And he taught devotees how to correctly and pleasingly serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindajī.

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you spent the first six years of your s*annyāsa* in Sevā-kuñja, serving Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī by compiling books on *bhakti,* the science of pure devotional service. Empowered by the service *ācārya* (Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī) you then left the service forest (Sevā-kuñja) and courageously entered the sophisticated jungle of New York City. You established Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s Deity of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindajī (the service Deities), and you taught the aborigines how to serve Them. In fact, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you compassionately ventured into the jungles of every major continent to convert the forests of material enjoyment into replicas of Śrī Vṛndāvana’s Sevā-kuñja. Sex and sense gratification were replaced by surrender and service to Rādhā-govinda.

Boldly you traversed the forest’s dangerous paths, filled with mad monkeys, envious snakes, and atheistic tigers. You tamed all the two-legged animals, filled their bellies with pr*asādam,* and led them in the “swami step” across the forest floors.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you conquered all the beastly conditioned souls with service to Kṛṣṇa. Patiently and expertly you taught us how to serve the Rādhā-govinda Deities, serve Tulasī-devī, serve Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, serve *prasādam,* serve the spiritual master, serve the Vaiṣṇavas, serve Lord Caitanya by preaching to others—serve, serve, and forever serve. *Jīvera svarūpa haya kṛṣṇera nitya-dāsa.* In his spiritual form every living entity is an eternal servant of Rādhā-Govinda.

As Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, Rūpa Gosvamī sat in Sevā-kuñja to serve humanity, and as Śrīla Rūpa Mañjarī she eternally resides in Sevā-kuñja (in the spiritual world) rendering loving service to Rādhā-Gopīnātha. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami you resided in Sevā-kuñja serving humanity by compiling your purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* teaching one and all to find happiness, peace, and perfection through the loving service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda. And now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as an intimate servant of Śrī Rūpa-mañjarī you eternally reside in Sevā-kuñja (in goloka Vṛndāvana) rendering pure loving service for the pleasure of Rādhā-Gopīnātha.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an eternal resident of Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma,* the blissful spiritual world, where everyone is fully surrendered and serves Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa with dedication and selfless love. Please bestow your mercy upon this lowly one, so far away from the world of pure devotional service. We are trapped in the jungle of exploitation and bound by the ropes of selfishness. We beg your mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please lift us out of this dark jungle of forgetfulness of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and into the brilliantly blissful forest of Sevā-kuñja. Please grant us eternal service at your lotus feet and the lotus feet of the divine couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha. Śrīla Prabhupāda, our only prayer is that you please grant us the completely undeserved privilege of pure devotional service and allow us to serve you birth after birth.

Your servant,  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Nava-yogendra Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are very merciful and kind upon the fallen souls like myself, who have no material or spiritual qualification.

My dear Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your appearance day. Yesterday was the appearance day of your Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa is yours, and if you like you can give Kṛṣṇa to anyone:

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra kṛṣṇa dite pāro*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to’ kāṅgāla, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

**“Kṛṣṇa is yours. You are able to give Him to me, for such is your power. I am indeed wretched and simply run after you, crying ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!’ ”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave Kṛṣṇa to the people of the whole world. Those who have received Kṛṣṇa by your mercy are very happy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were eager to give Kṛṣṇa to everyone. Without Śrī Kṛṣṇa, our lives are useless. Only Śrī Kṛṣṇa can make us happy, and the purpose of this life is to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. No one knew this real business of human life. After going through so many hardships and so many difficulties, you taught the people of the whole world their real business.

In this material world, if someone knows some business tactics, he won’t teach them to anyone. A materialistic person wants to be successful himself. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, gave this most confidential knowledge to the people of the whole world and saved them from gliding to a hellish condition of life. How can we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? We cannot repay our debt to you, even if we walk on our hands for the rest of our lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me the strength to become your true follower. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am full of defects and bad qualities, but at the same time I want to be your true follower. It is only possible by your causeless mercy. Your causeless mercy is boundless. Please make me the recipient of your mercy. As you personally instructed me to go to different parts of the world and preach, I am endeavoring to do that to please Your Divine Grace. You would become very pleased whenever anyone would give you preaching reports or book distribution reports. Your movement is a preaching movement. Kindly bestow your blessings so that I can become a good preacher and be instrumental to help the suffering souls.

Due to your causeless mercy, and with the help of some devotees, I have been able to acquire a piece of land in Udhampur, Jammu, India, where we have already begun planning to build a new temple. We have also acquired donations for two pieces of land in Katra, Jammu, and planning is underway for ISKCON centers in these areas.

This area is a place of pilgrimage for Hindus, and on average ten thousand people come here every day. We are endeavoring to distribute books to these pilgrims, and I hope many more devotees will join me to help expand this project.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is the most glorious day. You appeared today to save the fallen souls. Who can be like you? You appeared for others. Everyone is simply worried for their own welfare, but you were worried for others. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are wonderful.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your most fallen disciple,  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Paraṁ-gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The living entity, bound up in the cycle of birth and death, cannot really know what is right and what is wrong. In this condition he concocts different formulas to solve his problems, but he remains entangled, with no way to find real happiness. Among the very few people who understand that they must try to get free of the cycle of birth and death, most try to get out of it by such processes as mystic *yoga,* the performance of sacrifices, or philosophical inquiry. But by these processes they cannot arrive at a genuine understanding of the Absolute Truth, and thus they cannot get completely free of their sufferings; the only way to do that is to accept direction from a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought to this world the key to open the doors to reality. You taught the world the practical means of *yukta-vairāgya—anāsaktasya viṣayān yathārham upayuñjataḥ nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe—*and dispelled all doubts for the suffering human beings who heard your message. You showed them the intricacies of the law of *karma* and the way to get free of *karma* and return to the spiritual world, where, in His unlimited forms, the Supreme Personality of Godhead eternally enjoys pastimes with all His associates, culminating in His pastimes in Goloka Vṛndāvana.

By your causeless mercy you tolerated so many offenses, so many difficult situations, but at the same time you showed human society at large that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the practical means for solving all problems. Gradually humanity will discover this most precious jewel that you gave to the world. Everybody will be pleased, in all sections of society. Human society owes an eternal debt to you, and everybody will acknowledge this in the future. How far in the future depends on how fast we increase the distribution of your books. This was your main instruction, so we will never stop book distribution. Even if there are many problems, if we go on distributing more and more of your books, this will be the biggest service to you, to Lord Kṛṣṇa, to ISKCON, and to society at large.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly ask you to please always engage me in the service of your servants so that I can always execute your order. And may I never forget this most important of all your orders: “Distribute books, distribute books, distribute books.”

Your unworthy servant,  
Paraṁ-gati Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When aboard the ship *Jaladuta,* you humbly said that your qualification for preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness was your strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is an expansion of your desire to give the holy name of Kṛṣṇa to others. And when ending your manifest *līlā,* you repeatedly quoted from the *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam, nivṛtta-tarṣair upagīyamānād bhavauṣadhāt:* The best medicine in the world is *hari-kīrtana.* In the verse in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.65) where Śrī Kṛṣṇa reveals His most confidential knowledge, your confidential purport says, “One should arrange his life in such a way that throughout twenty-four hours he cannot but think of Kṛṣṇa.” Therefore, if we arrange our lives to live as simply as possible and always chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, then our next lives will be spiritual.

Simple living can be many things. It can mean *yukta-vairāgya,* that we are using all material resources at our disposal to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Our consciousness becomes simple as we see everything in its relationship to Kṛṣṇa, and thus our material desires to be independent lords and enjoyers of this material world are removed. Simple living can also mean to simplify our material lives by working only for the bare necessities of life. And the time saved can be used to engage in direct Kṛṣṇa conscious activities, beginning with hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa. By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society have been successful in many parts of the world using their material resources to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And that is most important. But especially devotees who are not fully engaged in preaching or assisting the preaching mission should show the example of how to live simply and engage fully in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

For preaching we must be very organized and we must be very pure. Organized means to arrange our movement systematically and intelligently. As you wrote in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* “Let there be systematic propaganda for popularizing reading of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which will act like sugar candy for the jaundicelike condition of sense gratification.” When people have a taste for this literature, the other literatures, which are catering poison to society, will then automatically cease to attract them. And pure means to love Kṛṣṇa and to learn to love everyone and everything as they are related to Kṛṣṇa.

Nothing is so intimately related to Kṛṣṇa in this material world as the preaching work of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. No one is so intimately related to Kṛṣṇa in this material world as one who is trying to spread His glories. No better method exists in this material world to show our love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, than to cooperatively preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the institution that you founded—ISKCON. By doing this we will naturally remember Kṛṣṇa. Thus we all have the same duty to do—to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by following your instructions completely, according to our best understanding. But let us not become complacent. You have given us a big mission—to save humanity by a Kṛṣṇa conscious cultural conquest, especially by distributing your books. And at the same time, we must save ourselves.

Your aspiring servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rāma-Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. Please accept this Vyāsa-pūjā offering at your lotus feet.

First meeting,  
Smiling face,  
Loving glance—  
You have conquered us,  
Your Divine Grace.

Philosophy,  
Books,  
Common sense—  
You have convinced us,  
Your Divine Grace.

“God is a person,  
Kṛṣṇa is God,  
You are spirit soul,  
You are His eternal servant,”  
You have taught us,  
Your Divine Grace.

Bhakti to Kṛṣṇa,  
Lord Caitanya,  
Śrī Vṛndāvana,  
Śrī Navadvīpa  
You have revealed to us,  
Your Divine Grace.

Bhakta-saṅga,  
Prasādam,  
Festivals  
You have given us  
To enjoy,  
Your Divine Grace.

ISKCON,  
Your istructions,  
Vaiṣṇavas,  
Deities,  
Tadīya  
You have given us to serve,  
Your Divine Grace.

Books,  
The Mahā-mantra,  
The saṅkīrtana movement,  
Prasādam  
You have given us to distribute,  
Your Divine Grace.

To teach us  
How to do all this,  
Books,  
Lectures,  
Letters  
You have given us,  
Your Divine Grace.

By your very life  
Among us  
You have taught us,  
Your Divine Grace.  
Lamentation,  
Loneliness,  
Weeping,  
Crying—  
Where are you now,  
Your Divine Grace?

Please melt my stone heart  
By your sweet words.  
I fall at your feet,  
Crying pitifully.  
You are the only savior for us,  
Your Divine Grace.

Rāma-Govinda Goswami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am writing this homage on Gaura-pūrṇimā, the most festive day of this whole universe. It is so because on this day, in the year 1486, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, descended directly from the spiritual world, Goloka Vṛndāvana.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have descended with the purpose of accomplishing Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, to spread the chanting of the holy names in every town and village. You went around this whole world energetically singing, teaching, and preaching the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By your infinite kindness I can occupy myself today in offering you a few timid words praising your lotus feet.

Today I make this humble attempt of praising you in order to purify myself. By glorifying such an exalted personality as you, I understand that the way of devotion to Kṛṣṇa will open itself up unlimitedly.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, many times I become astonished when I see how a materialistic person such as me, being completely controlled by the dictates of the tongue, stomach, genitals, and mind—how a person like this can maintain himself in this great *saṅkīrtana* movement. At these moments I can understand your unlimited compassion upon the fallen souls. Although I don’t have any qualification to maintain myself on this transcendental boat of ISKCON, you keep me on it. I can understand that this is happening only by your mercy. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me always perform this *saṅkīrtana-yajña* one way or another, sheltered in the shade of your lotus feet. Let me remain like an atom of dust at your lotus feet.

Although my mind is reluctant to accept Kṛṣṇa consciousness, please keep revealing that unlimited mercy to this fallen soul.

In one way or another, let me remain serving the Vaiṣṇavas, serving your mission. Permit me to be an instrument to accomplish your mission of establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. Also, by your kindness and by the kindness of my initiating spiritual master, now I am a *sannyāsī*. And as a *sannyāsī,* I must preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness unlimitedly. But my imperfections don’t permit me to do it.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, empower me to be an instrument of your mission. I sincerely desire to be such an instrument, and I know that, this being your desire also, one day I will be able to help you.

By glorifying your lotus feet each and every year, all my sinful activities will be eradicated, and then I will conduct myself in a pure way in this *saṅkīrtana* movement. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, always give me shelter in the shade of your lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Rohiṇī-kumāra Swami

Wandering along an overcrowded path,  
I didn’t know where I was going.  
Life after life I suffered nature’s wrath,  
As the stream of my karma kept flowing.

Then you came into my life  
And took a frightened boy by the hand.  
You ended my internal strife,  
Setting me on the way to a blessed land.

You didn’t know me,  
And I certainly didn’t know you,  
But you opened your heart to me,  
Obliging me to surrender to you.

It’s been a long time since that first day,  
And it pains me to see my shortcomings.  
But there really isn’t any other way,  
If you are to be the goal of my longings.

You came into my life at a time  
When I could make no conclusion.  
Holding knowledge in your hand like a knife,  
You cut me free from the bonds of illusion.

Every year on the day of your appearance  
I contemplate the wonder of your kindness:  
How you spread your mission despite interference,  
To dispel the world’s spiritual blindness.

Somewhere on the trails of eternity  
We happened to meet one day.  
By your glance of infinite fraternity  
You convinced me to follow your way.

Since then you decided to continue on  
To the place from which you came.  
But now I cannot go on,  
For without you I’m spiritually lame.

You came into my life.  
Now I need you to do so again.  
To serve you I will strive,  
If a drop of your mercy I can gain.

Your humble servant,  
Rohiṇī-kumāra Swami

## Romapāda Swami

**Part I**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

Buried amongst millions of shivering souls standing in abject darkness, stonelike of heart although humanlike in form, I came across your brilliant path, by the greatest kindness of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Infested with bad qualities, polluted by bad association, covered by ignorance, and misguided by so-called leaders and educators … “Gallons of blood I have spilled, gallons!! For each one of you to come to devotional service!”… Somehow, I received your merciful glance.

Although fussing and sputtering, speculating and dreaming, I have been tolerantly and kindly dragged by you into the *kīrtana* party of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

How bright must one luminary be for its rays to spread even to the darkest corners and crevices of creation?

How compassionate and fathomless in persistence can one preacher be to reclaim the whole world’s population to Kṛṣṇa’s loving service?

Your accomplishments indicate *no* limit.

How can one soul be everything to so many? It is more than our love for you that makes us see this way. It is your spiritual potency and how that potency has moved the world that makes us see your personal presence in all phases of our daily life. As Vallabha Bhaṭṭa said to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu,

*tāhā pravartāilā tumi,—ei ta ‘pramāṇa’*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi,—ithe nāhi āna*

**“You have spread the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore it is evident that You have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no question about it.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our father. You have given us life. You are our guardian. You maintain our spiritual existence. You are our ever well-wisher. This you have personally acclaimed in all correspondence to us.

You are our instructor. Your *vāṇī* is abundantly available, and is carrying us through our execution of devotional service.

You are the personal manifestation of Lord Nityānanda, the *guru* of all.

You are the dearest representative of Lord Caitanya, actively and with superlative excellence relieving His anxiety to return the fallen population back to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. How graciously you accept our devotion, overlook our offenses, yet train us to behave as saintly Vaiṣṇavas!

You are the bearer of devotional service, representing Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, without whose merciful presence the manifestation of devotional service in this world could not occur.

You are our constant companion and guide in service to Kṛṣṇa. All the regulations that we follow—you have personally given them to us. The standards we live by, the strength to uphold them ourselves, and the reason for propagating and training others in the same manner—you are that uncompromising principle in our life.

Our pushing forward for improvement in *sādhana* is lifeless without the presence and compelling force of your call for pure chanting and pure devotional service.

Your unseen hand appears *everywhere*—to engage us, test us, encourage us, remove obstacles, purify us, chastise us—embracing us with your loving service.

In Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī’s *Govinda-līlāmṛta,* Rādhā inquires from one *gopī*, “Where is Kṛṣṇa now?” The reply is given that Kṛṣṇa is in the groves of Vṛndāvana, receiving dancing lessons from none other than Rādhā Herself! How is this? Kṛṣṇa is seeing Rādhā in all the trees and creepers there, and in this He way is dancing. We are not Kṛṣṇa, but we also see you everywhere we go, and within everything we do. And we also want to dance to your calling.

Please pull the puppet’s strings and make us dance. Make us dance to the call of your will forever.

**Part II**

Your surrendered servants, from the ranks of disciples and granddisciples alike, are co-operating more and more. The effect is *wonderful*! And the crown resting upon such cooperative efforts is the noticeable increase of your presence within our collective devotional service.

Please make me more fit to likewise cooperate within your great *saṅkīrtana* movement, among all gradations of devotees alike.

**Part III**

*eka-deśa-sthitasyāgner*  
*jyotsnā vistāriṇī yathā*  
*parasya brahmaṇaḥ śaktis*  
*tathedam akhilaṁ jagat*

**“Just as the illumination of a fire, which is situated in one place, is spread all over, the energies of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Para-brahman, are spread all over this universe.”**

You are with Kṛṣṇa in Goloka Vṛndāvana, yet you are also still here with us, by the *kṛṣṇa-śakti* packed up within your instructions, or *vāṇī*. Lord Śiva explains in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the invisible hand of the Lord can be detected by its effects; your continuing influence, similarly, remains within your institution and your *vāṇī,* liberally delivering Kṛṣṇa in His fullness. This is your wonderful *līlā:* by adhering to your instructions we are with you, and we can only anticipate being with you in Goloka Vṛndāvana also in the eternal service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. As you so desire.

Please uplift my desires from the gutter of selfish pride and self-deceit. Please let me taste a drop of *your* desire—for in one drop alone I know I will find unlimited compassion, tireless determination, courage that knows no fear, and limitless love for Kṛṣṇa that will make this phantom world of misery appear to be nothing but a bad dream.

Please make me into your unalloyed devotee. This is my prayer and my faith in you, even if I have no good quality to encourage you to accept me.

Your aspiring servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

**Prabhupāda Gems**

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports give complete guidance and inspiration for devotional service. But particular passages will speak with greater meaning for particular individuals. The Prabhupāda gem that attracts me the most may be something that doesn’t particularly catch your attention, whereas the line that strikes you as the most forceful of all, and which you may build your whole life on, does not have that same meaning for me. But many gems will have universal appeal. With this in mind, here is something which Prabhupāda said in a conversation which I was very pleased to hear:

Using any language, you should submit to Kṛṣṇa, and you should feel that ‘I am worthless. My Guru Mahārāja has given me this chance to serve Kṛṣṇa, to offer to Kṛṣṇa.… My Lord, I am worthless. I have no capacity to serve You. But on the order of my Guru Mahārāja I am trying to serve You. Please do not take any offense. Accept whatever I can do.… I am offensive, so kindly excuse me.’ In this way be humble, meek, and offer your feeling, and Kṛṣṇa will be satisfied. Not that you have to show how you can speak in the Sanskrit language. \[Conversation, Hyderabad, April 10, 1975\]

I am glad to hear Śrīla Prabhupāda say this. It makes me aspire to serve the Lord in this mood. I also like to think of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his intimate relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa, in the terms he describes here.

There is also a hint in this statement by Prabhupāda for how we may live happily together in ISKCON. If each devotee thinks of himself or herself as an insignificant servitor and offers respects to other devotees, then the whole ISKCON will be conducted on the principle of “humbler than a blade of grass.”

In describing the various devotees of Lord Caitanya, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja appreciated them all. He wrote, “The associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu are many, but none of them should be considered lower or higher. This cannot be ascertained. All the great personalities in the line of Lord Caitanya enumerated these devotees, but they could not distinguish between the greater and lesser” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 10.4–5). In the word meanings for these verses the compound *guru-laghu-bhāva* is defined as “conceptions of high and low.” In other words, we should refrain from making the distinction that some devotees are important and some unimportant. Of course, when Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī comes to speak of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, he says, “They were the best of all.” But Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs did not think of themselves in that way. And Lord Caitanya loved *all* the devotees. For example, “Rāghava Paṇḍita’s sister Damayantī was the dear maidservant of the Lord. She always collected various ingredients with which to cook for Lord Caitanya,” and He was very pleased with her. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja writes, “Since Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, He extracts the purpose from everything. He accepted Damayantī’s affection for Him, and therefore He derived great pleasure even from the dried bitter leaves of *sukutā* and from the *kāśandi* (a sour condiment)” (*Antya-līlā* 10.18).

So this is another attractive aspect of Śrīla Prabhupāda—how he very humbly approached Lord Kṛṣṇa with his offering. Even though Prabhupāda was offering the Lord the entire Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement of devotees, he considered himself incapable. And because he himself was so humble before Kṛṣṇa, Prabhupāda appreciated the humility of his own disciples. In the “gem” I’ve quoted by him, he advises us not to be proud about our offerings but to ask Kṛṣṇa to please accept them, even though they are factually unworthy.

I would like to share another Prabhupāda gem, from a lecture Śrīla Prabhupāda gave on March 19, 1974. It’s about reading his books, specifically *Kṛṣṇa* book. Prabhupāda encourages us to overcome the weak-hearted attitude that comes when we think we have read his books “too many times”:

If we simply read *Kṛṣṇa* book, although it looks like stories, then you become delivered from the clutches of repetition of birth and death. It is so nice, because you’ll understand what is Kṛṣṇa.… He understands, “Now he is serious to understand Me.”

In the same lecture Prabhupāda advises that as we read we should pray, “Kṛṣṇa, kindly give me knowledge so I can understand.”

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day!

Normally when writing my Vyāsa-pūjā homage every year, I spend hours trying to get some glorification to come out from my dull brain. It is usually quite hard to find words to sufficiently describe your transcendental greatness.This year, however, I had a great deal of help writing my homage. That help came from a child named Jagannātha, the son of a *gṛhastha* I was visiting one day. The boy has a toy—one of those boards where you can draw a picture and with just a move of a bar erase it. I drew the Jagannātha Deity for him, and then he asked me to draw a new picture, so I drew Balarāma. Then he asked for a cow, and I drew it. But then he had a very strange request: he asked me to draw a man with a pig’s head, a goat’s leg, a camel’s body, a chicken’s hands, and a monkey’s tail. I exclaimed, “Wow! What kind of strange beast is that?” while thinking to myself, “What an imagination this child has!” But suddenly a section of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* came to mind:

*taravaḥ kiṁ na jīvanti*  
*bhastrāḥ kiṁ na śvasanty uta*  
*na khādanti na mehanti*  
*kiṁ grāme paśavo ‘pare*

*śva-viḍ-varāhoṣṭra-kharaiḥ*  
*saṁstutaḥ puruṣaḥ paśuḥ*  
*na yat-karṇa-pathopeto*  
*jātu nāma gadāgrajaḥ*

*bile batorukrama-vikramān ye*  
*na śṛṇvataḥ karṇa-pute narasya*  
*jihvāsatī dārdurikeva sūta*  
*na copagāyaty urugāya-gāthāḥ*

*bhārah paraṁ paṭṭa-kirīṭa-juṣṭam*  
*apy uttamāṅgaṁ na namen mukundam*  
*śāvau karau no kurute saparyāṁ*  
*harer lasat-kāñcana-kaṅkaṇau vā*

*barhāyite te nayane narāṇāṁ*  
*liṅgāni viṣṇor na nirīkṣato ye*  
*pādau nṛṇāṁ tau druma-janma-bhājau*  
*kṣetrāṇi nānuvrajato harer yau*

*jīvañ chavo bhāgavatāṅghri-reṇuṁ*  
*na jātu martyo ‘bhilabheta yas tu*  
*śrī-viṣṇu-padyā manujas tulasyāḥ*  
*śvasañ chavo yas tu na veda gandham*

**“Do the trees not live? Do the bellows of the blacksmith not breathe? All around us do the beasts not eat and discharge semen? Men who are like dogs, hogs, camels, and asses praise those men who never listen to the transcendental pastimes of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the deliverer from evils. One who has not listened to the messages about the prowess and marvelous acts of the Personality of Godhead and who has not sung or chanted loudly the worthy songs about the Lord is to be considered to possess earholes like the holes of snakes and a tongue like the tongue of a frog. The upper portion of the body, though crowned with a silk turban, is only a heavy burden if not bowed down before the Personality of Godhead, who can award** ***mukti.*** **And the hands, though decorated with glittering bangles, are like those of a dead man if not engaged in the service of the Personality of Godhead. Hari. The eyes which do not look at the symbolic representation of the Personality of Godhead, Viṣṇu, are like those printed on the plumes of the peacock, and the legs which do not move to the holy places are considered to be like tree trunks. The person who has not at any time received the dust of the feet of the Lord’s pure devotee upon his head is certainly a dead body. And the person who has never experienced the aroma of the** ***tulasī*** **leaves from the lotus feet of the Lord is also a dead body, although breathing” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.3.18–23).**

Then I started to draw a picture of the man described in the *Bhagavatam*. He had legs like tree trunks, the belly of an ass, and a raised tail like a dog. His chest was like the bellows of a blacksmith, he had a hunchback like a camel, and his arms and hands were like those of a corpse. He had a pig’s head, and from his mouth protruded a little frog’s tongue. He had a snake-hole ear, and his eyes were peacock feathers. He had a nice turban—but it was made of a lead cloth that weighed 200 lbs. And he was like a dead man—a walking zombie. I titled him “The Kali Man*.”*

I looked at the drawing and said to myself, “Wow! Actually, that’s me before meeting Śrīla Prabhupāda!” I also realized that it was an accurate representation of ninety-nine percent of the population in the world.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have come to transform that very strange species into a real human being, one who can go back home, back to Godhead. People in Kali-yuga are so foolish that cannot see real miracles. They are attracted by the false miracle of some magician who makes gold appear—and money from their pockets disappear. Unfortunately they do not see the real miracle: the fact that you gave a real—i.e., human—life to millions of corpselike Kali Men by giving them a way to develop love and devotion to Kṛṣṇa. You are such a great personality that you came into our zombie/animal society and made us into *real human beings,* giving us all that is needed to become pure devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Nevertheless, every time I meditate on your greatness I gulp, for I realize my shortcomings, and the next verse of the same section of the *Bhāgavatam* comes to mind:

*tad aśma-sāraṁ hṛdayaṁ batedaṁ*  
*yad gṛhyamāṇair hari-nāmadheyaiḥ*  
*na vikriyetātha yadā vikāro*  
*netre jalaṁ gātra-ruheṣu harṣaḥ*

**“Certainly that heart is steel-framed which, in spite of one’s chanting the holy name of the Lord with concentration, does not change when ecstasy takes place, tears fill the eyes, and the hairs stand on end” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.3.24).**

Due to my steel-framed heart, I am still full of *upādhi,* always looking for name, fame, and glory, always proud and making others suffer to defend my name, false prestige, and position. Thus I am losing the opportunity to be a good instrument to help you transform other Kali Men into pure devotees. Because of my proud, political, ambitious, envious mentality, I am missing the point and not helping you sufficiently in your mission.

In eighteen days Arjuna won the battle of Kurukṣetra. We can also change the world in eighteen days—on the strength of your instructions. You told us, “Just see: I am an old man, and I am alone; how much I have done. And you are all young men, and you are many; how much more you can do.” But due to our shortcomings we are struggling to keep your boat afloat.
