So, on this auspicious day of your appearance, I beg at your lotus feet: please make me humble, nonpolitical, nonambitious, and nonenvious so I can be a good instrument for continuing your mission. Please make me attached to the holy name. Please keep me at your door; continue to allow me to serve you even though I am so insignificant that I can never serve you properly.

Your unworthy servant,  
Somaka Swami

## Subhāga Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the shade of your lotus feet, which give cooling shelter to fallen souls such as myself who are victimized by the blazing fire of *saṁsāra*.

It is stated in the *Kāśī-khaṇḍa* of *Mahābhārata:*

*na cyavante ca yad bhaktā*  
*mahātmyaṁ pralayāpadi*  
*ato ‘cyute ‘khile loke sa*  
*ekaḥ sarvāvago ‘vyayam*

**“The devotees of the Lord never annihilate their individual existence even after the dissolution of the entire cosmic manifestation. The Lord and the devotees who associate with Him are always eternal, both in the material and spiritual worlds.”**

As Lord Kṛṣṇa eternally exists behind the veil of all things mundane, so do you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, exist eternally with Kṛṣṇa in His inconceivable glory. Those who penetrate that veil and attain Lord Kṛṣṇa by the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* also attain you, His pure devotee. Those who see the eternal Lord in His *arcā-vigraha* incarnation certainly see you with Him.

Those who are acquainted with the nectarean ocean of Kṛṣṇa’s unlimited divine qualities know that you eternally sport in that ocean. Those who relish Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental pastimes likewise relish your participation in them. Those who have actually developed a relationship with the Lord have a relationship with you.

Even if one is not advanced enough to penetrate the veil of matter, one can still perceive the transcendental influence of Your Divine Grace in this material world. In the last verse of *Bhagavad-gītā,* Sañjaya says that wherever opulence, victory, extraordinary power, and morality are in superexcellent abundance, there Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystics, and Arjuna, the supreme archer, must be displaying their pastimes. I find these same abundances in ISKCON today, a sure sign that your preaching pastimes with Kṛṣṇa as your helmsman (as per your dream on the *Jaladuta*) continue to unfold in the movement you established.

By the mercy of your ongoing ISKCON pastimes, fallen souls have the chance to engage in the five most potent devotional activities: association with devotees, service to the Deity, hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, and living in a holy place. Even if these are done only slightly or with little faith, as long as they are done without offense these activities are so wonderfully powerful that they propel anyone to the transcendental platform.

“My mercy is for everyone,” you once explained, “but you \[meaning your sincere followers\] are taking it.” Taking your mercy in your physical absence remains pretty much the same practical affair it was in the “good old days” of your ISKCON founder-*ācārya līlā:* Someone meets ISKCON devotees, renders a little service to the Lord (e.g., respects His *prasādam*), buys a book, chants Hare Kṛṣṇa, and finally moves into an ISKCON temple. And the result of such contact with your ISKCON pastimes is still the same:

*sādhu-śāstra-kṛpāya yadi kṛṣṇonmukha haya*  
*sei jīva nistare, māyā tāhāre chāḍaya*

**“If the conditioned soul becomes Kṛṣṇa conscious by the mercy of saintly persons who voluntarily preach scriptural injunctions and help him to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, the conditioned soul is liberated from the clutches of Māyā, who gives him up” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Mādhya*** **20.120).**

From the purport: “Saintly persons (*sādhus*), Vaiṣṇava devotees of the Lord, preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness on the basis of Vedic literature. It is only by their mercy that the conditioned soul is awakened to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When awakened, he is no longer eager to enjoy the materialistic way of life. Instead, he devotes himself to the loving transcendental service of the Lord. When one engages in the Lord’s devotional service, he becomes detached from material enjoyment.

*bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir*  
*anyatra caiṣa trika eka-kālaḥ*  
*(Bhāg. 11.2.42)*

**“This is the test by which one can tell whether he is advancing in devotional service. One must be detached from material enjoyment. Such detachment means that Māyā has actually given the conditioned soul liberation from illusory enjoyment.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am convinced more than ever that the release of the *jīvas* from the bondage of illusion depends upon your say so, for you are with Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of Māyā. When you request Kṛṣṇa to order Māyā to release a soul who becomes attached to your message as preached by your representatives, that soul’s material life comes to an end. And if you don’t, that unfortunate soul has no hope of liberation. This is confirmed by the Lord Himself:

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*ahaṁ bhakta-parādhīno*  
*hy asvatantra iva dvija*  
*sādhubhir grasta-hṛdayo*  
*bhaktair bhakta-jana-priyaḥ*

**“The Supreme Personality said to the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **\[Durvāsā\]: I am completely under the control of My devotees. Indeed, I am not at all independent. Because My devotees are completely devoid of material desires, I sit only in the core of their hearts. What to speak of My devotee, even those who are devotees of My devotee are very dear to Me” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **9.4.63).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever faults are visible in me are due to one mistake: my forgetting your continued mercy upon my head. You once said, “As soon as you think, ‘I’ll be able to do it \[get released from *māyā*\],’ you are a rascal. You cannot get released; therefore Kṛṣṇa says, ‘I’ll do it.’ ‘Practical’ means it will be done by Kṛṣṇa. Your only business is to surrender to Kṛṣṇa.” And because you are inseparable from Kṛṣṇa, my only business is to surrender to Kṛṣṇa by surrendering to you. With this Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I re-commit myself to fully surrendering at your ever-merciful lotus feet. Please consider me at you full disposal, and use me for whatever purpose you wish. I ask only that I may be always with you as a servant, an insignificant servant who receives your orders through a chain of command of billions, but your servant nonetheless.

Your servant,  
Subhāga Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble, prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. I am hoping to be allowed to offer you some small token of my appreciation on this, the occasion of your blessed appearance day.

First of all, thank you for teaching me this wonderful process of chanting the holy names of God, and for inspiring me to actually take up this chanting in a serious manner. Practically speaking, I had never taken anything seriously in my whole life, but somehow you induced me to take to this chanting with determination. And thus by your mercy I’m gradually getting a little taste for this sub-lime process. In Vṛndāvana, in September of 1974, you once stated, “This chanting is Lord Caitanya’s special gift. It gives us life. And if we don’t get life from this chanting, then simply sex life.”

This brings me to the second great benediction that you have bestowed on me—that by your mercy I have been able to avoid the poisons of illicit sex, intoxication, meat-eating, and gambling. Before I met you there wasn’t a day in my life when I wasn’t victimized by at least one of these vices. So this accomplishment is all your doing. As you once said when asked to show miracles, “This is my miracle—that my disciples have given up their sinful activities.” How can I ever repay you for being so instrumental in reforming my character?

I am personally very grateful for your sublime gift in the form of your transcendental instructions and knowledge. Your books, lectures, and conversations have supplied me with a wealth so vast that it overwhelms me. Thank you again for this wonderful gift.

Next I would like to thank you for providing me such a great instrument—your ISKCON Society—which affords me a chance to render some meaningful service to the cause of Lord Caitanya’s transcendental movement. I personally observed the great sacrifice you made to create this institution, and I can never properly repay you for that. ISKCON provides us not only our life-giving service but a vast network of spiritual association, consisting of your disciples, granddisciples, and aspiring granddisciples all over the world. How could we ever maintain our Kṛṣṇa consciousness without this Society?

But above and beyond all of these wonderful gifts, you have given me something of even greater value. You have given me someone to love, someone whom I could have full faith in, who I knew wasn’t capable of cheating me. Yes, that person who is so wonderful that even my cynical, skeptical, doubting mind couldn’t find fault with—you have given your very self. Yes, you gave yourself to your disciples, and by giving yourself you also included a whole *paramparā* of perfect persons, culminating with the Supreme perfect person, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. So how can I ever repay you for these gifts? I think it is not possible. I therefore beg to remain your indebted servant life after life.

Your servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

During *prasādam* one day, a devotee turned his head to ask me a question. “What has sustained you all these years?” he said.

I didn’t have to look far for the answer. It was right there—the magic thought that always kept me going when times got rough, the thought that pushed me forward every time I wanted to turn back and kept me fighting whenever my senses waged war on me. This thought is my weapon, and I always keep it close at hand, ready for the next emergency.

What is my magic thought? It is the memory of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s character.

First, consider Śrīla Prabhupāda’s intelligence. It goes beyond what any materialist could dream of. Who could have tricked Śrīla Prabhupāda into accepting something useless as the ultimate goal of life? Whenever Māyā-devī has me pinned to the floor and I am ready to give up, this thought saves me: Śrīla Prabhupāda is nobody’s fool, and he has chosen to be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It must be the best choice.

And there is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s honesty. He never cheats. He doesn’t need anything from anybody. None of Māyā’s creations can allure him. That’s freedom, isn’t it? Whenever I think I am weighted down, I can remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s impeccable dealings and realize that I have become Māyā’s beast of burden.

One day, a few devotees were looking at a photograph of Śrīla Prabhupāda sitting at the airport. “How can anybody be so relaxed and still be sitting up?” someone asked in amazement. Another reason to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda: Want freedom from tension? No need to spend money on stress therapy. Śrīla Prabhupāda knows how to do it.

The list could go on and on. And from whichever angle I look at Śrīla Prabhupāda, I come to the same conclusion: There is no danger in following him because every one of his qualities is desirable. There is no risk of acquiring something you do not want.

But what is the use of talking without practical advice? How can those who came later know the sublime character of Śrīla Prabhupāda?

There is a way. Look at the character of those who follow his instructions. Not that anyone will become a carbon copy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, of course. Individuality is an eternal fact. (That’s the first lesson in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.)

But we can look around and see the Vaiṣṇava qualities blossoming in those who strictly follow Śrīla Prabhupāda and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples. We can take a minute and stop talking about the fallen *gurus* and the social problems and enjoy the sweet company of the ISKCON Vaiṣṇavas. That sweetness is the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

“You can show your love for me by how much you cooperate with one another,” said Śrīla Prabhupāda. That’s the best Vyāsa-pūjā offering of all, isn’t it?

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Janmāṣṭamī, when our blessed Lord appeared within this mortal realm. All glories to Nandotsava, when Nanda Mahārāja announced the good tidings to his subjects.

It is truly appropriate that you would appear on that most blessed day when the appearance of our Lord was proclaimed throughout the land. Your mission is only to proclaim the glories of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, not only to the inhabitants of Vraja but to all the world as well.

Formerly, Kṛṣṇa blessed Gokula with the touch of His soft lotus feet. Five hundred years ago Śrī Kṛṣṇa again descended to deliver the world with the sound of His name. This appearance of the Lord is especially significant for the otherwise condemned souls of Kali-yuga.

Yet, who would have ever known the glories of the Golden Avatāra, the most unique, merciful, and rare incarnation of Godhead? Since He is the *channa-avatāra,* the incarnation whose *līlā* are most confidential, His glories are revealed through scripture in a concealed and secret manner. Only the wise devotees could ascertain the hidden treasure of all the scriptures. Until your appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, most people of the world remained unaware of their great fortune and divine inheritance. Now you have come to deliver this mercy to us, and for this blessing we praise you.

We are solely and forever indebted to you for the teachings of the Golden Avatāra. You are His agent, sent to deliver the fallen souls. This is the conclusion of *śāstra*. Now that you have trained us properly and directed our minds to the essence of the scriptures, we find that where the *līlā* of Caitanya Mahāprabhu are mentioned, His agent for delivering the fallen souls is also indicated.

It is described in the *śāstras* that a dear associate of the Lord would preach the message of Lord Caitanya in all the far and distant lands where the Lord Himself did not go; He delegated this task to His commander of devotees. This you have done, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as well as fulfilling the prediction and intense desire of Lord Caitanya: “In as many towns and villages as there are on the surface of the earth, the glories of My holy name shall be sung.” Lord Nityānanda predicted that one great devotee would inaugurate the building of a spiritual city in Māyāpur, where a grand temple would arise and propagate the *vāṇī* of Lord Caitanya as the basic universal and eternal religion of the world. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, the movement that will spread *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* worldwide. And as Bhakti-devī described, “In the future, I will leave this country and travel abroad.” This long awaited journey of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* was also performed through you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You carried bhakti with you on the *Jaladuta* and distributed *bhakti-śāstras* in all the lands that were once overcast with dark clouds of impersonalism, voidism, nihilism, and pessimism. Thus, you have brought light and life to our souls, which would otherwise have been lost forever.

All these predictions, as well as many more, explicitly point to you. You have made them all come true.

As Lord Caitanya is the most merciful incarnation, so you are the most merciful spiritual master, for you are most dear to Him. The essence of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava message is compassion on the fallen souls. Moved and inspired by that spirit, you have done what no one, not even Lord Caitanya Himself, has ever done before. You have proclaimed the glories of Śrī Kṛṣṇa in every town and village.

Lord Caitanya left indications of events yet to come, not during His manifest *līlā* but in the wake of His pastimes. He told of a time when millions of Jagāi’s and Mādhāi’s would be delivered just as the original ones were delivered by the mercy of Lord Nityānanda, the original *guru*, of a time when many rich men’s sons would forget their wives and wealth due to intense attraction to Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet, of a time when the greatest of intellects would abandon the egotism accompanying scholarship to be drawn by the irresistible current of the nectar of devotion, of a time when Kṛṣṇa consciousness would prevail during a Golden Age within the Iron Age, a Prema-yuga within the Kali-yuga.

These feats have been performed by and through you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and therefore we know that you are especially dear to and empowered by Lord Caitanya. Although it is most foolish for us to try to express or repay our unlimited debt for your kindness, it is nonetheless our duty to try.

And thus we remain ever hopeful and eager to serve in your mission, for in that way we will ever have your association. And in serving you lies our only hope of serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your humble servant,  
Varṣāṇa Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Again with Him You Can Be**

I’m not much of a poet,  
I think that you must know it.  
But for pleasing him I’ll try,  
And just hope that you don’t sigh.

So ‘scuse my poem and beg my pardon,  
I really want to please Janārdana  
And offer guru words of praise,  
Who helped us clear the māyā haze.

His holy birth was merciful,  
Making Calcutta beautiful.  
He crossed the sea to save us all  
And teach us of Gaurāṅga’s call.

Trust the one who does not cheat;  
I bow down at his lotus feet.  
His mercy’s all we have in life  
To keep us free from pain and strife.

He helps us keep on Kṛṣṇa’s ledge  
And keeps us on the razor’s edge.  
He smashed the cheats who claim they’re God  
And showed us they’re but dirt and sod.

His method was to chant the name—  
He taught that Kṛṣṇa was the same.  
He even was so kind, you know,  
He took us in to help us grow.

Prabhupāda is a lightning rod  
To shock us into accepting God.  
He was so strict and yet so kind,  
He knew how to attract the mind.

I want to go to follow him  
Who taught us how to nectar swim.  
But when I look I cannot see  
Where he went and where he’ll be.

He went away although we cry,  
But taught us that he’ll never die.  
“A Vaiṣṇava dies to live,” he said,  
“So never think that he is dead.”

That’s nice to say and nice to preach,  
And nice to hear the others teach,  
But still I feel the pain in me  
And wish with him again I’d be.

But then one day to have a look  
I opened up the Kṛṣṇa book.  
On every page I found him there,  
Showing what it means to care.

It’s nice to say and nice to teach,  
And nice to hear the others preach,  
But open his books and you will see  
How again with him you can be.

Vipramukhya Swami

## Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

On arriving in the Western lands,  
You saw how it would expand  
Through patience, determination, and prasādam  
Deftly dealt from your two lotus hands.

Your strong words dissipate all doubts,  
no contestants worthy to match your pace.  
Let them come, then hear and go,  
Will one but return to receive divine grace.

ISKCON soon exploded in force,  
Legally registered and officially named.  
New spiritual seekers begged to surrender,  
Your youthful force as yet untrained.

Your disciples, reliant, now huddle in fear,  
Your health having taken a sudden bad turn.  
They fervently pray in this hour,  
“Please, O Lord, let our master return.”

“You can see me there in my books—”  
A scientific truth the faithful perceive.  
“Please study them each carefully;  
Unlimited mercy you will duly receive.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, swan of the world,  
This stone heart now residing within,  
By the appearance of Your Divine Grace,  
Like ice by the sun, can be melted away.

All glories to this most auspicious day!

Your insignificant servants in Adelaide, Australia,  
(written by Kāliya Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

Granddisciples

## Ahmedabad

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to the Brāhma-Mādhva-Gauḍīya disciplic succession, which you so brightly illuminated by your auspicious appearance.

So many big, big personalities have appeared in last one hundred years and become very famous in their lifetimes. But no one will be remembered as much and as long as Your Divine Grace. When Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked Rāmānanda Rāya, “Who is the most famous person?” Rāmānanda Rāya said, “The devotee of the Lord is the most famous.”

Some of the most intelligent scholars of the world, such as Dr. Stillson Judah, Dr. Harvey Cox, and Dr. A. L. Basham, recognized your historical importance. They concluded that the depth of spiritual life you implanted in your disciples worldwide is matched only by very powerful religious teachers of the world like Jesus Christ, Lord Buddha, Śaṅkarācārya, Madhvācārya, Rāmānujā-cārya, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself.

Your activities have proved that you appeared in this horrible materialistic world by the superior arrangement of merciful Lord Kṛṣṇa. None of us wretched materialistic men of the Kali-yuga qualify for such exalted mercy. But it is not extraordinary for you to appear amongst us:

*mahānta svabhāva ei tārite pāmara*  
*nija kārya nahi tabu yāna tāra ghara*

**“It is the general practice of all saintly people to deliver the fallen. Therefore they go to the people’s houses, although they have no personal business there.”**

In this way you appeared in our *gṛhavrata* hearts.

Seeing how many intelligent, aristocratic, powerful people of this world are totally bereft of even a slight glimpse of spiritual knowledge, we cannot estimate our good fortune in receiving the highest spiritual knowledge from the unbroken chain of disciplic succession through you, a transparent via medium. Here we were, the most wretched sinful Kali-yuga worms in stool, and all of a sudden by your golden touch we became bright, brilliant Vaikuṇṭha men. Like the leper *brāhmaṇa* Vāsudeva from South India, whose leprosy disappeared by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s transcendental embrace, our ignorance—accumulated through many millions of births and far worse than Vāsudeva’s leprosy—has disappeared by your golden touch. Indeed, the leper Vāsudeva was physically sick but enlightened internally. He even put the fallen worms back into the sores in his skin! We, on the other hand, we were totally ignorant fools. No saintly person would touch us. Even your Godbrothers wouldn’t touch us. But you mercifully came forward, accepted us, instructed us, engaged us in Kṛṣṇa’s causeless service, allowed us to touch you and serve you—a Vaikuṇṭha man. What good fortune! We simply pray like the leper Vāsudeva and the *brāhmaṇa* Sudāmā:

*kvāhaṁ daridraḥ pāpīyān*  
*kva kṛṣṇa śrī-niketanaḥ*  
*brahma-bandhur iti smāhaṁ*  
*bāhubhyāṁ parirambhitaḥ*

**He said, “Who am I? A sinful, poor friend of a** ***brāhmaṇa*****? And who is Kṛṣṇa? The Supreme Personality of Godhead, full in six opulences. Nonetheless, He has embraced me with His two arms.” (SB 10.81.16)**

Of course, you are not Kṛṣṇa. But you are a manifestation of His mercy. Intelligent men know that Kṛṣṇa belongs to the Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura. Therefore the best course is to follow in the footsteps of a Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said an *uttama-adhikārī* is known by his ability to convert other people to the Vaiṣṇava fold. Your glories are obvious to any unenvious person.

Lord Caitanya is the Supreme Lord, so it is not a big surprise that He converted Jagāi and Mādhāi. He made the animals chant and dance in ecstasy. That is also not a very big surprise, because the Lord can do anything and everything. But when He empowers His devotees to perform such wonderful activities, that is a great display of His causeless mercy. That is the opulence of the Lord. Indeed, the Lord is wonderful, and so are His pure devotees.

By your mercy we have known Lord Kṛṣṇa from your books. Similarly, by the grace of the self-same Supreme Lord, we are able to understand your glories. If anyone can understand your unparalleled position, he is certainly blessed by Lord Caitanya.

Even though we see the goal and the path clearly, our ability to walk on this path is meager. On this auspicious day we beg you to give us the strength to progress on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In return, we humbly pledge ourselves to serve your mission with whatever capacity we have. Thank you millions of time for appearing in this world from your eternal pastimes in Goloka Vṛndāvana. Thank you again for giving us a glimpse of transcendental life. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants in Ahmedabad, India,

## Alachua

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances millions of times at your lotus feet.

All of the devotees in the New Ramaṇ-reti community sincerely desire to render some assistance to you in your preaching mission. We would like to take the opportunity on this most auspicious day to offer our miniscule efforts, like the spider in the *Rāmāyaṇa,* for your pleasure.

Since your last visit in 1971, Their Lorships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi have been relocated from Gainesville proper to a much larger and potentially much nicer preaching facility just twenty minutes away. They now preside over 127 beautiful acres that, with your blessing, will become a major hub for distributing Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

We dedicate ourselves with the express desire to continue your mission, especially in the matter of your remaining wish for development of *varṇāśrama* communities.

We are regularly observing the daily programs of *maṅgala ārati, tulasī-pūjā,* chanting sixteen rounds, attending *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and following the four regulative principles. We are also reinstating your evening program in order to arrange our day like a transcendental “sandwich.”

We are “Saving the Cow,” bulls, and oxen and raising our own flowers for Their Lordships. This season the gardens are full of fresh fruit, vegetables, and herbs. These include tomatoes, potatoes, zucchini, yellow squash, carrots, *karelā, laukī, brinjal, dhaniya,* blueberries, and watermelons (one of your favorite fruits). We will also soon be able to grow grains such as wheat and corn, as well as other crops such as sugarcane.

As all our endeavors, when successful, are the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa due to the fact that you have accepted our service, we pray to always be able to continue on in this way so that your mission can be fulfilled.

Sincerely, and with all the humility we can find,

*Your disciples and granddisciples at New Ramaṇ-reti Dhāma, Alachua, Florida,*

## Almviks Gard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace’s transcendental appearance, which is like the dawning of the Satya-yuga. In the midst of our hopeless, confused frustration, you cleared the path of life to save us from death, or worse yet, a so-called life in Kali-yuga.

The message of the *paramparā,* which you brought to the West, came in the nick of time, just before the whirlpool of material existence sucked us down to the abyss of darkness.

Your spiritual master ordered you long ago, in 1922, to prepare, and in 1944 you started to publish *Back to Godhead* (which this year, incidentally, took again a new life). In Delhi in the early sixties, in the hustle and bustle of Chippiwada, you prepared at great personal sacrifice your life’s great opus, the Ś*rīmad-Bhāgavatam*. How could you know that halfway around the world an entire generation was crying out for Your Divine Grace in dire desperation?

Now, someone may criticize that we are flattering you because we are your disciples and granddisciples. But no, among the many offerings in the twenty-fourth annual Vyāsa-pūjā book, this humble attempt at homage is just one insignificant entry from one small corner of the world. Your glories are heard, sung, and accepted by all purified men who are thoroughly honest. That was already predicted long ago by Your Divine Grace’s taskmaster, Śrīla Vyāsadeva himself: śṛṇ*vanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*.

It is well known that a parrot can repeat without change, but it is a little-known fact that when a tree-ripened fruit is partially cut open by the beaks of such birds, the sweet flavor is enhanced. Analogously, it is clear that you never changed the meaning of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* but rather made it sweeter with your purports. The purports written before you came to the West in the early sixties in so-called obscurity and those written in the late seventies after you had become the recognized world *ācārya* are exactly consistent. This proves that the fruit of knowledge you handed down has remained, from beginning to end, natural and unspoiled by the artificial additive of false ego.

But there is another reason why your purports—your “emotional ecstasies”—bring you such great credit, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In them you presented the science of *rasa* from the fountainhead of all *rasas* (*raso vai saḥ*) in such a perfect manner that they touch the hearts of even condemned persons like scientists and māyāvādīs, what to speak of the pious and innocent. They will continue to do so for the next ten thousand years—*śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam*. The words *amṛta drava* perfectly describe your purports: “They are like fruits that are nectarean, being semisolid and soft and therefore easily swallowable.”

In April 1977, shortly before the closure of Your Divine Grace’s manifested pastimes in this world, we had the immense good fortune of witnessing you from a couple of meters’ distance as you sat onstage one evening at a Bombay *pāṇḍal* program. Although you were practically like Bhīṣma on the bed of arrows of physical frailty and old age, you were ready to again take up from scratch the battle against the atheists, or even the demoniac Bombay municipal government, which had once before been taught a sound lesson from Kṛṣṇa’s undaunted hero devotee, the *senā-pati bhakta* of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Gaurāṅga, Śrī Mahāprabhu Caitanya.

Thank you, our dear lord and master, for establishing this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and for your saving us. On the present occasion of your ninety-fifth Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, our mission is to maintain what you created and to vigorously preach, since the suffering of humanity is more poignant than ever. Since we have become fortunate, why not the whole world?

Please help us recognize your followers who are pure devotees, advanced in undeviated service, and to faithfully serve them (ś*uśrūṣayā bhajana-vijñam ananyam anyā-nindādi-śūnya-hṛdam īpsita-saṅga-labdhyā)*.

Please let us offer our obeisances to the initiated devotee who faithfully worships the Deity *(dīkṣāsti cet praṇatibhiś ca bhajantam īśam),* and to mentally honor the devotee who chants the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa *(kṛṣṇeti yasya giri taṁ manasādriyeta)*.

As far as the envious are concerned, please save us from them by allowing us to neglect them entirely and thus avoid getting entangled with them at all.

Please guide us to actually serve you tangibly, not simply ritualistically. For example, when no one will buy your books, please strengthen our determination to present them as best we can.

We know that the uncontrolled mind is our worst enemy. So please allow us to ruthlessly slash all doubts by exposing them to *śāstra, sādhu,* and especially Your Divine Grace.

In conclusion, we, the devotees of New Chaitanya Chandrodaya Mandir, take this most auspicious opportunity of your annual Vyāsa-pūjā celebration to bow our heads to the floor in offering you, our lord and master, our humble obeisances.

Your servants at Almviks Gārd, Sweden,

Yogīndra dāsa / Haraka dāsa / Locana dāsa Ṭhākura dāsa / Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa dāsa / Dhruva dāsa / Ekanātha dāsa / Hariścandra dāsa / Janeśvara dāsa / Lava dāsa / Mārkaṇḍeya Ṛṣi dāsa / Parāśara Muni dāsa / Rāsa Maṇḍala dāsa / Rudracandra dāsa / Supaina dāsa / Tapan Miśra dāsa / Taṭastha Śakti dāsa / Vrajendranandana dāsa / Bhakta Tom / Bhakta Prahlāda / Haripūjā-devī dāsī / Nandinī-devī dāsī / Ambodā-devī dāsī / Annapūrṇā-devī dāsī / Apūrvadā-devī dāsī / Dhaniṣṭhā-devī dāsī / Ekāṅgā-devī dāsī / Harikāntā-devī dāsī / Kalyā-devī dāsī / Kamalasundarī-devī dāsī / Kiśorī-devī dāsī / Kṛṣṇa-bhakti-devī dāsī / Kuntī-devī dāsī / Līlā-devī dāsī / Mukhyapradā-devī dāsī / Mūrtī-devī dāsī / Nareśvarī-devī dāsī / Puṣkariṇī-devī dāsī / Rasajñā-devī dāsī / Sāvitrī-devī dāsī / Śrīvatsa-devī dāsī / Tārakā-devī dāsī / Viśākhā-devī dāsī / Yaśomatī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Anna

## Atlanta

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarsavatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Although we’re not so proud as to think that we perform such wonderful feats or that we speak so eloquently, we understand that the progress we have made thus far is only by your mercy, which is certainly a miracle considering the condition we were in before meeting you, for we were certainly dumb and lame.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we see how you and Lord Caitanya are maintaining this movement. The present ISKCON—and all it will become—is simply the blossoming of the flower of your pure love of Kṛṣṇa. We can also see that ISKCON is the movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that will certainly save the fallen souls of Kali-yuga. Every deviating fraction, individually and collectively, falls to the Lord’s deluding potency. Only pure ISKCON finally remains. The Lord makes this quite obvious. Of course, there will always be problems. Actually, the fall to the illusory energy takes place prior to the predicament’s finally being observed by others, though the actual fact of the situation is not understood by those involved. This is the mystery of *māyā.*

The fallen souls of this age can be saved only by the Lord’s pure movement (anything less will not do), and we can see that the Lord will not tolerate deviations and nonsense in His movement. This fact is quite reassuring and gives us real security and confidence. We thank you and the Lord for keeping ISKCON pure, despite our shortcomings. We also see that when we follow your instructions carefully, not changing them to suit our *māyā,* keeping ourselves pure, honest, and simple, we achieve some level of success in your mission, and certainly in our devotional lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what great opportunity there is in your movement! What privilege and benevolence you have bestowed upon us! The taste of service is so sweet and relishable! And to imagine what awaits us in the future!

*bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir*  
*anyatra caiṣa trika eka-kālaḥ*  
*papadyamānasya yathāśnataḥ syus*  
*tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭiḥ kṣud-apāyo ‘nu-ghāsam*

**“Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things—these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in the same way that pleasure, nourishment, and relief from hunger come simultaneously and increasingly, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.2.42).**

*itthaṁ satāṁ brahma-sukhānubhūtyā*  
*dāsyaṁ gatānāṁ para-daivatena*  
*māyāśritānāṁ nara-dārakeṇa*  
*sākaṁ vijahruḥ kṛta-puṇya-puñjāḥ*

**“In this way, all the cowherd boys used to play with Kṛṣṇa, who is the source of the Brahman effulgence for** ***jñānīs*** **desiring to merge into that effulgence, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead for devotees who have accepted eternal servitorship, and who for ordinary persons is but another ordinary child. The cowherd boys, having accumulated the results of pious activities for many lives, were able to associate in this way with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. How can one explain their great fortune?”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.12.11)**

Here in Atlanta, New Śrī Pānihāṭi Dhāma, we have, by your mercy, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, who elicited from your divine eyes divine tears of love of God when you personally visited us here in 1975; Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Their sister Subhadrā-devī, who beam brightly from Their altar and shed their radiance profusely upon the conditioned souls when They go out for Their Rathya-yātrā every year; and beautiful Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, who help us establish our relationship with the Lord and keep us attracted to Him rather than to *māyā*. And the Deities are being served very nicely.

Also here in Atlanta we have the most wonderful *prasādam,* now being served through the Hare Kṛṣṇa Dinner Club to all types of receptive people, who are being recruited to engage in the Lord’s service in various ways; the Hare Kṛṣṇa Books and Boutique to distribute your books and supply the growing congregation with devotional paraphernalia, transforming their homes into temples; and the most amazing festivals and *kīrtanas* throughout the year. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have brought the festive atmosphere of Vaikuṇṭha here.

Your congregation here is growing tremendously. Many more people are being attracted. The Sunday Feast is attracting 150 to 200 or more people weekly, and the devotees go out distributing your books and the Lord’s *prasādam,* His message and holy name through various preaching programs throughout the year, all by your mercy. Improvements are constantly being made.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are amazed at how things go on nicely even though we fall so very short of the mark of pure devotion and full surrender. Imagine what could be done if we were all to become really serious and sincere about pleasing you in every way.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, hearing your glories and the glories of your devotees is so inspiring that all the cobwebs of *māyā* and pent-up frustrations and anxieties of petty, selfish desire and reservations against serving you in various ways vanish, and a flood of inspiration comes rushing into the heart and takes us along into the activities of spreading your divine uplifting message.

We pray that our service to you and Kṛṣṇa will become an expression of love, born of realization and purification rather than just a habit or daily ritual.

We understand that a devotee who serves the Lord without any secondary interests wins the Lord’s favor and gets what he intensely desires and strives for. So may we intensely desire and endeavor to please you in every way by working together cooperatively and lovingly, putting aside all secondary interests for your sake and Kṛṣṇa’s sake.

*durgame pathi me ‘ndhasya*  
*skhalat-pāda-gater muhuḥ*  
*sva-kṛpā-yaṣṭi-dānena*  
*santaḥ santv avalambanam*

**“My path is very difficult. I am blind, and my feet are slipping again and again. Therefore, may the saints help me by granting me the stick of their mercy as my support” (Cc** ***Antya*** **1.1).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you sacrificed everything, your entire life, for Kṛṣṇa! You tolerated every situation to please your spiritual master to save us from hell and suffering!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the world!

Your fallen but hopeful servants at New Pānihāṭi Dhāma, Atlanta, Georgia,  
(written by Nirguṇa dāsa)

## Auckland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Foolish and demoniac leaders have created an intolerable situation for us all. Murder, rape, theft, exploitation, torture, poverty, and madness have become commonplace. Death simply ushers in a new round of suffering in another body.

How auspicious are your lotus feet? Your lotus feet offer sanctuary to all refugees fleeing the struggle for existence in the material world.

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-*  
*vāditra-mādyan manaso rasena*  
*romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Leaving behind the seedy nightclubs and dance halls packed with prostitutes and brain-dead drunkards, we now dance the swami step with pure-hearted devotees amidst fragrant scents from burning incense. Intoxicated with love of Godhead, we no longer serve a greedy boss, but instead dedicate our lives at your lotus feet and thus expand the limits of the nectarean ocean of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement throughout New Zealand.

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāṁś ca niyuñjato ‘pi*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your strong preaching you exposed our worshipable heroes as merely humanlike dogs and hogs. Now the bubble has burst and the rock stars and movie idols are seen for what they really are: simply dogs that bark louder than others. You also revealed the blank, post-dated promises of our leaders and politicians, who are just blind rogues. Now, by your strong will, our worshipable Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī, Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā, as well as Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, have manifested Their divine presence on our New Varshana temple altars.

Like humming bees around a lotus flower, the devotees, united in purpose, engage blissfully in unlimited varieties of service to Their Lordships. As if by a mystical metamorphic process, those who were once dirty in body, mind, and deeds are becoming beautiful and respectable ladies and gentlemen through these services you taught to us.

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Leaving behind untouchable foods that pollute one’s existence and bring disease and death, we now lick, chew, suck, and drink with enthusiastic relish the remnants of food offered to the Deities through the via medium of your grace. This staple diet is the inoculation you have given to us to make us strong in the fight with *māyā.* For you are everything to us—our loving father, guiding teacher, healing physician, and true friend.

*śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-*  
*mādhurya-līlā-guṇa-rūpa-nāmnām*  
*prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Hearing and chanting the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His devotees is the medicine to cure our material disease. This medicine has now become candy-coated, having emanated from your lotus lips, and so we are eager for the medicine, which may otherwise appear bitter for us.

Now the trash literatures that flood the world with ignorance are no longer attractive to us. We know those literatures to be only fit for crowlike persons. Instead we swarm like swans to a lake of pure water that is decorated with lotus flowers, and we relish the transcendental literatures you worked so hard to leave with us. They are like windows to the spiritual world, where you now reside once again with Kṛṣṇa and His associates.

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can understand that as the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, who were all confidential servants of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, descended to this world from Vṛndāvana *dhāma* to teach us the loving sentiments of the residents of Vṛndāvana, so Your Divine Grace, being a confidential servant of the six Gosvāmīs, have continued that same mission to expand the pleasure pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may take birth as a blade of grass or a speck of dust to be trampled by your lotus feet in Kṛṣṇa’s abode.

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glory is unlimited, for you hold the spiritual world in your hand and you can distribute it as you please. We fall at your feet with great awe and reverence, for the Lordships we worship on the altar embrace you in Their hearts and offer prayers of love and glorification to you. Pleasing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the only means to please Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Every step we take and every inch we move on our trek back to Godhead is simply another manifestation of your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

From the devotees at New Varshana, who are floating and diving into the ocean of your mercy, we thank you.

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Your servants at the New Varshana farm community, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Gaṅga dāsa)

## Augusta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this ninety-fifth anniversary of your appearance day, all of us at the Augusta Nāma Hatta consider ourselves fortunate to have been given the chance to offer a few words in glorification of Your Divine Grace. We are grateful to Lord Kṛṣṇa for sending His pure devotee to the Western countries and starting a society of devotees, ISKCON, which is engaging us in helping to spread Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have mercifully provided us with the association of your wonderful, advanced disciples like Jayapatāka Mahārāja, Romapāda Mahārāja, and Balabhadra Bhaṭṭācārya Prabhu, who have kindly been coming to Augusta regularly to inspire us. Under their guidance and by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s teachings in your books, our Thursday feast program has been going on for over two and a half years now. Although we started out with a meager attendance (sometimes only four guests), nowadays on some occasions we have had sixty or seventy guests. Many families are consistently attending and have made it a regular part of their lives.

We have also been blessed with many preaching opportunities, such as the *prasādam* booth at the Earth Day Fair and the Arts in the Heart of Augusta Festival, and the *kīrtana* party in the Arts of Augusta parade. By Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy, we were also able to play *bhajanas* onstage at the Augusta Mall, and the Interfaith Council of Augusta has asked us to become members and to share “the Hare Kṛṣṇa philosophy.” Now we are in the process of starting a *bhakti-yoga* club at Augusta College with the help of some college students who come to the Thursday feast programs. There has also been a *prasādam* distribution program at the medical college.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are actually seeing that you have given us a house in which the whole world can live. The Nāma Haṭṭa programs here are regularly attended by both Indians and Americans, and even one Chinese girl has been attending and chanting with us. At the last Earth Day Fair, several young high school kids with their own drums played along with the *kīrtana,* and some also chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa. By your mercy, we hope they can also attend the feast programs.

However, we can see that an unlimited amount of work needs to be done in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness here. We are just praying to Lord Kṛṣṇa that you will be pleased with us for whatever little we have done so far, as Lord Rāma was pleased with the spider kicking dust in the Indian Ocean. You have said that purity is the force, and we fall down in the dust of your lotus feet and beg for purification so that we may become instruments in assisting your mission of spreading the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Your servants at the Augusta, Georgia, Nāma Hatta,

Bakula dāsa / Rāma-rāghava dāsa / Kṛṣṇa-smaraṇa dāsa / Bhakta Damen/ Mike Dholakia / Ramesh Shah / Neel Shah / Saidutt Dholakia / Prakash Bhende / Kṛśāṅgī-devī dāsī / Anaṅgalatā-devī dāsī / Rasāmṛta-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Debbie / Palavi Dholakia / Manju Shah / Palma Dholakia / Mayuri Shah / Rajashree Shah / Hema Patel / Smita Patel / Allison Hurst / Lillian Wan

Children

Devin/ Nitāi-Gaura / Dhruva

## Bali

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the world and the envoy of Lord Kṛṣṇa!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are an exemplary disciple of His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. It is you who have materialized the hope and desire of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. It is you who have made the prophecy of Lord Caitanya true. Now, in every town and village, there is heard the singing Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. Nothing can check the flood of *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* which is like the Gaṅgā pouring down on the earth from the spiritual world.

O truthful spiritual master, how can we measure your boundless compassion upon all living creatures? You save us from hellish fire, you prevent us from gliding down into the abyss of darkness, and you cleanse this mortal world of the reactions of sinful activities.

O high-souled savior, you are the genuine representative of Śrī Vyāsa. You are an exemplary *paramahaṁsa*, spreading the Lord’s holy names all over the three worlds. You taught us that the instructions of the bona fide spiritual master are the true life’s guide.

O exalted spiritual master, please be merciful upon us. Guide us so that we can go on with our service to your lotus feet. The dust of your lotus feet is like a ship that we board for crossing the ocean of repeated birth and death. Let everybody race to board it for their eternal benefit!

All glories to you, the lofty personality who is the envoy of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and who is very desirous of delivering everyone from ignorance and taking them back to Goloka.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you sow *bhakti* seeds in the hearts of all living entities. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glory is eternally chanted. Your greatness is known all over the three worlds. And from all points of the compass are heard the calls: “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Your low servants in Bali, Indonesia,

## Barcelona

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day, our remembrance of your great personality increases more and more, along with our desire to glorify Your Divine Grace. But we are so insignificant and contaminated that we cannot do it properly. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the sun that is illuminating ISKCON. You are the sun that illuminates our lives.

**Prayer for a Sacred Place**

A place where we can preach your teachings, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we are lacking the spiritual force to do it properly.

A place where we can worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we don’t have enough devotion.

A place where the devotees can be together, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we don’t appreciate and take proper care of the temple and things related to the temple.

A place in our hearts where the pure love begins to bloom, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes the blazing fire of lust burns us and it’s so difficult to overcome.

A place in the city where we can go on chanting the holy name, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we don’t have attachment for the name due to our minds’ reluctance to chant.

A place where we can get the association of devotees, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes our pride and envy disturb and hurt others.

A place where we can honor and distribute *prasādam,* Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we don’t appreciate *prasādam’s* tremendous spiritual value.

Any place in this world where we can render devotional service, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although sometimes we avoid services that we see as difficult and complicated.

A place at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, although we are so fallen that we don’t deserve it.

A place in your heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that’s what we are longing for, because you are our shelter, our guide, our life, our vital force, and our everything, although we are so weak and conditioned.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us advance spiritually so that in future Vyāsa-pūjā books we will not mention anymore this undesirable “although,” and in this way we will be able to properly serve your exalted personality.

We don’t want spiritual advancement just for our own satisfaction, but to be able to render you the best service.

The Western countries, and also ourselves, were without life before you came. You are the soul who is giving us life and who doesn’t allow us to die in the anxiety of this world.

What a great fortune for those devotees who could see you, touch you, hear you! What a great fortune for those devotees who, although they couldn’t be present during those wonderful years when you were giving classes and arriving at the temples and everyone was greeting you with *kīrtana* and flowers, can now see you in videos and can hear your tapes.

But we know well that the best of all fortunes is to follow without deviation all your instructions. And what a great fortune we have, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that year after year we can be here together celebrating your Vyāsa-pūjā. And what a great fortune it would be to bring back to the temple those who have left devotional service so that more and more persons would assemble together to sing, *Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, patita-pāvana!*

Praying fervently for your mercy, your servants in Barcelona, Spain,  
(written by Sādhya-śreṣṭha dasa)

Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda

Sādhya-śreṣṭha dāsa

## Belfast

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you, the selfless servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You satisfied the desires of all the previous *ācāryas* by single-handedly fulfilling the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. All alone, you brought the message of *bhāgavata-dharma* and the congregational chanting of the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa to the four corners of the world. You never entertained any thought for your own safety or comfort. Instead you took full shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord and accomplished a miracle.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, every day, all around the world, an ever-growing number of people are witness to that miracle. And for many, that miracle comes in the form of your books—hundreds of millions of them distributed all over the planet by your dedicated disciples, granddisciples, and followers everywhere. By their presence alone your books are purifying the atmosphere of the Kali-yuga, what to speak of the effect they have on those who read them. And so many people *are* reading your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda. One of our book distributors was told on the street that a Presbyterian minister from a local church sometimes reads from your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* during his Sunday sermon. And why not? Nowhere else can anyone find such a scientific presentation of the Absolute Truth as is revealed in your translations and purports.

For others that miracle comes in the form of *harināma-saṅkīrtana.* With over three hundred ISKCON centers around the world, the holy names of the Lord are being constantly vibrated, twenty-four hours a day, in people’s homes, in your temples, and on the street. Even on the radio and television people are hearing the *mahā-mantra* sung by devotees in a popular song about the names of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Over the past two or three years, more then ever before, we notice the young children who seem spontaneously attracted to the *kīrtana* party. Their wide eyes and smiling faces reveal their sincere appreciation of the holy names. Sometimes they stand rooted to the spot and cry when their parents attempt to pull them away. Sometimes they clap their hands and try to dance in step. At other times they just jump up and down in enthusiasm, barely able to contain themselves. These children are the devotees of tomorrow, and their presence more than compensates for the lack of sunshine on the windy streets of this city.

Another common feature on the streets of Belfast are the born-again Christians. Brandishing their Bibles, straightfaced and unappreciative of anything but their own brand of Christianity, they are generally cold towards the devotees. To have a philosophical discussion with them is an almost certain waste of time. But on a recent *harināma* party, when devotees had come from all over Ireland for the monthly national *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* and there was a bigger than usual chanting party, one man with his sandwich board and banner just couldn’t take his eyes off the *kīrtana.* He simply wandered up and down in front of the *kīrtana,* smiling from ear to ear. What more attractive way to broadcast the glories of the Supreme Lord than to chant His holy names on the street, attracting the hearts and minds of young and old, atheist and religionist, office worker and down-and-outer alike?

Compared with the book distribution figures and the *kīrtana* pastimes of other temples, our efforts in Belfast may appear insignificant, but small incidents like the ones we mention here help remind us of the mercy you shower upon us. Your miracles are all around us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we but have the eyes to see. It is a miracle that you were able to accomplish what you did in the time you had, and an even greater miracle that your movement continues to grow and will grow for thousands of years to come. But the greatest miracle of all is that you were able to save the likes of us.

One famous English historian, Sir Arnold Toynbee, who met you once at Bhaktivedanta Manor, wrote that the last quarter of the twentieth century would not be noted by future generations for its technological advances but rather as that time when the philosophy of the East first made a significant impression on the ways of the West. On several occasions you yourself have said the same thing: “This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will bring about an historical renaissance,” you said in December 1976. “Historians will note this period, how the world was changed.…”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us to participate in the making of history.

Thank you for taking the time and trouble to engage us in your service.

Thank you for continuing to show us your fathomless mercy.

All glories! All glories! All glories unto you,  
The savior of the whole world,  
The most merciful ambassador of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu,  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

Your servants at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava in Belfast, Northern Ireland,

## Belgium

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this day made holy by your divine appearance on this Kali-yuga, misery-ridden planet. As the rising sun dissipates darkness and bestows new life and energy to all conditioned souls, so your glorious appearance fills the heart of any soul willing to receive you with great hope and longing for the real eternal shelter of your Lord’s abode.

Now the time of your absence from our earthly vision is numbering more in years than your physical presence here after you founded ISKCON. But those who have the sincerity and good fortune to cling to your divine instructions are ever-increasingly relishing the nectar of your association despite the inevitable intrusions and assaults brought about by this relentless Age of Kali. As inadequate and impure as one may be, you are ever more merciful and reassuring. When we stumble and come face to face with Māyā’s treacherous tests, by turning to you once again we are purified and rejuvenated, ever more determined to act as your faithful disciples, shaded by the cooling shelter of your instructions and the merciful, forgiving association of those who hold you in their hearts as their life and soul.

Those whose vision is like yours have great faith in the prediction of the Lord, the previous *ācāryas,* and your divine self that transcendental love for Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, developed through the chanting of His holy name will be effectively spread all over the world despite all impediments. We pray to be used as instruments in this, your mission. In this way we can simultaneously transcend all inebriety and extend your mercy to all those who are suffering, as we were before we were blessed by your association.

Kindly forgive our offenses and foolish mistakes and consider us to be surrendered souls unto you, despite our low birth and bad character. What do we have to offer you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, except our gratitude and sincere desire to remain as the eternal servants of your servants? By your mystical devotional prowess, which has captivated even Lord Kṛṣṇa, certainly you can take this desire and mold us to become Vaiṣṇavas who can thus reciprocate with your unlimited compassion by distributing it to all others who are in dire need of it.

Your servants in Rādhādeśa (Belgium).

## Beograd

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When His Holiness Harikeśa Swami told you that in Yugoslavia people were chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, you were very satisfied. When he presented you with a new *Īśopaniṣad,* you blessed him to produce and distribute more and more books in Yugoslavia.

Thank you for sending him to save us and engage us very nicely in your *saṅkīrtana* mission.

Thank you for giving us these wonderful books, which are traveling preachers. They make us Kṛṣṇa conscious when we read them and distribute them, and they make people Kṛṣṇa conscious when they take them and read them.

We have learned that you become pleased by each book produced and distributed and by each devotee made. Therefore please bless us more and more so that we can flood this mad world with your books and thus bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere, as much as possible. In this way we can do the best for ourselves and for everybody else.

Your books are unique in the history of mankind because Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, jumps from each and every page of your books. That is the exquisite quality of your books—that Kṛṣṇa is so thickly all-pervading within them.

Your fallen servants in Beograd, Yugoslavia,

## Bergamo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once my temple president asked you how it was that we were so fortunate to be devotees, when normally it requires great volumes of previous pious activities, which it certainly seemed we hadn’t performed. You said that you had created our good fortune. This is clear: that you have created the greatest fortune that exists and offered it to anyone. My only problem is how much I am willing to accept this fortune. I am so fortunate that you gave me very clear personal instructions two or three times, and these instructions are the whole goal of my life. This is a proof of your greatness: that in just a few words, a few moments, you can and did make the lives of so many persons fortunate, filled with meaning and direction.

Your example of perfection and pure devotion is something we will go on appreciating forever, because our relationship is forever—as long as we are not so foolish as to intentionally break it. The more I read your purports and try to understand the position of a pure devotee, that position seems ever so far away from me. I just pray that you give me the strength to continue trying to carry out your orders with ever more faith and enthusiasm. Your countless descriptions of pure devotion are not just intellectual explanations but states you have fully realized and attained. In recently studying your conversations, I can see this exemplified continually. You tirelessly speak about Kṛṣṇa without deviation, defeating the atheist scientists and bogus philosophers and *yogīs*. The amazing thing is that sometimes I find full discussions on the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness one after another, all with the same date. This means all day long and into the night you were glorifying Kṛṣṇa, then resting one or two hours, and then continuing in your Bhaktivedanta purports in the early morning hours.

Your unceasing devotional service is the example for us to aspire for, but at least for me it seems ever further away as I read more and learn what that goal is. I pray again that you please give me the strength to keep on trying and never give up. The only meaning in my life is to try someday to live up to your expectations, given in those few words that you so kindly spoke to me. In all the world only your words wash away the clouds of illusion and explain, in the most crystal-clear way, things as they are, reality without any doubts.

You said that the most difficult thing was to run a spiritual movement here in the material world, but you are so expert that you did that and are still doing it while simultaneously preaching and writing volumes of books. Some complain that things have decreased since your physical presence, but this is by no means true. When I look at a BTG published at the time you so mercifully picked me up out of the gutter of the material world, there were about ten to fifteen centers. Then in 1977 there were more than one hundred, and now there are more than three hundred. This movement will go on increasing by your insurmountable desire to fulfill the order of your Guru Mahārāja and our strict adherence to your instructions.

It is a source of great hope and faith to see so many Godbrothers following your orders with thousands of times greater sincerity and determination and how they have become such advanced devotees capable of carrying your message to all corners of the world. I just hope and pray to you for the strength to go on trying to fulfill your orders, inspired by the example of so many exalted Godbrothers.

Faith comes by association with devotees. So the more I get their association, the more I have faith and determination to go on trying because I see that the process works. It’s just that for some like me it is slow due to lack of intelligence, and I just hope that in some lifetime I become useful to your mission to save all the conditioned souls. I was too fallen to have your personal association for more than a few moments in this lifetime. I aspire, in some future lifetime, to have your personal association. This is the only real goal in life—to some day have your personal association and become useful in carrying out your mission.

Your mercy on me and all of us is unlimited and manifests itself in countless ways. I must say, though, that for me the greatest mercy is to be allowed to go out amongst the conditioned souls and deliver you personally to them in the form of your transcendental books. This one activity is so special and powerful that it brings the greatest fortune to them and is the only time that my mind and senses are completely peaceful and satisfied. This one activity—distributing your books—is the cause of good fortune for everyone. I just pray again for your mercy to have the strength to execute this service more and more. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for tolerating all my offenses and nonsense, and please never kick me away. Just let me keep trying to please you, even if it may take many lifetimes.

Your faithful servants of the Bergamo Yatra, including Milano, Torino, Brescia, Varese, and Milano West.

At the lotus feet of our spiritual master,

May we one day render real service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
It has not become easier with each occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā to approach your *vyāsāsana* and offer you obeisances in the appropriate mood. Rather, every year it seems a more awesome task. Despite such reservations, it is our duty and ecstasy to make the best effort we can, thinking that at least today we must try to glorify you with heartfelt gratitude and sincere devotion. Being the topmost swanlike devotee, you can extract such sentiments as there are from amongst the abundant pollutants in our consciousness as we recite your *praṇāmas:*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You once joked that if the sun never sets on the British Empire, how come it never shines on Britain? In truth, the only sunshine the British populace has ever enjoyed has been your rays of compassion toward them. There was hardly a glimmer of light before you carefully trained your chosen devotees to bring your mission to these shores. Since then the sunshine of your mercy has caused a radical climatic change.

No more the endless depression of grey material existence, enveloped in the mists and drizzle of selfish, hopeless attempts at pleasure—now an anticyclone in which the dark clouds of illusion have parted to allow a glimpse of the clear sky of pure *bhakti*. All at once our spirits have lifted as we discover our eternal nature, the source of our existence, and life’s true purpose. It is a veritable Indian summer in the dark winter age.

The warmth of your compassion has activated numerous, dedicated devotees anxious to bring the miserable Brits out of their dark hovels into the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Books are being distributed as never before, more temples opened, more programs and festivals, more people chanting and taking up devotional service. And all of this because you continually bestow your blessings on the efforts of those who try to please you. Their bright faces bloom like lotuses in the sunlight.

The British Empire has had its day. It suffered the fate of so many materialistic empires before it, rising and falling on the waves of *karma*. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have created a transcendental empire that spans the globe. Indeed, the sun never stops shining on an ISKCON temple. As it traverses the planet, it always beholds devotees out distributing your books. Wherever it is, it witnesses ecstatic *harināma*. And, hour after hour, it chases the strains of the *Gurv-aṣṭaka* prayers glorifying you.

The sun must marvel at your incredible potency. In every *Bhāgavatam* class at every location on the face of the earth, it hears consistent presentations of the conclusions, examples, and realizations the devotees have relished from your lips.

Perhaps it wonders, as we do, Who is this personality that, by the strength of his desire to serve his spiritual master, has rescued countless persons from the grips of *māyā* and established a worldwide association of liberated souls? Who can it be that has been so blessed by Lord Caitanya to unfold His mission of establishing a golden age within the Kali-yuga, and yet that cares for each individual, expertly nurturing the wavering faith and fragile creeper of each of his followers?

The more we discover Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the more we realize your glories and our deficiences. That’s why, once again as we come before you, we are filled with more wonder in our minds and more trepidation in our hearts, and we feel much less qualified to approach you, what to speak of making an attempt at glorification.

Therefore, we find ourselves simply begging for benedictions. May we always be grateful for your enormous mercy toward us. May we always appreciate the life you have given us within this ISKCON society. May we always want to please you through all our activities.

And may we always remain your humble servants.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, England,

Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda
