## Birmingham

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your most divine lotus feet.

We were recently listening to one of your early classes in New York. You were explaining that you were quite happy living in Vṛndāvana and didn’t like New York, but that you were duty bound to execute the order of your spiritual master. Out of genuine humility, you said that you were in New York preaching out of duty. However, we know that your reason for coming to the West was much deeper than duty. Rather, you came out of your deep compassion for the suffering conditioned souls. It is not possible that the feats you performed in establishing the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement could have been accomplished by a person impelled merely by duty alone. You came and you gave so much, yet in return you asked for so little. In years to come, people will realize what a great contribution you have made to the world. In fact, you have made the biggest contribution. In times to come people will know how you have brought light to the world when it was in its darkest hour.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are a *mahā-mahābhāgavata* devotee, on an equal platform with *mahājanas* like Bhīṣma, Nārada, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, etc., your are completely perfect in your *pracāra* and *ācāra*. In your Bhaktivedanta purports you have synthesized and crystalized the conclusions of such great *ācāryas* as Rāmānuja, Madhva, the six Gosvāmīs, and Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura. You have presented the *siddhānta* of Vaiṣṇava philosophy in such a way as to change crows into swans. Your preaching energy is so potent that it is clearly evident that you are the person whom the previous *ācāryas* foretold would take Vaiṣṇavism across the seas to the land of the *mlecchas.* Your *ācāra* is no less impressive. Your personal example is so spotless that even our enemies who look for defects in our movement have never found fault in you. They look for and find fault in many aspects of our Society’s affairs, but they have always stopped short of your divine lotus feet. In this way they pay homage to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as time progresses we are observing that ISKCON is becoming a more and more prominent in Society, and in fact people are increasingly looking toward the devotees for direction. So especially in times like this, when there are so many favorable opportunities to present the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we pray to you for the strength to be able to represent you in a way that would please you. We pray to be able to preach boldly but sensitively, purely and without compromise.

So on this most auspicious day of your appearance, just as the worshiper of mother Gaṅgā offers Gaṅgā water back to her in supplication, we having nothing more valuable to give you than the devotion to you that you yourself have bestowed on us. So we humbly pray that we may be able to give you pleasure by acting as instruments for spreading your movement, a task which, though having already been accomplished by you, we have been allowed to partake in by your unlimited mercy.

Your worthless servants in Birmingham, England.  
(written by Govinda-bhāṣya dāsa)

Govinda-bhāṣya dāsa / Parantapa dāsa / Kṛṣṇa-kathā dāsa / Sādhu-saṅga dāsa / Bhakta Mark / Bhakta Steve

## Bombay

*bhṛtya-lakṣaṇa-jijñāsur*  
*bhaktaṁ kāmeṣv acodayat*  
*bhavān saṁsāra-bījeṣu*  
*hṛdaya-granthiṣu prabho*

**“O my worshipable Lord, because the seed of lusty desires, which is the root cause of material existence, is within the core of everyone’s heart, You have sent me to this material world to exhibit the symptoms of a pure devotee” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.10.3).**

“A devotee who comes here in accordance with the order of the Supreme Lord shows by his personal behavior how to become a pure devotee. A pure devotee, therefore, is a practical example for all living entities, including Lord Brahmā.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

The description of Prahlāda Mahārāja in the above *śloka* is most certainly applicable to you. By your perfect vision and understanding you so kindly established the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, wherein you have unlimitedly provided the opportunities for conditioned souls to engage in both prescribed duties as well as spontaneous service. As you have explained in *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* prescribed duties gradually purify one from the particular modes in which he is entangled, while duties in attachment elevate one in transcendental loving service to the Lord. Thus, those who are fully convinced about the ultimate goal of life can make perfect progress in your movement, even bypassing *varṇāśrama-dharma.* Establishing such a wonderful movement is only one of your great successes. But to ensure that we can properly conduct this movement, you personally showed by your exemplary life how one can and should engage every facility in the material world in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Hence the relevance of the *śloka* of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* quoted at the start.

In Bombay—your “office”—you have set up a magnificent temple with incredible facilities for preaching. We are insignificant conditioned souls full of material desires, with no actual interest in serving the Supreme Lord. For us the above prayer of Prahlāda Mahārāja is particularly significant because here at your Bombay temple it is especially true that only by faithfully following your example of *yukta-vairāgya* can we execute your mission. Because your temple is so opulent, even if we forget your preaching vision to a minor degree, havoc will greet us.

You intended the Bombay temple to be a perfect example of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You established the Bombay temple to particularly attract the topmost segment of the Indians and foreigners, so that when they become Kṛṣṇa conscious the rest of the world will automatically follow them. So Bombay has the particular mission of preaching to the *śreṣṭhas* of the material society. Then it follows that our own devotional performance here also must be first class.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance anniversary, we pray to the Supreme Lord to give us the energy and the wisdom to come to the platform of using everything here only for your pleasure. We wish to make your “office” administratively and spiritually the best. Naturally the first ingredient for success is our own cooperative endeavor. Additionally, we are solely dependent on your blessings and mercy to accomplish this mission—the “best’ people of the society coming to your best office to get the best introduction to you. In this way, when your glories spread in the material world and the conditioned souls come in touch with you, then and only then will they get the taste of spiritual life and abandon their materialistic pursuits. Then, as the Ganges water is offered to the Ganges, we can offer your project to you for your transcendental satisfaction.

Your eternal servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī Temple in Bombay, India,  
(written by Rasarāja dāsa)

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to your divine appearance in this otherwise meaningless world.

O Prabhupāda! My mind and words are groping for a handle at this point. Although your stature and person are far, far beyond our comprehension and words, everything is still going on by your grace, and we are always remembering you. There are so many books about you, there are devotees’ remembrances of you on cassette tape, and ISKCON is always buzzing with the phrase “Prabhupāda said…” We have you personally present in your books and recorded lectures, or “spoken *kīrtana*.” In your books you glorify the pure devotees. And you have said that these books were not written by you but by Kṛṣṇa, word by word. Kṛṣṇa enjoys glorifying His devotees like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have done what no other *ācārya* has done. Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra is Himself enjoying relations with you perpetually, so what can we say of your stature? We may only beg a little mercy from the dust of your lotus feet and pray to stay engaged in faithful service to your ISKCON.

That the Lord enjoys His relationship with Your Divine Grace is proven in this nectarean verse and purport from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which I will quote. If the whole Sixth Canto were a golden ring, inlaid with very beautiful engravings and set with many valuable gems, this purport would certainly be the central diamond, and the purport’s final three sentences the diamond’s luster. I have heard from your disciples that you very much enjoy reading your own books, so I am hopeful that this will be pleasing to you. In this way our insignificant lives may become successful.

**O my Lord, because You are endowed with causeless mercy, all opulences, all prowess, and all glories, strength, and transcendental qualities, You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of everyone.**

In this verse the words *tato ‘si bhagavān prabhuḥ* mean “Therefore You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of everyone.’’ The Supreme Personality of Godhead is endowed with all six opulences in full, and moreover He is extremely kind to His devotee. Although He is full in Himself, He nonetheless wants all the living entities to surrender unto Him so that they may engage in His service. Thus He becomes satisfied. Although He is full in Himself, He nonetheless becomes pleased when His devotee offers Him *patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyam—*a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water—in devotion. Sometimes the Lord, as the child of Mother Yaśodā, requests His devotee for some food, as if He were very hungry. Sometimes He tells His devotee in a dream that His temple and His garden are now very old and that He cannot enjoy them very nicely. Thus He requests the devotee to repair them. Sometimes He is buried in the earth, and as if unable to come out Himself, He requests His devotee to rescue Him. Sometimes He requests His devotee to preach His glories all over the world, although He alone is quite competent to perform this task. Even though the Supreme Personality of Godhead is endowed with all possessions and is self-sufficient, He depends on His devotees. Therefore the relationship of the Lord with His devotees is extremely confidential. Only the devotee can perceive how the Lord, although full in Himself, depends on His devotee for some particular work. This is explained in *Bhagavad-gītā* (11.33), where the Lord tells Arjuna, *nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savyasācin:* “O Arjuna, merely be an instrument in the fight.’’ Lord Kṛṣṇa had the competence to win the Battle of Kurukṣetra, but nonetheless He induced His devotee Arjuna to fight and become the cause of victory. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was quite competent enough to spread His name and mission all over the world, but still He depended upon His devotee to do this work. Considering all these points, the most important aspect of the Supreme Lord’s self-sufficiency is that He depends on His devotees. This is called His causeless mercy. The devotee who has perceived this causeless mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead by realization can understand the master and the servant.

O Prabhupāda! Who can understand you? You are as fathomless as the Supreme Lord, being so intimate with Him. We simply desire to remain forever your grateful and humble servants; there is no higher benediction. Any conclusion other than this we boldly declare to be ignorance.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have tolerated so much of this horrible world just to allow us to associate with you and thus become lovers of your most lovable Śrī Kṛṣṇa, through Your divine Grace. Please accept our attempts at completely bathing ourselves in the dust of your lotus feet at all times, in all places, and in all circumstances.

Your servants at New Guṇḍicā Maṇḍira, Boston, Massachusetts,  
(written by Caitanya-Nitāi dāsa in complete helplessness)

## Brihuega

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

By Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, every year we feel more and more fortunate to be part of this movement founded for saving the world, for in this way we are rising up to the mode of pure goodness from the lower modes in which we had been immersed. As you have said in your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is:* “If one wants, he can develop, by practice, the mode of goodness and thus defeat the modes of ignorance and passion.… Although there are three modes of material nature, if one is determined he can be blessed by the mode of goodness, and by transcending the mode of goodness he can be situated in pure goodness, which is called the *vasudeva* stage, a stage in which one can understand the science of God.” (*Bhagavad-gītā,* 14.10 purport)

After being beaten by the modes of ignorance and passion for so long, you have touched us with your mercy and have opened for us the door to the path of pure devotional service. You have given us the greatest gift—the spiritual pleasure that comes with Kṛṣṇa consciousness—and in this way we are giving up the taste for “chewing the chewed.” You have stopped our course to hell and given us the formula for making our lives perfect. Now it is our responsibility to follow this formula without becoming victimized by material desires and the tendency to speculate. Then we will succeed in this unique opportunity, which we don’t deserve.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, being neophyte devotees, we are still very much concerned with our material and spiritual well-being. But we know that if we make an effort to give the maximum number of conditioned souls that which you have given us, you will be very pleased and Kṛṣṇa will bestow His blessings upon us.

We pray to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā Govindacandra that we become more and more attached to your divine instructions and to those of your representatives.

Your eternal servants at New Vrajamaṇḍala, Brihuega (Guadalajara), Spain,  
(written by Paraṁ-dhāma dāsa)

## Brisbane

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On behalf of all the assembled devotees at Śrī Śrī Gaura Nitāi Mandira, we would like to present our fervent prayer that you always guide us on our progressive path back to the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Many, many lifetimes we have spent in this material world, transmigrating through countless species of life before we came in contact with this most sublime path of reconnecting to the Supreme Lord. This was due to your accepting the order of your spiritual master as your life and soul and making his desire your desire.

In this way the transcendental message has somehow or other come to us and changed our lives from crows to swans. Although we are completely unworthy of your merciful glance, you have nonetheless forced us to take it anyway, and in many daily events you continue to give us that mercy.

Please therefore let us somehow or other continue on this great mission of *saṅkīrtana.* Please make us dance in whatever way you see fit. Let us never become familiar in the association of the Vaiṣṇavas, and let us never leave their company. We make this prayer to you because we have no one else to turn to. You are the captain of our ship, you are the *ācārya,* you alone can save us.

Therefore we always beg for your instructions and aspire to always be your humble servants.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-KĪ JAYA!

Your servants in Brisbane, Australia.

## Bulgaria

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

On this glorious and most auspicious day of your appearance, we want to offer you our love and gratitude and to humbly ask you for your causeless transcendental mercy so that we can go on rendering service unto you through the medium of your bona fide representatives.We are somehow confused and afraid of addressing you directly, and we ask our compassionate spiritual masters to help us face this extraordinary responsibility. For the second time we have the opportunity to write a Vyāsa-pūjā offer-ing for you, and this time we have something that we can actually offer, although it is very smalland insignificant. We began our *saṅkīrtana-yajna* in Bulgaria and participated for the first time in the Prabhupāda Marathon (and fortunately we were not in last place). We have a new temple in the center of Sofia, where Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi mercifully reside (they agreed to appear again in Gulabsthana), and we already have some of your transcendental books in the Bulgarian language.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us surrender to the preaching mission and help us give Kṛṣṇa’s holy name to the thousands of suffering conditioned souls in our country.

Please help us not to lose our determination and enthusiasm now, since we have such a favorable opportunity to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to people who really hanker for it. Since you have so kindly written your immeasurably valuable transcendental books, please help us translate them, produce them, and distribute them.

Please help us establish ourselves steadily on the way to Kṛṣṇa. We need your kindness and compassion because as a young *yātrā* we are prone to get discouraged when we are faced with the difficulties and problems of the material world. We are simply children in spiritual life, and we need help, although we feel we must grow up and take more responsibilities in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Please help us be firmly established in the basis of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement—transcendental book distribution—because we see that there is no other way to advance in spiritual life.

A Vyāsa-pūjā offering is meant to glorify the spiritual master, but ours is just full of prayers and demands for help. We must frankly admit our total inability to present suitable prayers for your pleasure. We have done so little for you, and that is why our realizations of your glories are so meager. We can only pray for mercy and work hard under the guidance of our spiritual masters, and maybe one day we will be able to actually relish the nectar of your glories. And maybe then Nārada Muni will come to sing about you and will tell us who you actually are.

Your unworthy servants in Bulgaria,

Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## Capetown

**Govardhana—First Time Around**

Starting outside of Rādhā-kuṇḍa  
Before Sūrya shows his face,  
Offering to you obeisances,  
Praying for your grace,

We set off along the road  
That greatest souls have tread,  
The holy name upon their lips,  
Vaiṣṇava tilaka on their head.

A secret known to but rare few  
Of this world’s present population,  
One of the precious gifts you give  
To overcome our contamination.

The mind, of course, a great offender  
And taints of sin loom dismally,  
But we hold the holy names you’ve given  
And chant them incessantly.

This land is all-auspicious,  
Its dust worth more than gold.  
To purchase the most valued thing  
By your mercy love will unfold.

Your words have made the difference,  
Shown that happiness can be found  
Not in skin and bones and flesh,  
But in rolling on this ground.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave so much.  
We see you are giving still,  
More and more sublime treasure,  
We can never pay the bill.

But if we preach and work for you  
And give up lust and pride,  
Overcome duality—then  
We can feel you by our side.

On this day Lord Kṛṣṇa sent you  
To give His benediction,  
Please bless us with the love  
To keep pushing on your mission.

Your servants in Capetown, South Africa,  
(written by Medhavī dāsa)

## Caracas

From a distant land came a great spiritual master  
To teach us the Absolute Truth, giving us real life.  
By his transcendental words he is still living in sound.  
To increase our appreciation we have to read his books more.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you expressed your infinite compassion on all living entities in this passage from your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.31.10 purport): “We are very anxious to establish universal brotherhood among human beings, but we should take into consideration that even the worms are our brothers, what to speak of other living entities. Therefore, we should be concerned about all living entities.”

Who else but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is so concerned about the conditioned souls that he would sacrifice all his energy for their eternal benefit? You dealt with everyone so personally and mercifully. We beg you to instill the same sentiment of *para-upakāra* in our heart. You are the topmost *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,* a real *paramhaṁsa* Vaiṣṇava. When will the day come when this spirit will fill our hearts, thus allowing us to treat every living entity Kṛṣṇa consciously?

You know perfectly what our disease is and how to cure it, as you perfectly express in the following passage from your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* (10.11 purport): “Due to the contamination of material association through many, many millions of births, one’s heart is always covered with the dust of materialism, but when one engages in devotional service and constantly chants Hare Kṛṣṇa, the dust quickly clears, and one is elevated to the platform of pure knowledge.… The pure devotee does not have to worry about the material necessities of life; he need not be anxious because when he removes the darkness from his heart, everything is provided automatically by the Supreme Lord, who is pleased by the loving devotional service of the devotee.”

You are teaching us how to depend on Kṛṣṇa’s protection, to always put our faith in the spiritual energy instead of trying to counteract the material reactions, which are due to the three modes of material nature. Once the darkness of the heart is removed, one is able to see his real position as a humble servant of the servant of the servant of the Lord, and in this way one will always stay in the association of devotees. Once you said, “No matter what happens, never leave this ISKCON movement.” So our love for you can be shown when we tolerate any difficulty to remain under the shelter of your lotus feet.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Caracas, Venezuela.

## Chicago

*bhavanti puruṣā loke*  
*mad-bhaktās tvām anuvratāḥ*  
*bhavān me khalu bhaktānāṁ*  
*sarveṣāṁ pratirūpa-dhṛk*

**“\[Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva said to Prahlāda Mahārāja,\] Those who follow your example will naturally become My pure devotees. You are the best example of My devotee, and others should follow in your footsteps.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.10.21)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Just as Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva accepts Prahlāda Mahārāja as the best example of His devotee, so we, your ever-struggling yet still aspiring servants, accept you as the best example of a pure devotee. We are thus inspired to offer our praise to you, our eternal spiritual master, on this most glorious anniversary of your appearance in this world.

While trying to glorify Your Divine Grace today, we are reminded of your explanation of worship of the Ganges. When one wants to worship the Ganges, one takes a little water from it and offers it back with devotion. Also, when one wishes to worship the Supreme Lord, one can take flowers, fruits, etc., that have been supplied by the Lord and offer them back to Him with love. Similarly, we wish to worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your own words, which perfectly glorify the personality of a pure devotee.

While discussing your books on one occasion, you remarked that Kṛṣṇa wrote the books, that He chose the words. Thus we have no choice but to believe that the words you chose for us to read in praise of pure devotees were also chosen by Kṛṣṇa for us to read in praise of you. We therefore offer you a tiny sample of your own words that we feel strongly establish your position as the best example of a pure devotee. In so doing, we beg you to please find some hint of love and devotion in our dark hearts to mercifully accept.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.13 *purport:*

“*Śrīmad Bhāgavatam,* therefore, is meant for all the living beings all over the universe for total liberation from all kinds of material bondage. Such transcendental narrations of the pastimes of the Lord can be described only by liberated souls like Vyāsadeva and his bona fide representatives who are completely merged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord. Only to such devotees do the pastimes of the Lord and their transcendental nature become automatically manifest by dint of devotional service.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.4 *purport:*

“The great sages are always anxious to do good to the people in general.… Forgetful men do not know the right path for peace and prosperity. However, the sages know it well, and therefore for the good of all men they are always anxious to perform acts which may bring about peace in the world. They are sincere friends to all living entities, and at the risk of great personal inconvenience they are always engaged in the service of the Lord for the good of all people.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.4.12:

“Those who are devoted to the cause of the Personality of Godhead live only for the welfare, development and happiness of others. They do not live for any selfish interest.…”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.10.3 *purport:*

“*Nitya-siddha* devotees come from Vaikuṇṭha to this material world to teach, by their personal example, how to become a devotee. The living entities in this material world can take lessons from such *nitya-siddha* devotees and thus become inclined to return home, back to Godhead.… A pure devotee, therefore, is a practical example for all living entities.…”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.15 *purport:*

“The pure devotees whose only business is serving are honored by the names Prabhupāda and Viṣṇupāda, which indicate such devotees to be representatives of the lotus feet of the Lord. Anyone, therefore, who takes shelter of the lotus feet of a pure devotee by accepting the pure devotee as his spiritual master can be at once purified. Such devotees of the Lord are honored equally with the Lord because they are engaged in the most confidential service of the Lord, for they deliver out of the material world the fallen souls whom the Lord wants to return home, back to Godhead. Such pure devotees are better known as vicelords according to revealed scriptures.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Teachings of Lord Caitanya, page 28:*

“Vaiṣṇavas, or devotees, are usually very compassionate upon conditioned souls. Without even being invited, a devotee will go from door to door to enlighten people and to bring them out of the darkness of nescience by injecting knowledge of the living entity’s constitutional position as a servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Such devotees are empowered by the Lord to distribute devotional consciousness, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to the people in general. They are known as authorized spiritual masters, and it is by their mercy that a conditioned soul gets the seed of devotional service. The causeless mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is first appreciated when one comes in touch with a bona fide spiritual master who can bring the conditioned soul to the highest position of devotional life.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.8 *purport:*

“A pure devotee of the Lord like Brahmā and persons in his disciplic succession are always unhappy to see the perplexities of the conditioned souls, who are suffering the onslaughts of the threefold miseries which pertain to the body and mind, to the disturbances of material nature, and to many other such material disadvantages.… Unless the devotees of the Lord take pity on them and teach them the right path, their lives are hopeless failures. The devotees of the Lord who voluntarily take the responsibility of raising the foolish materialistic sense enjoyers are as confidential to the Lord as Lord Brahmā.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

\* \* \*

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.4.17–18 *purport:*

“As an astrologer can see the future fate of a man, or an astronomer can foretell the solar and lunar eclipses, those liberated souls who can see through the scriptures can foretell the future of all mankind. They can see this due to their sharp vision of spiritual attainment. All such transcendentalists, who are naturally devotees of the Lord, are always eager to render welfare service to the people in general. They are the real friends of the people in general.… The unfortunate men of this age are always reluctant to give a reception to the transcendentalists who are representatives of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and selfless workers always busy in planning something which may help everyone in all statuses and orders of life. The greatest philanthropists are those transcendentalists who represent the mission of Vyāsa, Nārada, Madhva, Caitanya, Rūpa, Sarasvatī, etc. They are all one and the same. Their personalities may be different, but the aim of the mission is one and the same, namely, to deliver the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the best example of such a devotee.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of your perfect Vaiṣṇava humility, you may never admit your incomparably exalted position. But we know that you are the perfect example of a pure devotee. This fact no one can deny. We have unflinching faith that “those who follow your example will naturally become \[the Lord’s\] pure devotees. You are the best example of \[the Lord’s\] devotee, and others should follow in your footsteps.”

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the guidance and strength needed to properly follow in your footsteps one by one on the safe road back to Godhead, and to also provide this most wonderful opportunity to others.

Your grateful and aspiring servants at New Sevā-kuñja, Chicago Illinois.  
(written by Dānakelī-devī dāsī)

## Chittagong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. Today, as every day, as the weeks, months and years continue to roll past, I am suffering from a severe and ever-increasing case of *rasābhāsa*.

I feel so blessed to have had but even a few rare opportunities of your *darśana,* fortunate at having felt your causeless mercy deep inside, and grateful for the chance to attempt to serve your mission. But at the same time I suffer from a deep depression at being so totally useless to you, unable to rise up to the high platform that you demand, and at being so completely inadequate to sincerely do anything to serve you or your true disciples.

Oh, how many more years must pass before I will be able to live with myself, without this shame and embarrassment overcoming me whenever I attempt to remember and serve you? Will this condition ever pass? Will I ever be able to stand up to answer your call with my thoughts clear and unencumbered by these feelings of inadequacy and incompetence? Oh, when will I, realizing that my own feelings are insignificant and not worth caring about, begin to serve you unflinchingly and without being burdened down with these shackles? O Śrīla Prabhupāda, pray have mercy upon this fallen soul.

Your useless servants at Śrī Puṇḍarīk Dhām, Chittagong, Bangladesh,

Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda

Nisthula dāsa

Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## Christchurch

1\.

We think we don’t need God,  
This is our illusion,  
Seeing death all around brings only confusion.  
We think we’ll live forever,  
This is not a fact,  
Death is waiting for us all,  
And will stop us in our track.

2\.

We were going down, into a lower species of life,  
By using this human form for what it is not meant for—  
Eating, sleeping, mating, and defending,  
and nothing more.  
Look at this world.  
This is what we were depending on  
To give us all some so-called pleasure,  
Not knowing that the goal of life  
Was in serving Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

3\.

Then one so dear to Kṛṣṇa  
Appeared on the scene.  
He was one of the greatest ācāryas  
There has ever been.  
He appeared in 1896—  
This was Kṛṣṇa’s plan  
To spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness across the Western land.

4\.

He came but did not compromise,  
He presented it as it is.  
He destroyed the very illusion  
Which we thought to be our bliss.  
We were going in the wrong direction,  
We were going down,  
Until we met you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
And you turned us right around:  
“Back home, back to Godhead,  
That is where you belong.  
Can’t you see, my lost child,  
How Māyā is so strong?

5\.

“Now chant Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
This is what you should do,  
And enter my ISKCON, that will  
Keep you safe.  
You have been here so long,  
You have forgotten what it is all about.  
Just follow my direction  
And the plan I have laid out.  
This will destroy the very illusion  
Which brought about your confusion.  
You were thinking you were Kṛṣṇa,  
Of this there is no doubt.  
By following this process  
You will find that out.  
You don’t have to change a thing,  
Leave it as it is.  
It’s coming down from Kṛṣṇa,  
It’s full of transcendental bliss.”

6\.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All glories to you!  
Where would we be right now  
If it weren’t for you?  
You are such a great example,  
You followed your guru’s instructions  
To perfection.  
When you got lakṣmī,  
You printed books  
And distributed them to the English-speaking people.  
You did so well,  
You kept yourself from Māyā’s illusion,  
At no point was there confusion  
On what had to be done.  
Guru’s instruction was there,  
This kept you from all fear,  
You did what no one had done before:  
Spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness door to door in the Western world.  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All glories unto you!

From your eternal servants in Christchurch, New Zealand,  
(written by Kalasaṁvara dāsa)

Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## Cochabamba

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances to your holy lotus feet.

We are mainly grandsons of Your Divine Grace, having taken shelter at the feet of your good sons. Although we are not fortunate enough to have had a vision of your *vapuḥ,* we feel nonetheless blessed by the huge heritage coming down from our spiritual ancestors, and especially from Your Divine Grace.

Naturally we feel a special affection toward our grandfather, as we see through the pages of your wonderful books the extraordinary warmth, kindness, care, and love of a true well-wisher managing your far-flung ISKCON movement expertly, guided undoubtedly by the Supreme Lord to be, whenever needed, as soft as a rose or as hard as a thunderbolt.

Our movement is growing, giving us the chance to serve you, although our efforts to preach are usually plagued with defects and lack of spiritual qualities, making it harder for the conditioned souls to enter upon the path toward you.

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we beg at your lotus feet to help your grandsons accomplish your will. Please bless us with your kindness and wisdom to be able to reclaim the fallen souls back to sanity at your lotus feet. And bless us that our attempts may be free of false pride, which certainly marks our fail-ures and threatens to push us down again at any moment. Please straighten up our hunched mentality so we may serve your servants eternally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu—a *mahā-vadānya avatāra—*for you are making the conditioned souls become mad after Kṛṣṇa. We are feeling that our hearts are hard like stones, and we are restless after sense gratification. Nonetheless, your blessings can make out of these wretched conditioned souls blissful deliverers of your mercy.

The servants of your servants in Cochabamba, Bolivia.

## Columbus

On behalf of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
On your order and guidance as perceived living in sound!  
Living in your books with Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
in the spiritual world!  
Our qualification should be to personify your mercy!  
Our purity should be to remain your disciples  
birth after birth!  
On your order we give you to our disciples,  
On your order we give our disciples to you!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us Kṛṣṇa,  
and Lord Kṛṣṇa has give us you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
What Śrīla Prabhupāda has given me I will distribute—Kṛṣṇa!  
What Lord Kṛṣṇa has given me I will distribute—  
Prabhupāda!  
Our disciples may look at us as masters, but we serve  
Prabhupāda!  
We all should share the nectar of this divine person  
Prabhupāda!  
When the time comes for us to leave this mortal world behind,  
we will pray for His Divine Grace, calling out his name.  
The name—”Kṛṣṇa”—what a treat,  
sweetness from his lotus feet.  
This person—Prabhupāda—what a friend,  
his mercy never ends.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are not just a memory for us. Our spiritual life depends on realizing you every day. By your personal life, books, disciples, temples, and granddisciples, you teach us by your spiritual presence. Our wish is to have our consciousness purified by the words emanating from your books. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind and grant us purified ears to hear you and transcendental vision to perceive you in our hearts.

Here in Columbus we are now back to the basics, distributing your books. Door after door and soul after soul, your mercy remains available for everyone. As your disciples, we have to fulfill your mission, bringing you into everyone’s home. Your spiritual presence is light. Nescience is darkness. Wherever you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no nescience.

Help us realize that you are still here with us as long as we follow you in devotion. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Columbus, Ohio,

Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## Copenhagen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of the devotees in the Copenhagen *yātrā,* let me offer my prostrated humble obeisances unto the dust of your holy lotus feet.

You appeared to me in a dream in 1982. It was the first time that I dreamt of you. The instruction that you gave me then was one of the most important in spiritual life. At that time there was some confusion among some devotees after one of the original eleven spiritual masters left the camp of ISKCON. Confronted with the question “How can this happen to a pure devotee?” I was unable to give an appropriate answer, but my faith in you remained unshaken. In the dream I was sitting very close to you on a sofa, my knees almost touching yours. I asked you, “How can this happen?”

You replied, “This can happen to anyone.”

“But … he is a pure devotee!” I said.

Your next answer dispelled whatever doubts and confusion that may have been in my mind and also reaffirmed the most important principle in spiritual life: “They’re all made of my mercy.”

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvāṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyāṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is all that we are made of. Your mercy is so great that you have made so many into pure devotees. You often referred to your devotees as pure devotees simply by the fact that they are abstaining from sinful activities by following the regulative principles. One who has no desire other than to serve you according to your instructions and who is fully dedicated to your mission is certainly a pure devotee. You are the most perfect pure devotee of the Lord, and your instructions are most pure and sublime. Simply by following your instructions one can attain the highest perfection of life. You showed this principle of following the spiritual master’s order by your own example, and you often said, “I have no idea \[how these preaching successes are taking place\]. I simply follow the order of my spiritual master.” This is the *paramparā* system.

Whatever success there is, the disciple offers the result to his spiritual master, understanding full well that it is only by his grace and blessings that one’s endeavors are successful. As long as we sincerely adhere to your instructions and diligently execute your mission with firm faith and devotion, we are guaranteed success in life—*kṛṣṇa-prema.* Our purity and advancement can be measured by how strictly we serve your lotus feet.

Whether we are serving you as your direct disciples or as your granddisciples, we are all serving you under the banner of ISKCON, with you as the leader, the founder-*ācārya*. There may be so much agreement and disagreement about what your instructions are, but we know for sure that if we simply dedicate our lives to somehow or other producing and distributing your books, we are pleasing you. Of this there is no doubt. We can feel your protection and guidance if we follow your instructions, and people may be amazed at our achievements. It is not at all astonishing, actually, as by the mercy of the spiritual master even a lame man can cross mountains, a blind man can see stars, and a dumb man can recite poetry. However, if we, out of false ego, think that we are so great and advanced and begin to act independent of your instructions, then *punar mūṣika bhava,* we again become fallen.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given me a most important lesson in spiritual life, and I fervently pray that you grant me constant remembrance of this most sublime truth while serving my spiritual master, who is your very dear son. Thank you for guiding me to your very dear representative, who is so perfectly revealing your teachings and mission that even I can get a glimpse of your unfathomable glories. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your eternal servants in Copenhagen, Denmark,

Disicple of Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Navadvīpa-pati dāsa

## Costa Rica

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are so dear to all the followers of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your work is unparalleled. Your books contain everything needed for self-realization. You are present in our lives through your books, through your disciples, through your classes, through your *mūrti*. You always live with us.

Your kindness toward all humanity reminds us that we are all brothers and sisters and leads us to serve Kṛṣṇa, and your teachings are like the moon that dissipates the ignorance of this cold material world. By your grace we can understand who is God: Kṛṣṇa is God. By Kṛṣṇa’s grace we can understand who you are, a pure devotee.

Kṛṣṇa brought you, and you brought Kṛṣṇa. That is pure love.

We were lost, not knowing what to do. You came close with your divine grace (your divine books) and changed our lives. Knowledge and devotion were your gifts. We continue to be like children. The child does not grow up just because the father comes close, but he is not afraid anymore and feels loved and protected.

We need your physical presence. But even though you have withdrawn yourself from material vision, you continue to be present. You are beyond time and continue taking us out of this world.

Yes, Prabhupāda, you are beyond time, so much so that you are taking control of the future for ten thousand years, through thousands of millions of books about Kṛṣṇa, which will serve as the saving boat for everybody.

Anywhere in the world where there is an ISKCON temple, a place to fight *māyā,* your plan is working.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us to take shelter at your lotus feet so that every day we can carry out your will as our life and soul. Please help us to understand your books, to follow them, and to please you by preaching, so that others can also be benefited by this process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your humble servants in Costa Rica,

## Coventry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is the time of year that we look forward to because it is the time when we have the opportunity of speaking to you directly. It also gives us the opportunity to hear about your wonderful glories from so many devotees who knew you personally.

In our *yātrā,* apart from your two wonderful disciples whom you sent to us to guide us—His Holiness Kṛṣṇadāsa Swami and His Holiness Śivarāma Swami—not a single member serving in this *yātrā* has been blessed to have your personal *darśana* while you were on this planet. However, the amazing thing is that almost all of us feel that we know you personally, that you have always been amongst us, and that you have always been guiding us. There is no other explanation for this except that it is the result of your limitless mercy, which is not bound by the limits of time and space, and which reaches out to everyone, regardless of who they are.

When we learn more and more about you by hearing you in your tapes, by hearing about you from your disciples, and by reading the “Conversations” and “Letters” series of books, our love and admiration for you grows more and more. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you make us laugh with your wonderful sense of humor, you amaze us by your limitless spiritual realizations, you inspire us by your dynamic preaching, you settle our disputes through your lawbooks, and you train us how to run a *yātrā* by your own personal example.

We are indebted to you for rescuing us from the degrading lives that we were leading. We were pursuing the Western lifestyle, having abandoned *sanātana-dharma,* thinking it to be irrelevant and out of date. But you appeared in time through your books and through your devotees to prevent any further rot, and you saved us and put us back on the right path, the path back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if it weren’t for you, our spiritual lives would never have started. Please forgive our offenses at your lotus feet. We will always strive to serve you better and to improve our devotion to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. We are your granddisciples, and as you have mentioned in your books, the grandfather is more compassionate to his grandchildren than to his own children. Of all your grandchildren, we are the most foolish and useless. Therefore our need for your compassion is the greatest. Please take us by our hands and lovingly take us back to our real home, Goloka Vṛndāvana.

*Your grandchildren in Coventry, England,*

Haridāsa dāsa and family / Madhusūdana dāsa and family / Vidura dāsa and family / Bhakta Astley / Bhakta John / Bhakta Simon / Ramesh and family / Nanubhai and family / Jeetendra and family / Lalji and family / Sunil and Mona / Kṛṣṇa-prema

## Curitiba

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you who are the savior of the fallen souls.

All over the world people are becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious because of coming in contact with your books. Your books are distributed profusely, and your glories are increasing every day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to carry your instructions in such a way that will please you, by always distributing the holy name, *prasādam,* and your books. You are full of compassion for the fallen, conditioned souls. We are the most fallen and need your mercy constantly. We are praying to you to please give us your kind blessings so that we may be able to follow the instructions you so kindly gave us and to fulfill your desires to preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness as strongly and as purely as you did.

You personally demonstrated how, simply by maintaining firm faith and conviction in the orders of your Guru Mahārāja, you were gloriously successful in spreading Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for teaching us to appreciate the transcendental qualities of the pure devotee of the Lord.

It is our great fortune to have gotten your transcendental association, and by that association we can develop the transcendental qualities and the purity we need to help you spread this movement.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to bless us so that we may act with all good intelligence in accordance with your desire. We need your guidance. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to sustain our Kṛṣṇa conscious lives. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep us in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your servants in Curitiba, Brazil,

## Czechoslovakia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣitām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as the captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of human beings” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22).**

Sixteen years ago, by the will of providence, I had the opportunity of meeting your good disciples, whom I accepted as the captains and commanders of the ship called ISKCON, a ship you have so carefully designed and constructed under the expert guidance of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and since then I have become increasingly aware of the difficulty of crossing the dangerous ocean of Kali. At the start I thought that surrender was a matter of months or years. Now I’m slowly becoming aware that it’s really a question of a whole lifetime, provided that we’re serious. Otherwise it may take even longer—lifetimes until we fully, unconditionally, and lovingly surrender unto Kṛṣṇa. Anyone who considers surrender something cheap—something one can do “just like that”—is simply cheating himself as well as others. Yes, you have many times pointed out that unless we fully surrender to the lotus feet of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, Māyā will always capture us. It would simply be pretense and hypocrisy to say that since I’m a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, Māyā will never touched me. On the contrary, since I’m a devotee, since I’ve been engaged in devotional service, I have become increasingly aware of how powerful Māyā is. The power of Māyā is truly insurmountable!

But if we surrender to Kṛṣṇa, we can surmount her influence: *mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te*. One may say that shaving one’s head, wearing robes and *tilaka,* and following the rules and regulations make one surrendered enough. But what is enough? It is not for nothing that Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura speaks of *kali-chelā*, or the so-called devotee of Kṛṣṇa who wears *kaṇṭhī-mālā* and *tilaka* but is simply attached to women and riches. How deep is my attachment? As deep as my sinful birth. As deep as my most subtle desires for fame, profit, and distinction. As deep as millions and millions of births in the deepest regions of ignorance. Who was I before I had the great fortune of meeting your graceful devotees? An animal. A beast. Now that I have trudged the path of devotion for sixteen years, I’m slowly becoming aware of what it means to be a human being. I’m slowly becoming aware of the fact that sixteen years in devotional service is absolutely insufficient for having the audacity of pretending to be advanced enough to know what is surrender, what is pure love of Kṛṣṇa.

Surely it is much more realistic to consider myself fallen, for it is a statement of fact, whereas knowing myself to be liberated when engaged in your service—and only when engaged in your service—is a pragmatic statement describing my sixteen years of effort to escape Māyā’s implacable influence, but it is not a statement of fact. The fact is that I am a fallen conditioned soul, and the fact is also that as long as I engage in your service, in the service of the Vaiṣṇavas, in the service of Kṛṣṇa, I remain on the liberated platform. But by nature I’m fallen. Once, when a devotee was lamenting to you about his so-called fallen condition, you very gracefully countered his “pride” of being fallen by saying, “You’re not fallen, you’re insignificant!” It’s a fact, which becomes so much more evident when by the force of providence we become separated from the “cheerleading crowd of self-glorifying success-mongers,” or if we simply go out in the streets, or door to door for the purpose of spreading your literatures—without the hallelujah and gloria of the denizens of heaven. Without the big numbers, big sums, and big results.

No, it is really too great of a position to consider oneself fallen. It is a kind of subtle pride. The ocean of Kali is much too difficult to cross for those who have not yet really developed a desire to cross it in the first place. What to speak of those who bask in the glories of the inglorious, who find great significance in insignificant achievements of mundane facts and figures. There’s no greater achievement in this world than consistent and dedicated, pure, unalloyed, unmotivated service to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa.

The difficult ocean of Kali is very, very vast. There’s danger at every step that we shall be swept overboard from the ship. The storms in this ocean are very ferocious, so much so that we may become seasick and vomit all our false pride of being the enlightened ones, the selected ones, those who will save the world. In your *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma* poem you so nicely described yourself as an insignificant beggar. Why should we consider ourselves something more than beggars, more than you, the most “transcendental, insignificant beggar” New York City has ever seen? In fact we are much less than beggars. We are debauchees, who somehow or other have been blessed by the mercy of the “most insignificant beggar,” the pure and humble servant of the Lord, to understand that we have to really desire to cross this vicious and dangerous ocean of Kali. If we do not develop at least this desire, then we remain debauchees, we go on acting as debauchees, being deceitful toward others and toward ourselves as well.

Because this age has a very specific nature, it deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being. Being simply a *smārta-brāhmaṇa,* considering oneself superior by birth and education, a member of some elite which is destined to rule over the world for the next ten thousand years, doesn’t help things at all. It simply aggravates the symptoms of Kali’s influence in as much as such an attitude is a splendid example of false pride, deceitfulness, and illusion.

There’s still so much work to be done all around the world to propagate the teachings of Your Divine Grace, “the most insignificant beggar,” the humble servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja. Let us all assume our insignificant role as the insignificant servants of the most glorious “insignificant beggar” of the Supreme Lord. That will be our significant progress on the path of true surrender.

Your insignificant servants in Czechoslovakia,  
(written by Jaya Gurudeva dāsa)

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O most exalted and worshipable lord, kindly accept our humble and loving obeisances unto your divine lotus feet on this, the glorious anniversary of your brilliant appearance in the dark and troubled waters of this material world.

May we, your lowly servants of ISKCON’s Dallas *yātrā*, offer you our continuing and heartfelt gratitude for the exquisite grace and spiritual love that you unhesitatingly deign to bestow upon us all, year in and year out. We pray too that the insignificant measure of devotional service we awkwardly attempt to render you and your mission move you, in the infinite reaches of your all-compassionate heart, to look kindly upon us and ask Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandjī to shower Their sweet blessings upon us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we miss you more than mere words can convey, yet most happily we feel your divine presence in your sacred instructions, in your sublime literature, in your temple *mūrti*, and everywhere else in your worldwide movement. Please grace us, O lord, with the wisdom and determination by which each of us may overcome the devilish obstacles that so often attempt to foil our efforts to push on Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Above all, please bless us all with the nectar of unalloyed love for you, our ever well-wisher, and for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Jayate, Śrī Gurudeva! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your loving disciples and granddisciples in Dallas, Texas.

## Debe

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

Lord Kṛṣṇa was so kind to give us you, who, out of your causeless mercy, delivered to us the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This you did so expertly in innumerable ways.

The daily chanting of our sixteen rounds was your gift of regulated meditation on the Lord’s holy names, while in the *kīrtana* that you gave us we can overcome all differences to join together in ecstatic glorification of Him. *Maṅgala ārati* and the whole morning program you gave us to strengthen us spiritually to meet the demands of the day and the demands of our whole lives. Your books, so painstakingly written, were given as the law books to guide your society for the next ten thousand years, and also as our personal daily sustenance. You gave us four regulative principles to steer us away from sinful life and its inevitable karmic reactions. Your gift of Deity worship trains the once-*mlecchas* in brahminical standards and helps us see the Lord as a real person. Your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness you gave us as our mission—a lifetime’s full engagement, by your mercy, to become dear to Kṛṣṇa. The gift of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam* controls our otherwise uncontrollable tongues, and thus all our senses, and engages them in Kṛṣṇa’s service, and it is your secret weapon for the unsuspecting nondevotees who do not care to be engaged. You gave us temples that are *tīrthas* in the lands of godlessness, oases in the desert for the parched and weary traveller through this material world. You gave us the disciplic succession, firmly rooting us in the bona fide *paramparā* amidst an ocean of bogus philosophies and practices. You gave us ISKCON—a house in which the whole world can live and associate with devotees. Your life was a precious gift to us, an enduring example of pure and perfect devotion to Kṛṣṇa, selflessly given to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to millions.

Your gifts to us are endless—so many facilities for engaging in devotional service, so many instructions on how to engage. In fact, anywhere we look we can see something that you gave us—an activity, a facility, a proper attitude and understanding for becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious.

And all these wonderful gifts of yours are opportunities to constantly be reminded of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And remembrance of you is also your gift, for that remembrance is our greatest inspiration to continue in devotional service and to not neglect all that you have given us.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, as your humble servants we want to try to thank you (although our gratitude is very meagre compared to your generosity) for this gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We were spiritual paupers, but we have become wealthy by your mercy, and we pray that we may never become ungrateful and turn away from what you have given us.

We will never by able to repay the debt we owe you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we pray for the determination and sincerity to offer our lives in your service, making your gifts available to countless other conditioned souls who are suffering untold miseries without Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We beg to always be recipients of your mercy and to remain your humble servants.

Your servants in Debe, Trinidad.  
(written by Jaya Lalitā-devī dāsī)

## Debrecen

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances in the dust of your most glorious lotus feet.

You are our most kind, compassionate, ever-blissful, most wonderful, greatest, dearest spiritual grandfather.

You have presented to us the wisdom of Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, and now on this most glorious (ever-increasingly blissful) day, please allow us to glorify your greatness with his wonderful words:

*patita-pāvana tomā bine keho nāi*  
*jāhāra nikaṭe gele pāpa dūre jāy*  
*emona doyāla prabhu kebā kothā pāy*  
*tomāra hṛdoye sadā govinda-viśrām*  
*govinda kohena—mora vaiṣṇava parāṇ*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, please stay in our heart and give us the intelligence to read your most glorious books, to distribute them, and to preach all the time everywhere. Without you our life is completely useless.

Your eternal servants in Debrecen, Hungary,

Lavaṅga dāsa / Subala dāsa / Caitanya dāsa / Raghunātha dāsa / Bhakta Tamas / Bhakta Janos

## Denver

Dear Śrila Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

When we meditate upon you, everything becomes clear. You have given us Kṛṣṇa and the method to attain Him, the ultimate goal, your most beloved. Our *pūjās,* sacrifices, and austerities have meaning when we remember how you would perform them on the highest level, and how we too can perform on that level by following in your footsteps.

Your words purify our consciousness by cutting away the misconceptions of who we are and who God is. By meditating on your words and how to apply your instructions, we cross beyond this world of death and sorrow, for your instructions are Kṛṣṇa’s instructions, meant to deliver us.

By meditating on you, the meaning of *guru* becomes clear. The *guru* is Kṛṣṇa coming to teach us how to serve Him. You, being in pure love with Kṛṣṇa, are one with Him in the sense that lover and beloved never oppose each other’s desire. So you would never say or do anything not in harmony with His will.

By meditating on you as *guru,* we learn to love and respect and honor those who try with all their hearts to do and say and act as you would. And we learn to correct any fault in our own consciousness that prompts us to worship “my *guru*” as separate from you, thus propping up our own shaky position as distinct and separate from others. The *guru* is everyone’s *guru*. And you are everyone’s *guru*.

Though we still may be puzzled in the details of our lives, by meditating on you we can gain courage to act in the way you would act.

By meditating on your labor of writing your books, we can understand the importance of your books in our lives and of these books being the basis of the great change taking place as the world is brought under the sway of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

You desire the best for us, Śrila Prabhupāda. And by meditating on you, we desire the best for ourselves. So please let us meditate upon you. Let us meditate on you as the body of ISKCON, and on ourselves as various parts of that body, working together to facilitate the success of the whole. Let ISKCON move in the world as you did, pushing on the mission of putting Kṛṣṇa in His rightful place.

Let us meditate on you, Śrila Prabhupāda, for you are purely spiritual, and by this spiritual meditation we will reawaken our love for your love, Śri Śri Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants in Denver, Colorado.

## Dublin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your servants in ISKCON Dublin are becoming more and more eager to serve your mission; they see that you will eternally be in the center and leading this movement. Who wouldn’t be attracted by your glorious leadership and by your books, which are forever goldenly inscribed in this Kali-yuga?

All the previous *ācāryas* become present through your pen. The repeated chanting and distribution of your books is pounding constantly at the veil of ignorance that covers this isle. Though the devotees have suffered losses, temporary setbacks, they have overcome them and pressed forward by following your orders.

One day, every person who lives in Dublin—indeed, in all of Ireland—will get the opportunity to hear Kṛṣṇa’s name in peace and the opportunity to hear Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes and Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. It is a glorious treasure you have brought us in the form of this *saṅkīrtana* movement: so many facets, so many qualities to be aspired for—qualities of the transcendental Vaiṣṇava, gentleman, and lady.

We have full confidence in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please shower your mercy unrestrictedly here in Dublin and remove from our hearts the wicked impediments and thorns that prevent the awakening of the flickering flame of service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, in the hearts of the Irish citizens.

Your servants in Dublin, Ireland,

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Sent by Kṛṣṇa,  
ambassador of truth,  
distributing love of Godhead,  
the ripened fruit.

Resident of Vṛndāvana,  
you carry its charm,  
Ramaṇ-reti,  
Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.

Instructions of *guru*  
you took to heart,  
from the Absolute Truth  
you did not depart.

Love of Kṛṣṇa,  
the only cure,  
given to one  
whose heart is pure.

Auspicious meeting,  
your Guru Mahārāja knew,  
desire expressed  
in 1922.

Paramahaṁsa Ṭhākura,  
transcendental sage,  
transcendental vision,  
all living beings you engage.

Rādhā-Dāmodara temple,  
the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is born,  
the three worlds rejoice,  
the Golden Age dawns.

A*rcā-vigraha*  
you lovingly adore,  
you are the first  
on America’s shore.

Books are the basis,  
guide for all nations,  
Bhaktivedanta purports,  
a lifetime of dedication.

Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,*  
gift divine,  
unflinching trust  
in the order sublime.

Utility is the principle,  
the perfection of renunciation.  
Everything belongs to Kṛṣṇa,  
no reason for speculation.

Preaching is the essence,  
purity is the force.  
Redirecting humanity,  
you changed its course.

Always meditating on  
how they will be saved,  
you, like Lord Caitanya,  
love of Kṛṣṇa freely gave.

Dancing at Ratha-yātrā,  
a childhood dream.  
Distributing *prasādam,*  
*halavah* and cream.

Atlanta to Calcutta,  
you did not tire,  
traveling to save us,  
trapped in this material quagmire.

Kind to the fallen,  
helpless souls,  
you send your preachers  
door to door.

Invested with  
guru-śakti,  
you gave us  
prema-bhakti.

J*īvera svarūpa haya*  
kṛṣṇera nitya-dāsa,  
“Everyone is God”  
you expose as a farce.

Armed with the vision of eternity,  
you make your stand,  
and make millions of devotees  
throughout the land.

**“Y**our love for me  
you show by cooperation,  
not self-aggrandizement  
and self-adoration.”

Always situated at your lotus feet,  
we pray  
from the devotional path  
we do not stray.

Your aspiring servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādha-Rādhānātha Temple of Understanding, Durban, South Africa,  
(written by Nanda-kumāra dāsa)

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I feel somewhat like a hypocrite writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering. I find it difficult due to my offenses.

I know that in recent history there has been no greater devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa than yourself. By your mercy so many people now know of Lord Kṛṣṇa who would have otherwise never heard of Him. By your mercy so many people have surrendered to Lord Kṛṣṇa who would have otherwise cleared a large and wide path to hell. You are my *guru* and you have initiated many others into the Caitanya cult. Many of your disciples have fallen away. Others are following your instructions strictly, and then there are those like myself, who are doing something to help push on your movement, but not as much as we should be.

Because of your mercy I am maintaining and managing a small preaching center in Connecticut. We have Sunday feast programs, and between twenty-five and forty guests come every Sunday. We sometimes have home programs and sometimes college programs, but not very often.

I am surrendering somewhat, but not as much as I should be, nor as much as I have in the past. I’m sure other devotees are in similar situations. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me and others like myself continue surrendering at least as much as we are now and to increase more and more in the future.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being so kind to us all these years. Please continue showering your mercy upon us, and help us remain under that shower.

Your servants in East Hartford, Connecticut,  
(written by Pyāri-mohana dāsa)
