## Māyāpur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It seems that as we grow older, time goes faster. But still we are laden with material contamination, namely, lust, greed, envy, and the lot!

Just dreams of pure devotion won’t suffice any longer. There’s no time! Our own promises of full surrender to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet sometime in the future are no longer practical. It cannot be put off and delayed any longer, day by day, decade after decade, sin after sin, regret after regret.

WE MUST ACTUALLY DO IT, SURRENDER TO ŚRILA PRABHUPĀDA’S LOTUS FEET.

The philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is irrefutable, and the truth of it all is clearly perceptible. The material world is a hopeless labyrinth; it’s totally mad! We now, to some degree, understand that factually, not just theoretically. But still we cling to the material world, at least in part, sometimes making spiritual progress and sometimes yielding to temptation.

THUS THE BATTLE AGAINST MĀYĀ GOES ON.

The example of the little sparrow’s determination to drink up the ocean and receive the mercy and protection of Śrī Garuḍadeva is our only consolation.

WHAT CAN THIS LITTLE WRETCHED CONDITIONED SOUL DO?

We regret our wrongdoings. We can think of Śrīla Prabhupāda. We can remember his activities and personal dealings. We can meditate upon his numerous pictures and *mūrtis*. We can see and hear his tapes and read and distribute his books. We can render devotional service by constantly working hard to spread his movement.

We can learn to feel appreciation for all that Prabhupāda has given us, and also appreciate his glorious spiritual position.

We can begin to feel real affection for him, which will always sustain us in all situations of life; everything may be taken away, but that remains perpetually. That affection for Śrīla Prabhupāda is not a mundane or artificial thing, and it grows exponentially.

We pray that some day soon we may be immersed in waves of such affection for Śrīla Prabhupāda, which can soften our hearts and well up in our eyes.

THERE IS NO DIFFERENCE BETWEEN LOVE OF ŚRILA PRABHUPĀDA AND LOVE OF GOD (ŚRĪ ŚRĪ RĀDHĀ-KṚṢṆA). ŚRILA PRABHUPĀDA IS THE TRANSPARENT VIA MEDIUM.

We can only beg. There is no other means at our disposal. We beg for one fraction of a drop of mercy and protection from Śrīla Prabhupāda to keep us at his lotus feet.

THERE IS NO OTHER TREASURE TO SEEK BUT ŚRILA PRABHUPĀDA’S LOTUS FEET. THEY ARE SUFFICIENT FOR US. PLEASE, PLEASE, WE BEG, KEEP US AT THOSE LOTUS FEET AND DO NOT LET US FALL AWAY, IN SPITE OF OURSELVES!

From the devotees of Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandira, Śrīdhām Māyāpur, India.

## Melbourne

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As I enter the kitchen to prepare the Sunday Feast, I pray, “Let me meditate on your instructions.” My only desire is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from your lotus-like mouth. You established the Sunday Love Feasts many years ago as an important part of our preaching. In Melbourne in 1975 you gave your solemn decree: “No one within a ten-mile radius of this temple should go hungry. Let all the hungry men of Melbourne come and eat.” Since then we have distributed over five million plates of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*.

**Kitchen Meditations**

1\. The yogurt has thickened overnight. It was once milk, but in contact with the souring agent it has changed. Similarly, our original love for Kṛṣṇa has been transformed into lust by the sour mode of passion. Śrīla Prabhupāda—please help me to transform my lust back to love of God.

2\. I unpack the box of mangoes; some are ripe, some green. They’re all mangoes, but the ripe ones are better, tastier and more relishable. My daily devotional service is service no doubt, but it’s like the green mango. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me ripen and mature my love for Kṛṣṇa in the association of your devotees.

3\. I slice the eggplants one by one. Every so often, I expose a tiny worm that has been living there, illusioned by Māyā’s covering potency to think that “This life is not so bad.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect me from Māyā’s spell. Don’t let me forget that this material world is “no place for a gentleman.”

4\. I take the dried coconuts, one by one, and shake them to see whether they’re fresh. One produces a rattling sound. It has dried and become detached from the husk. Similarly, although you walked with us, your transcendental body was never attached to this material world. Śrīla Prabhupāda—allow me to attach myself to your feet. Let your divine instructions increase my knowledge in devotional service and help me to detach myself from this dry material world.

5\. I am ready to cook. I never tasted anything you cooked, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but I heard it was very nectarean because you were cooking for your dearly beloved Kṛṣṇa. I feel like an imitation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow me to serve you nicely, with no tinge of personal desire. And then maybe one day I can actually cook for Kṛṣṇa.

6\. I melt the butter for the *halavā*. It has been expertly prepared from the milk of Kṛṣṇa’s cows. You have stated, “As no layman can separate butter from milk, so no one can extricate the merged self from matter by acquiring some material qualification.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are expertly disentangling my real self, which has been merged in the external energy of the Lord.

7\. Toasting the *besan* for the *laḍḍus,* I remember the saying, “A confectioner is never attracted by sweetmeats.” Kṛṣṇa has produced innumerable spiritual beauties and is not at all attracted by the false beauty of this material creation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me develop my attraction for the all-attractive Lord Kṛṣṇa; then only will my attraction to the so-called beauty of this world become slackened.

8\. I heat the ghee and toss in a handful of mustard seeds. One of those tiny seeds is like a universe containing innumerable planets. We are living on this tiny cold, dark planet, thinking we are enjoying, but our universe is like one mustard seed in a whole bag of mustard seeds. “The planets in the spiritual sky are at least three times the number of those in the material sky.… Each of them is eternal, indestructible, free from all kinds of inebrieties experienced in the material world. Each of them is self-illuminating and more powerfully dazzling than (if we can imagine) the total sunshine of millions of mundane suns. The inhabitants of those planets are liberated from birth, death, old age, and diseases and have full knowledge of everything.… They have nothing to do there except to render loving service to the Supreme Lord.… \[The\] spiritual happiness in the eternal kingdom of God cannot be imagined even by the great *brahmacārīs* or *sannyāsīs, …* but such happiness is factually attained by the unalloyed devotees of the Lord, by His divine grace.”

9\. I chop a bunch of fresh coriander leaves. Each large stem was produced from a single coriander seed. Each and every coriander seed on the spice shelf will similarly sprout if planted in the ground. But if we fry these seeds, they will never sprout. Similarly, you have ignited the fire of devotional service in our hearts to burn up the seeds of sinful desire. Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me the strength to keep this fire of Kṛṣṇa consciousness burning in my heart.

10\. In fact, Kṛṣṇa is everything. He provides the moon’s cooling rays, which enliven the vegetables; He sends the rain to nourish the grains, and the sun to ripen the fruits. He is the fire on which I cook, and from Him comes the ability to perform the actual cooking task. The cooking pots and the floor on which I stand are nothing but Kṛṣṇa’s external energy. The ability to remember all this comes from Kṛṣṇa, and, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all this detailed knowledge can be realized by your grace.

11\. “If one offers Me, with love and devotion, a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water, I will accept it.”

12\. The hungry men of Melbourne arrive. Soon they will sit and eat Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam* and be released from all kinds of sins. The duration of their lives will increase, their existence will be purified, and they will gain strength, health, happiness, and satisfaction. But first they hear the *kīrtana* and the *Bhagavad-gītā* class. “This hearing is the expert treatment for the mad mind,” you have stated, “and eating the foodstuffs offered to Kṛṣṇa is the appropriate diet for the suffering patient. This treatment is the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

13\. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the hungry man feels pleasure, nourishment, and relief from hunger with every morsel of food. Similarly, by the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which you have kindly given, we gain devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from all other things. Thank you for saving me, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please allow me to stay in your ISKCON, and give me the strength to push on the preaching as you desire.

Your servants in Melbourne, Australia,  
(written by Kūrma dāsa)

## Mexico City

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Although we are unfit to glorify your unlimited transcendental qualities, we want to make a humble effort to mention a drop of the unlimited ocean of mercy and kindness you have shown toward us.

As you revealed, you came to this material world just to fulfill the most intimate desire of your beloved Lord, and so you have given us the greatest of all homes, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

From the time you were a little child you always showed ideal behavior. In each one of your thoughts, acts, and words, you just had one goal: to preach the most pure love of God. Thanks again and again, our dear master and lord.

Although many of us never saw your divine form in person, we can just imagine your unlimited grace and power when we see hundreds of ISKCON temples, millions of your books distributed, your unlimited perfect instructions in your books and tapes, and the many great souls who are completely surrendered to you. All we can say from the depths of our hearts is:

ALL GLORIES, ALL GLORIES, ALL GLORIES TO YOU, ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA!

Your servants in Mexico City, Mexico,

## Miami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept this poem as our homage on this most glorious anniversary of your appearance in this world.

In the line of Nityānanda,  
Who relieved the brothers foul,  
You possess the fullest śakti  
Lord Caitanya can endow.  
On the order of your guru,  
Like the wielder of the plow,  
From you comes all strength of spirit,  
Give us strength to serve you now.

Manifesting Kṛṣṇa’s mercy,  
Like a budding lotus flower,  
You defeated the rākṣasas  
While protecting mother cow.  
In your standard books of knowledge  
You direct the reader how  
To be blissful serving Kṛṣṇa,  
Give us strength to serve you now.

In the park you started kīrtana,  
Visage grave, with knitted brow,  
Playing strongly on your karatālas,  
Chanting hour after hour.  
For an afternoon there with you,  
Who would not lay his life down?  
Bless us with your sweet remembrance,  
Give us strength to serve you now.

In this stormy sea of Kali,  
Being far from solid ground,  
We accept you as the captain  
Of our ship, from stern to bow.  
In your lessons, clearly given,  
Heard contently, hour by hour,  
You disclose the path of bhakti,  
Give us strength to serve you now.

So often did you warn us  
Of the then so distant hour  
When we’d be out of the nest,  
Flying on our fragile power.  
Now the ground is coming quickly  
And my mind begins to cower,  
O beloved spiritual master,  
Give me strength to serve you now.

As Arjuna once surrendered,  
At the the battle’s fateful hour,  
To the words of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa,  
And thus became empowered,  
I return to your sweet shelter,  
At your lotus feet I bow,  
A disciple’s only treasure  
Is the strength to serve you now.

Your servants at New Naimiṣāraṇya, Miami, Florida.

## Monterrey

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your lotus feet! Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Thank you so much for leaving so many wonderful pure devotees on this planet to carry on your preaching mission. Where would your granddisciples be without your pure devotee disciples? The world can see your purity and greatness even after you have left the planet by the way your mission is still expanding: devotees are being made, your wonderful books are being distributed—all by your empowerment of your strict followers.

Thank you so much for the instructions and protection you have left behind in your books. When we read your books, not only are we worshiping Kṛṣṇa with our intelligence, but we are also directly associating with your intimate devotional ecstasies. This morning we read this in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.2.30: “Not only should one chant and hear the holy name of the Lord as Rāma, Kṛṣṇa, … but one should also read and hear *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the association of devotees.” You describe this as the process of watering our devotional creeper. Then setting down eight points, you so mercifully instruct us in exactly how to protect our creeper. The first two are:

1\. Offenses by one at the feet of a pure devotee may be likened to the mad elephant who devastates a very good garden it enters.  
2\. One must be very careful to guard himself against such offenses at the feet of pure devotees, just as one protects a creeper by all-around fencing.

This means that we cannot even hear or see someone offend the feet of a pure devotee. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us! We have heard so many offenses against your pure disciples, the main one being that your pure disciples who are in the position of *guru* are not pure, nobody is pure, and that they are fallible. Of course we all know that no one is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You alone are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, Kṛṣṇa’s empowered representative sent here to write books about Him and spread His glories all over the world. But does it end there? It is actually a criticism of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to say that none of your disciples are pure after twenty years of chanting, following strictly, and working tirelessly to propagate your teachings. You, being an *uttama-adhikārī,* are perfectly competent to create many more *uttama-adhikārīs*. And we can judge who is pure by the many lists of symptoms in your books. And we can also judge who is a *kaniṣṭha-adhikārī* and who is trying to be independent of you and your ISKCON body.

Thank you so much for setting up spiritual life in Kali-yuga so simply and so beautifully. If we just follow your teachings, of course we will be personally directed and empowered by you. But if we don’t rise early for *maṅgala ārati,* chant our rounds, and, as you said, “read and hear *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the association of devotees,” then of course we will turn into *gṛhamedhīs*—critical, envious faultfinders. We have even heard the criticism that a pure devotee was a hard-liner and unbending. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the hardest liner against Māyā’s influence and the most unbending in presenting Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy.

Thank you so much for setting the strict, pure example for all your disciples to follow. Thank you so much for coming here, writing your books, and giving us your disciples. Thank you so much for teaching us how to avoid and counteract offenses. Thank you for chastising us every time we read your books. Thank you for saving us through the via medium of our spiritual masters, your disciples. Thank you, thank you, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please forgive us for the offenses we have heard and committed against you, your pure disciples, and your granddisciples.

Your aspiring servants in Monterrey, Spain,

Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda

Rāma-svarūpa dāsa

Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## Montreal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is saving the world from hellish conditions. All glories to your divine *guru,* who has saved us from continuous birth and death.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we would like to express our deep feelings of appreciation for all the trouble you have undergone to wake us up from the ignorance of material existence.

Here in Montreal, as you know, the struggle to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness is still going on. By your mercy devotees are still being made, books are still being distributed, and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara are still being served nicely. Please continue to give us your mercy, for without your help we can do nothing.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us remember to take shelter of your lotus feet when we have difficulties. Actually, there should be no doubts because faith in the *guru* is the ultimate shelter in this world.

Will you please bless all the devotees of the Montreal *yātrā* to be able to cooperate together in a humble attitude, in a spirit of service to your lotus feet, so that by taking shelter of your divine instructions we may go on spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in eastern Canada.

Your humble servants in Montreal, Canada.

## Munich

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories, all glories unto you!

You will be pleased to know that every year we experience more and more aspects of the unlimited ocean of nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which you have kindly brought to this world. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness is like the famous sugar candy—from whatever side you taste it, it is sweet.

Let’s take philosophy. How bewildered and ignorant everyone is about the ultimate goal of life! Even people who consider themselves theists are forced to speculate, helpless in their endeavor to cut through the veil of material existence. They don’t know the Supreme, and they dare to go so far as to declare that nobody can really know Him. If we boldly say, “Yes, of course we know who God is! Why not?” they even feel insulted. On every page of your wonderful books we can learn who God is, but they cannot see.

Devotees are uneducated sentimentalists? We can simply laugh at that. Let’s take music. Has the world ever seen such a huge repertoire of different kinds of music! The materialists have to constantly produce so-called new music. Devotees are satisfied to just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa every day. Every devotee knows how blissful it is to dance and chant in a “destruction-*kīrtana*” (as we call the most ecstatic *kīrtanas* here).

Self-hypnosis? We simply laugh at that.

Let’s take *prasādam*. How abominable and tasteless is the food consumed by the materialists! How satisfying it is to dive into wonderful *prasādam* cooked by pure devotees and offered to the Lord! There is nothing comparable in this world whatsoever.

Boring vegetarianism? We simply laugh at that.

Let’s take people. How horrible are the relationships between the nondevotees! Everything is simply based on sense gratification, which results in exploitation, quarrel, and all kinds of catastrophes. Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have manifested such well-behaved and attractive people. It is so nectarean to live in the association of these s*ādhus* that we sometimes have a hard time controlling ourselves so we do not violently roll on the floor in the dust of their lotus feet.

Having lost the family? We can simply laugh at that.

Let’s take lifestyle. Is there anywhere a better life than that of a person who is in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness? How satisfying to elevate oneself to the mode of goodness, or especially to the transcendental platform. How nice to overcome suffering by learning how to control the mind and senses. Oh, one feels so relieved when the enemies of lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness, and envy disappear.

Nothing to enjoy by serving g*uru* and Kṛṣṇa? We simply laugh at that.

Let’s take personality. What a contribution Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to this world, filled as it is with the all-destroying philosophies of *nirviśeṣa* and *śūnyavāda*! You gave us a process by which we can find our own personality; plus you gave us your own, most wonderful personality along with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, a person who is complete in all aspects. Has anyone done this before?

Brainwashed zombies? We simply laugh at that.

Let’s take… There are unlimited themes to be mentioned, but let’s take only one more: your ingenious, most innocent but most powerful books. Has the world ever seen such a wonderful medium for spreading unlimited happiness to all by giving everyone a chance to change his life? They reach every nook and corner of this planet and change lusty fools into merciful jewels. Oh, our life becomes so dry when we cannot read your books regularly. How enlivening to sit down and hear you speak the “Description of Autumn”! how exciting to be able to join with Lord Caitanya and His devotees in the cleaning of the Guṇḍicā temple just by picking up one volume of your personal ecstasies!

How ecstatic it is to develop the taste to distribute these books! Sometimes we don’t distribute so much and simply come back to the van quite exhausted; but even if we have distributed only one book on that day, that one day had more value than all our previous *karmī* births taken together. And if we come back and play one of your *bhajanas* (we especially like the *Śikṣāṣṭakam* prayers), our batteries of compassion become immediately filled up again and we develop the intense desire to bring all the people into contact with you and with what you gave us in such a wonderful way—Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Thank you very much for everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda. thank you very much.

Your fallen but thankful servants in Munich, Germany,  
(written by Diviratha dāsa)

Bṛhad-bhānu dāsa / Diviratha dāsa / Bhakta Ingo / Muralī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Susanne

## Murāri Sevaka

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You came to preach the mission of Lord Caitanya. You came to complete the vision of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. You came to fulfill the desires of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. You came to please Lord Krsna. You came to teach love for Krsna. You came to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the earth. You came to save the whole world. You came for us. And then you left. Only you didn’t leave. You left yourself behind in your books. Śrīla Prabhupāda, these books are for us after all. And as you said, whenever possible we must continue to distribute your books.

Your servants at Murāri Sevaka,

## Nagpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine self.

It is the supreme imperative of the authentic Vedic literature that a person endeavor with all his sincerity to revive his dormant spontaneous love for Lord Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa advented Himself five thousand years ago in Mathurā, and He appeared once again five hundred years ago in Māyāpur as Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to distribute pure love for Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu distributed it freely and entrusted his associates, especially the six Gosvāmīs, to preach this mission widely. They preached, and to enhance the preaching they wrote many books on devotional service. They established the principle of devotional service as the highest end of human life.

But in due course of time the message was forgotten and to some extent even ignored. It is exclusively your dedication to Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that has enabled the supreme secret to become revealed to the world and accessible to all mankind. The whole world is indebted to you forever for the matchless gift you have kindly bestowed.

Here in Nagpur, a city in central India, we are struggling to follow your instructions. We feel very much embarrassed to admit that we have as yet simply exhibited our recurring foolishness, individually as well as collectively, in discharging our devotional service and in our preaching efforts. Still, sometimes we feel ourselves fortunate enough that you have, despite our disqualifications, kindly given us opportunity to remain in devotional service and serve the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we beg at your lotus feet: please bless us so that we may be able to maintain our purity and the purity of your temple.

Your servants in Nagpur, India,  
(written by Rādhā-ramaṇa dāsa)

## Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Actions Speak Louder than Words**

It’s not satisfying anymore  
to make lofty statements of appreciation  
of your greatness and your fame—  
if we have failed in fulfilling your desires,  
if we didn’t act as you expected,  
proving ourselves still foolish, blind, and lame.

It’s not satisfying anymore  
to go on with the same old pace,  
tied up by those selfmade limitations,  
thinking one’s commitment good enough,  
while losing ground in māyā’s race.

It’s not satisfying anymore,  
making it look all right externally  
for the satisfaction of the mind and senses  
and within remaining filled with doubts—  
Kṛṣṇa the Supreme—can it be?

It’s not satisfying anymore  
to lead a life filled with offenses,  
to chant the holy names and not shed tears,  
to see so many living entities in darkness  
and not be capable to open their ears.

It’s not satisfying anymore  
to take so much of your generosity  
while remaining still a kṛpaṇa—  
to repeat what you have said only with the lips  
and stay behind when it comes to showing it  
by one’s own activity.

It’s not satisfying anymore  
to speak of surrender and devotion  
while not giving everything with body, mind, and soul,  
to demand respect and honor only for the dress,  
while inside bewildered still by worldly glitter and promotion.

It’s not satisfying anymore  
just going on maintaining our days  
like living in a cage,  
resigning forever to the fact  
that we are fallen in this age.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
O purifier of the worlds!  
Let us learn to act in purity,  
and let us glorify you forever  
by such deeds.

Your servants at Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, ISKCON’s farm in the Bavarian Forest, Germany,  
(written by Aṣṭaratha dāsa)

## New Dvārakā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead—*ṣaḍ-aiśvarya-bhagavān*—the possessor of six transcendental opulences in full. Moreover, He possesses three kinds of energies: internal (*antar-aṅgā*), marginal (*taṭa-sthā*) and external (*bahir-aṅgā*). The external energy comprises the material energy, from which all the objects of this world are manufactured. The marginal energy constitutes the myriad living entities who are finite spirit souls, or eternal fragmental parts and parcels of Lord Kṛṣṇa. The internal energy is utilized by Kṛṣṇa for the manifestation of His transcendental abode, paraphernalia, etc. This internal potency is also divided into three: *sandhinī*, *saṁvit,* and *hlādinī.* From the *sandhinī* potency are manifested Kṛṣṇa’s mother, father, house, bedding, and so forth. By the *saṁvit* potency Kṛṣṇa knows Himself and causes others to know Him. But *hlādinī* is the potency by which He relishes transcendental bliss and causes His devotees to experience transcendental bliss.

There is a catastrophe of galactic proportions: an incalculable number of souls are in this material world, having lost their original spirituality due to association with matter since time immemorial. Due to this forgetfulness they are suffering birth, death, old age, and disease in a seemingly endless cycle in different species of life. By constitutional nature they should be enjoying eternal life, perfect knowledge, and unlimited bliss, but instead they are perpetually afflicted with death, ignorance, and suffering. There is no hope for them to get out of this predicament except by engaging themselves fully in the devotional service of the Lord. They are, however, unable to do so because of their fallen condition.

Devotional service to Kṛṣṇa should not be considered like the mundane activities of the conditioned souls. Our founder-*ācārya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, has explained, “When the pleasure potency of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is exhibited by His grace in the person of a devotee, that manifestation is called love of God. Love of God is another epithet of the pleasure potency of the Lord. Therefore devotional service reciprocated between the Lord and His devotee is an exhibition of the transcendental pleasure potency of the Lord” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 4.60).

Śrīla Prabhupāda mentions that the capacity for devotional service is manifested in the person of a devotee by Kṛṣṇa’s grace. A little further on in the same purport Śrīla Prabhupāda clarifies, “The *hlādinī* potency gives the Lord all transcendental pleasure, and the Lord bestows such a potency upon His pure devotee” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 4.60). The ability to perform devotional service is bestowed upon the pure devotee by the Lord.

Because Śrīla Prabhupāda is a confidential servitor of the Lord, he is definitely endowed with the internal potency of the Lord. Serving him is therefore the means (*upāya*) for attaining the mercy of Kṛṣṇa: *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ.* “By the grace of the spiritual master one attains the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.” Therefore Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has sung: *kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro, tomāra śakati āche.* “Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the ability to give Kṛṣṇa to me.” By his own causeless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda descended into this mortal world and offered whomever he met the opportunity to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This he did not only in his motherland, India, but all over the world. None of the living entities in this world have the qualification for devotional service, yet Śrīla Prabhupāda has created their qualification—such is the extent of his mercy and potency. And because he knew that he would eventually have to leave his fledgling followers behind in this world, he created a body that would outlive his material body—ISKCON—which would serve as the instrument to continue making his mercy available in perpetuity. On this auspicious occasion we contemplate our good fortune in having the shelter of ISKCON and marvel at the unequalled qualities of our spiritual father, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, without whose grace there would be no hope of salvation for the fallen conditioned souls.

The citizens of New Dvārakā, the ISKCON community in Los Angeles, California,

## New Gokula (Australia)

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of transcendental knowledge.**

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The *śāstras* declare that a disciple is unable to repay the debt he owes his spiritual master. You have given us a life so sublime, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we are happily indebted to you, eternally. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is invaluable, beyond comparison. It comes to us from the spiritual world through the via medium of the *guru-paramparā.*

Many times you explained that the only safe way to come to Kṛṣṇa is to follow in the footsteps of the *mahā-janas: mahā-jano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.* The great souls (*mahā-janas*) are fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, their lives are surrendered to Kṛṣṇa, and their only desire is that every conditioned soul has the opportunity to know, serve, and love Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our shining example of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Through you, we can see; without you, we remain blind. In every respect your life is a perfect example of pure devotion to the Lord. Peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, knowledge, wisdom, and religiousness—these are the qualities by which you worked.

Your strength was amazing, your love was pure, your kindness was humbling, and your compassion universal and based in truth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you clearly said that if we strictly follow your instructions we will go back home, back to Godhead, at the end of this life. Your foremost desire is that we all follow you out of this world of illusion, back to the supreme abode.

Surely we must try to fulfill our obligation to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please, please, please give me just one drop of the vast ocean of love you have for your Lord. Kṛṣṇa is yours, and your fallen disciple is shamelessly begging your mercy.

Your servants at the New Gokula farming community in Millfield, New South Wales, Australia,  
(written by Jaya Śīla dāsa)

## New Gokula (Canada)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement! All glories to your lotus feet!

Where would we all be now if you had not appeared in this material world? Where would we be now if you had not been instructed by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your spiritual master, to preach in the Western countries? Where would any of us be if Śrīmate Morarji had not given you passage on the S.S. *Jaladuta*? What would have become of us if you had not survived the journey across the ocean? What would be our fate by now had you decided to return to India on the *Jaladuta* after spending only a short time here? Some thoughts are just too horrible to contemplate.

What would have happened had we not met you or your disciples? What would have happened to us had we never taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness? What would have happened to us had we never seen your books, heard you speak, seen you lecture, tasted your *prasādam,* or bowed down at your lotus feet?

Would the Berlin Wall still stand? Would the sun still rise in the east? Would life have any meaning? Would we still breathe and live in a wandering, useless, aimless existence devoid of *ātma-tattva*? Would mankind still be surviving? Would we not have perished like a riven rain cloud?

Your mercy is all that we are made of, Śrīla Prabhupāda. With you we can be strong and brave, like a dog protected by its master. With you we can be invincible, like a stick in a bundle of sticks. With you we can conquer the world, like five brothers who have the Lord as their friend. Without you we would wither up. Without you we would fade away. Without you we would sink again into the abyss of *māyā,* covered hopelessly by the waves of the modes of nature, drowning in the ocean of material desires.

By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you came, Śrīla Prabhupāda. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you walked among us. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you accepted us, and by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you gave us your books to read and to distribute. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you gave us our threads, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you put us on the altar, and by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you chanted on our beads. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you made us shave our heads, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you put us in *dhotīs* and *sārīs,* and by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you made us rise early in the morning. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you made us sing, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa you inspired us to dance, and by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa the crows became like swans and peacocks. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa we met you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by the mercy of Your Divine Grace we can meet Lord Caitanya.

All glories to your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your ISKCON movement! All glories to your disciples, and all glories to all of your followers! All glories to your books, all glories to book distribution, all glories to *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and all glories to preaching in every town and village! All glories to your temples, all glories to the wonderful Deities you installed all over the world, and all glories to *prasādam,* which makes you happy when we eat it! All glories to the FATE museums, all glories to the BTG, all glories to the Bhaktivedanta Archives, and all glories to the BBT! All glories to the artists who painted the pictures for your books, all glories to the proofreaders who checked your books, and all glories to the book distributors! All glories to the Māyāpur, Vṛndāvana, and Bombay temples! All glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana here in Vancouver, and all glories to your farm projects all over the world!

And most of all, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your appearance day. Let us glorify you to our heart’s content with renewed vigor and enthusiasm, faith and determination, purity and honesty. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the treasure you have given us that is greater than the greatest gem, greater than the most valuable metal, and greater than millions of dollars. All glories to the *mahā-mantra* and sixteen rounds of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare!

Your eternal servants at the New Gokula community in Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Vipramukhya Swami)

## New Goloka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. You, as *jagad-guru,* have appeared to establish the principles of thinking of Kṛṣṇa always, becoming His devotee, worshiping Him, and bowing down before Him. It is unfortunate that when an *ācārya* such as yourself disappears, rogues and nondevotees tend to take advantage of the situation. Immediately, unauthorized principles are introduced. We beg to be able to relieve the situation by sincerely following your instructions. Otherwise it would be better to die. We desire, therefore, to take up your mission and to execute it properly, laying down our life without personal consideration.

You assure us that you are always present for the disciple who strictly follows your order. You write that the Supersoul immediately appears to the sincere devotee who is purified in heart by following those instructions.

So we beg that our attachment to you goes beyond affectionate sentiment. Let us be firmly attached, without a drop of deviation, to both the spirit and letter of your orders. Please give us the strength to chant our sixteen rounds with humility, to follow the four regulative principles, to rise early and attend the Deities’ *ārati,* to hear *Bhāgavatam,* and to live a life of purity and preaching. We pray that we may please you by our cooperation and our determination. We beg again and again that we may manifest your books as brahminical temple worship, a *varṇāśrama* community, *gurukula,* and compassion for all fallen souls.

Please use us as instruments to fulfill all the desires of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants in the New Goloka Community, Hillsborough, North Carolina,  
(written by Urmilā-devī dāsī on the basis of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports to Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.28.48–51)

## New Govardhana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. On this auspicious day we offer our homage to you. We offer our prayers and extol your glories and virtues.

As Kṛṣṇa’s representative, you demonstrate extraordinary potencies. Your love for Kṛṣṇa is so great that you and Kṛṣṇa are inextricably linked together. Normally conditioned souls develop all kinds of attachments. These attachments cause every conceivable variety of distress. Under the influence of ignorance, the true beauty of the soul becomes transformed. Nasty, spiteful, and greedy, we become weighed down by the cumulative effects of millions of sins. We are suffering under this burden. Adamantly rejecting the Lord’s service, we remain determined to enjoy happiness on the material platform. What fools we are!

Like a visitor to a distant place, you’ve descended and captured our thoughts and given us a glimpse of reality, illuminating our consciousness like a ray of sunlight in a cold, dark, damp place.

Perfection exists in you. Among so much imperfection, you have become manifest. Let us latch on to your lotus feet and grab hold tightly. We need your mercy, and you just keep giving it. Although we have eyes, ears, legs, and a tongue, we remain blind, deaf, crippled, and dumb in comparison with you. However, we are confident that by following in your footsteps and serving you, we may be delivered. We hanker for that day.

Your servants at the New Govardhana community in Murwillumbah, Australia,

## New Kulīnagrāma

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most fallen obeisances on this most celebrated day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your most progressive *saṅkīrtana* movement!

Your appearance in this dark age of Kali-yuga has ushered in a new era of spiritual awakening. You are eternally empowered to reclaim the conditioned souls for the pleasure of Lord Caitanya. Because of your dedication to religious principles, the potency of this process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has been established and recognized, and this is all due to your precept and mercy. Moreover, you personify the sacred tenets of religion, and thus you have infused your dedicated servants with the tenacity to continue what you have so graciously inaugurated. Thus to extol your achievements becomes a rare privilege and an opportunity to thank you for the innumerable gifts you have given us all.

In reciprocation for the sacrifice you have made, we should be conscientious enough to represent you and the pure teachings of the *Vedas* in a tangible way, yet we tend to minimize our representation of you and so become unwitting offenders at your lotus feet.

Here in the Guyana *yātrā* we have experienced your reciprocation in many varied ways. How else could we have accomplished even the insignificant amount we have? The towns and villages are benefiting from the sound vibration of the holy names through *harināma,* the *arcā-vigraha* is being worshiped opulently in the temples here, the festivals attract thousands, and your servants still expound the pure science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As infinitesimal as our service may be, we have full faith that our little steps are prodding us home to Godhead through your mercy. Please, we implore you on this day to allow us to continue assisting you in your glorious preaching activities here in Guyana

Your eternal servants in New Kulīnagrama, Guyana,  
(written by Candrāvalī-devī dāsī)

## New Māyāpura

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Since the day you came to New Māyāpura, this place is certainly no longer an ordinary place. Every one living here or simply passing by can feel the transcendental vibration emanating from here. All that is due to your instructions. You gave us an order to serve our most beautiful Deities—Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda-Mādhava—thus making devotional service accessible to us. Still we struggle from day to day to maintain what you have created. We are trying our best to please you, but we are in the dark Age of Kali, illusioned by a material concept of life. However, after many years we are convinced that you are engaging us in the transcendental pastimes of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. All the troubles we are going through help us to take shelter at the lotus feet of our Deities. Because of our materialistic vision, we have the tendency to become dejected, to fall into criticism and politics. We would like to thank you today particularly for sending some of your best disciples to help us understand the spiritual aspect of New Māyāpura’s tribulations. We don’t have any qualifications, but we know that by following your instructions with faith, we will be engaged in the service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, although we do not deserve such mercy.

Your servants at the New Māyāpura farm community in Valencay, France,

## New Nandagrama

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. And please accept this poem as our offering on this glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day.

At Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara Temple you stayed,  
And for the mercy and blessings of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī you prayed.  
And indeed he appeared in your room,  
And you were inspired to go westwards soon.

In your room under the turning fan,  
You would write and write and show others your plan  
To leave the holy dhāma for “prachar,”  
To tell others about Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara.  
Hastily walking through the crowded Loi Bazaar,  
This time you did not buy dāhl and rice to their surprise,  
But you walked by with a thought in mind  
To bring Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara to humankind.

You crossed the ocean, which showed its fierce face,  
But you safely landed on the order of His Divine Grace.  
By his order you went to bring Vṛndāvana to an unknown land,  
And you did not know if they would understand.

I still remember that glorious day when I saw your face so bright,  
With your orange dhotī reflecting in the setting sun’s light.  
You chanted Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava in great ecstasy,  
Which woke me up from the world of illusion and fantasy.  
Now by hearing Śrīmad Bhāgavatam—it is your translation—  
I hear about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in Vṛndāvana, and in the purports  
I have your association.  
You told us be clean and neat  
And spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness on the street.  
And as we tried day by day,  
Our happiness increased and all our problems went away.  
“I am with you as my Guru Mahārāja is with me,” you said,  
And that is eternally a fact.  
“Just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa” was your simple instruction,  
And I would be free from the world’s destruction.

On your command I have come to the holy land from a far remote  
corner of this earth  
To have a glimpse of the land of Kṛṣṇa’s birth.

Hastily I walked through crowded Loi Bazaa  
To have a glimpse of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara,  
And there in a small room at the side  
I saw your lotus face like soothing moonlight.  
I served you rice, dāhl, sabjī, and capātī while you were looking at  
Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s samādhi.

Now my journey had come to an end:  
I served you in Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma, the holy land.

I am not a poet or writer, as you may read,  
But my intention with this poem to you is to serve your lotus feet.

Your humble servants in New Nandagrama, Bellinzona, Switzerland,  
(written by Sudarśana dāsa)

## New Orleans

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *sahasra-koṭī* prostrated obeisances in the shimmering saffron dust of your divine lotus feet. Warm golden shafts of light shoot out in all directions, and the darkness flees in fear. You are brilliant, effulgent, and beautiful like the sun. The Supreme Lord’s holy names radiate from your lips in the ten directions, and because you are completely pure and they are self-effulgent, they dissipate all darkness born of ignorance. Please bless us that we may become pure and one-pointed in our determination to spread the light of your glorious movement throughout the universe.

Your fallen servants in Saṅkīrtana Purī, New Orleans, Louisiana,

Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## New Tālavana

Stumbling and staggering, bewildered and confused,  
Fighting and arguing, sadistically amused,  
Looking for fun, controlled by our senses,  
Searching for pleasure in the sewers and trenches …

Yes, Kali had claimed us—our resistance was weak,  
Hedonistic joys, our business to seek.  
Eat, drink, and be merry—the rule of this age—  
Had conquered and destroyed many a sage.

Then you came along—a saint beyond compare—  
Directing us away from suffering and despair.  
The words that you spoke cut right through the lies,  
Cleansing our hearts and opening our eyes.

“Surrender to Kṛṣṇa”—your message was clear—  
“Give it all up, you have nothing to fear.”  
You coaxed and you pleaded with compassion divine:  
“Give up this lower taste and experience something sublime.”

Serving your divine feet is not easy to do,  
But the rewards are unlimited to those who stay true.  
We beg for your favor and pray for your smile,  
Hoping to please you through the very last mile.

Your remembrance maintains us to keep us on track,  
Reminding us to serve so we won’t have to come back  
To a place that is both painful and full of despair—  
No place for a gentleman—an intolerable lair.

Each day is a challenge—our minds such a load,  
Only occasionally hankering for Kṛṣṇa’s abode.  
Then you come to mind to remind us of our goal,  
“Don’t waste another life and be an unfortunate soul.”

We thank you for coming and conquering our whims,  
Forcing us to see the malady of our sins.  
Please bless us to be in your service alone  
So Goloka Vṛndāvana will be our next home.

Your servants in the New Tālavana farm community in Carriere, Mississippi,

Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda

## New York

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Searching for truth, looking for friends, we get no satisfaction  
From beginning to the very bitter end.  
Uncalled for, truth beckons at our door,  
The dearest of friends, we feel alone nevermore.  
Revolution of love, Kṛṣṇa consciousness we found out,  
Delivered by mahā-mantra, divine sound emanating, omnipresent,  
Via medium, Lord Gaurāṅga’s lotus mouth.  
Guided through treacherous terrain, Kali’s domain,  
Darkness, quarrel, hypocrisy, short-lived, insane,  
Through one great hope for freedom, peace, happiness and love,  
One ocean of mercy, one illuminating, cooling soothing moon  
On the desolate horizon of this vast desert, so painful,  
hot and dry.  
Your transcendental benefactor is knocking at the door,  
With matchless gifts of kṛṣṇa-prema—the holy name—  
Eternal bliss and knowledge, fear no more.  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, the transcendental autocrat, savior from  
Māyā’s miserable plight, our ever well-wisher and loving father,  
And millions upon millions more.

Daydream and nightdream, a myth you can’t see,  
Prabhupada speaks truth, imparts wisdom,  
distributes love to you and to me.  
Episodes of illusion, a nightmare it seems,  
Birth, death, disease, and old age in Māyā’s prison,  
the fallen souls shall scream.  
Episodes of reality as we awake from bad dreams  
By pure Kṛṣṇa bhakti relief, friend be free.  
With faith in guru, His Divine Grace journeyed west,  
Across the ocean of nescience, Prabhupāda passing Māyā’s tests,  
He delivers Śrī Kṛṣṇa, supreme, absolutely the best.  
Prabhupāda the refuge in whom weary souls find rest.  
A wiser soul we haven’t found.  
Giving Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, Prabhupāda’s glories abound,  
Grace our fellow man, spreading Kṛṣṇa’s holy name  
from town to town.  
Prabhupāda, well-wisher, master, O lord who art living still in sound,  
We desire to serve and please you eternally, with its limits knowing no bounds.

Lost in the masquerade, deceived, wandering in Māyā’s dream  
Like rats in a maze, bewildered and dazed, trapped in Devī’s scheme,  
Foolish, misguided, lazy, waiting in freezing cold, scorching heat, driving rain,  
Deluded by desires to enjoy poison, we abuse the senses,  
live in vain.  
Episode of illusion is confusing our brains,  
Misuse of independence is causing great pains.  
Birth, death, disease, and old age, we must transcend  
this illusory plane.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy purifies and delivers the fallen,  
unfortunate, mundane.  
With Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, Prabhupāda gives us the greatest gain.  
With Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, His Divine Grace purifies our hearts  
and waters the creeper of devotion with transcendental rain.  
And with Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s divine name Śrīla Prabhupāda delivers  
the matchless gift of kṛṣṇa-prema.

Vibrations of love, like a pebble in a stream,  
Expanding from the spirit, never-ending, no more dreams.  
His Divine Grace has compassion and he knows where to apply.  
He has real love and devotion in an unending supply.  
His guidance quite clear and no doubt we comply,  
All obstacles removed on the path to the spiritual sky.  
Always grateful to thee, striving to serve you respectfully,  
As we endeavor to please you eternally.  
Sweeter than spring, Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!  
All the world sings.  
He enlightens us with wisdom, Kṛṣṇa consciousness reigns king.  
The sweet mood of spring, Prabhupāda’s mercy in May,  
Ācārya of all in Kali-yuga’s mundane display.  
We adore His Divine Grace, to our dear father we pray,  
Please forgive our offenses, engage us faithfully today.

Episode of reality, Prabhupāda’s mercy—our favorite scene.  
His Divine Grace distributes apples, quite a wonderful treat,  
Simply wonderful, gulabjamins, cookies, kṛṣṇa-prema so sweet.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, the transcendent chanter and dancer most pure.  
He taught us “the Swami,” step lively, sweet cure.  
Chant and be happy.  
Dance and feel sublime.  
Feast and be satisfied.  
Preach and please Prabhupāda, be elevated to Kṛṣṇa  
consciousness and make your life divine.  
Inspired by your mercy, guru, father, friend, well-wisher,  
we adore thee and your mercy we implore.  
We will guide our lives on the path you reveal for us forevermore.  
Now illusion’s short-lived daydream, nightdream, nevermore.  
Kṛṣṇa consciousness the answer, eternally we’re sure  
Prabhupāda’s devotion unparalleled, His love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa is pure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, everyone loves you. We desire forever to say more …

*cakhu-dān dilo jei janme janme prabhu sei*  
*divya-jñān hṛde prokāśito*

**“He who has given me the gift of transcendental vision is my lord, birth after birth. By his mercy divine knowledge is revealed within the heart.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration. Your glories, like the waves of the ocean, are endlessly manifest. In your first meeting with His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, your beloved Guru Mahārāja requested you to preach the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English-speaking countries. Your Divine Grace took this instruction to heart and intelligently meditated on how to enact a program for offering the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the fallen conditioned souls. In a wonderful story related by Ācārya Prabhākara, your first initiated disciple, you had a transcendental dream in which Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa appeared carrying a *Bhagavad-gītā* in one hand and a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in His other hand. Śrī Kṛṣṇa said, “You must take these books to foreign shores, and you must preach this message.” Śrī Kṛṣṇa was imploring you to deliver His mercy to the unfortunate masses of this miserable material world.

Being directed and protected by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the original father of the Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* movement, you were guided to this hellish, ignorant, passionate abode of Durgā commonly referred to as The Big Apple, The Empire State. The empire of Māyā, the empire of suffering, of hell, of birth, death, disease, and old age.

Your mission’s success was guaranteed not only because you wouldn’t compromise your pure Kṛṣṇa conscious presentation—a sure “success” formula in the world of the cheaters and the cheated—but rather because of your unflinching faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. You were successful also because you possessed in abundance the divine qualities of a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. With those wonderful qualities you attracted many disciples who were willing to give up their lives of material suffering and take enthusiastically to the topmost path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, love and devotion, because they knew you were real, you were sincere, you were their friend, well-wisher, and spiritual guide, and perhaps most important of all they knew you loved them. They found in Your Divine Grace someone they were able to love in the most realistic and meaningful sense of the term.

Back in 1966 there was a large movement of so-called True Seekers who wanted love and peace but instead found only lust and pain. Now, by your mercy, your sincere endeavor and sacrifice, real love and peace is available worldwide. Through your International Society for Krishna Consciousness, millions of fallen conditioned souls are being afforded the opportunity to traverse the true path of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*.

By the distribution of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Śrī Īśopaniṣad, Nectar of Devotion, Kṛṣṇa* book, *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Easy Journey to Other Planets, Perfection of Yoga,* and all your other transcendental writings, love and peace have really become available in every part of the world.

This year marks the twenty-fifth anniversary of the incorporation of ISKCON. We would like to thank you very much for bringing ISKCON into our lives, and we would also like to congratulate you for successfully fulfilling the desires of your beloved Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, and the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and every fallen, unfortunate, confused living entity who comes in contact with and gets the loving mercy of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi through the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. We humbly pray that you allow us to never forget your lotus feet and to assist you as you see fit in the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through this worldwide ISKCON movement.

We beg to remain your servants forever.

Your fallen, insignificant children at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira, New York City.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## Newcastle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances and heartfelt gratitude on this auspicious anniversary of your birth. Your appearance in this world was an inestimable boon for all living entities, as your incredible preaching feats touched the lives of thousands of conditioned souls, and the society you created provides the ever-present opportunity for others to benefit from your sublime association. Your accomplishments were predicted by the great Vaiṣṇava Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura: “A personality will soon appear to preach the teachings of Lord Caitanya and move unrestrictedly over the whole world with His message.” Fulfilling this prophecy and the mission of all the previous *ācāryas,* you founded ISKCON, and in so doing you established a unique worldwide society for propagating spiritual knowledge to all. Only a transcendentally empowered pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa could have accomplished such a task. Needless to say, we are completely and eternally indebted to you. We fervently desire to be true representatives of the wonderful ISKCON that you have created, and to always remain in its merciful shelter.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, savior of the world! Please bestow your causeless mercy upon us.

Your servants in Newcastle, England,

Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda

Brahmī-devī dāsī
