## Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Many years ago as a student I used to read through many books, almost a different one every other day, searching for answers to my metaphysical questions. The great writers, poets, philosophers, and theologians masterfully portrayed the predicament of life, but offered no solution.

Then I turned East—to Buddha, Lao-tse, and the *Bhagavad-gītā—*but found only obscure hints and impossible suggestions. Eventually I met Śrīla Prabhupāda—jolly but grave, lively and wise, and as brilliant as the sun. Although we lived with him and served him as his attentive sons and daughters, he always steered our attention to his writings, which he worked on day after day in the solitary pre-dawn hours, while the world slept.

I gave Śrīla Prabhupāda my most prized possession, my typewriter, which he utilized in typing out his *Gītā* commentary letter by letter. After Śrīla Prabhupāda’s heart stroke, my brother Gargamuni bought a dictating machine so that Śrīla Prabhupāda could compose and then speak his translations and commentaries of the *Gītā* and *Teachings of Lord Caitanya.* The manuscripts were given to me to publish, which we did; and then we distributed the books the best we could in those early days. When I read and studied these precious books, I awoke to the truth—God lives and we can live with Him. When I expressed my understanding, Śrīla Prabhupāda, wide-eyed and somber, declared, “You have understood everything.”

I cannot judge that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are more important than himself, as he asserted, but I do admit that they are equally as important as himself. He saved me both by his sweet, intimate association and through his remarkable purports. Now that Śrīla Prabhupāda is absent from my sight, his books continue to enlighten, revealing the truth of Vedānta, which has escaped almost all pedants. Śrīla Prabhupāda and his writings did what nothing else could—they transformed us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted to create Kṛṣṇa conscious scientists, translators, writers, teachers, poets, pundits, artists, and speakers. The Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies consists of some of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotee-scholars—doctoral theologians and philosophers, students of history, academic researchers, assistants, and editors. Its mission is to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda with our God-given talents, so that he will be recognized and appreciated by the world’s intelligentsia. Śrīla Prabhupāda is accepted by them merely as an authentic practitioner but not as an authoritative exponent or as a thinker who made an outstanding contribution to religious thought. Śrīla Prabhupāda should be acknowledged as the most recent representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Gosvāmīs, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

Traditionally, a *sampradāya ācārya* achieved recognition after having written commentaries on the *prasthāna-trayī—*that is, the three sources of Indian theological epistemology, namely, *nyāya, śruti,* and *smṛti.* The corresponding texts are the *Vedānta-sūtra;* the *Upaniṣads* and Vedic *mantras;* and *Bhagavad-gītā, Mahābhārata, Rāmāyaṇa,* and the *Purāṇas,* respectively. Scholars acknowledge that different schools of Indian thought accord relative importance to the different categories and the respective texts. Māyāvādīs emphasize *nyāya* first, then *śruti,* and discriminate against *smṛti,* while the Vaiṣṇavas regard *smṛti* as vital. Even though Baladeva had written the *Govinda-bhāṣya* commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra,* Śrīla Prabhupāda intended to write his own (see purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 7.121). Also, Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas consider the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* to be a commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra,* written by the same author, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, with both works commencing with the same *śloka.*

In addition to his significant commentaries on the *Bhagavad-gītā* and on most of the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa,* Śrīla Prabhupāda’s third undertaking was the major works of the Vṛndāvana Gosvāmīs, whose core studies and anthologies of the Vedic, Upaniṣadic, and Vedāntic literature—namely the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta—*promulgate the *siddhānta* of Kṛṣṇa *bhakti,* which Lord Caitanya personified. Thus it can be concluded that Śrīla Prabhupāda is qualified to be in the company of the great *sampradāya ācāryas,* such as Śaṅkara, Rāmānuja, Madhva, Nimbarka, Viṣṇusvāmī, Vallabha, and Lord Caitanya.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was scholarly, but not a scholar; religious, but not a religionist; philosophical, but not a philosopher; mystical, but not a mystic; poetic, but not a poet; literary, but not a writer; disciplined, but not a *yogī;* renounced, but not a monk. Śrīla Prabhupāda was not a teacher, a minister, a translator, or a *guru*. Śrīla Prabhupāda did not fit into any one mundane classification, hence the world has so much difficulty in appraising him. Śrīla Prabhupāda was all of these but was, furthermore, a savior, a saint, a *paramahaṁsa,* a sage, an empowered incarnation (*āveśa* and *vibhūti*), and a pure devotee. Śrīla Prabhupāda was not only one thing, but everything; and even something more. Therefore, it is completely appropriate for us to perceive him to be as good as God (*sākṣād-dharitvena*).

This humble servant asks, When will we be “all packed up together” with Śrīla Prabhupāda in the spiritual kingdom of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, as we used to be?

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies,  
(written by Brahmānanda dāsa)

Aghāri dāsa / Agrāhya dāsa / Bill Griffith / Brahmānanda dāsa / Dayānanda dāsa / Duḥkhahantrī-devī dāsī / Garuḍa dāsa / Gopīparaṇādhana dāsa / Joe Gardiner / Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī / Sudharma-devī dāsī / Sundararūpa dāsa / Vaikuṇṭha-devī dāsī

## Padayātrā India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*gaura āmāra, ye sava sthāne*  
*karala bhramana raṅge*  
*se-saba sthena, heriba āmi*  
*praṇayi-bhakata-saṅge*

**"May I visit all the holy places associated with the** ***līlā*** **of Lord Caitanya and His devotees.”**

Śrīla Prabhupada, you wrote, “A devotee should make a point of visiting all the places where Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu performed His pastimes. Indeed, pure devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu even want to see the places He simply visited for only hours or minutes.”

O dear Śrīla Prabhupada, another year has passed, and what have we really done for you? Just walk around India like puffed-up fools! But due to your unlimited, causeless, merciful love, you are giving us your association as we travel together on your Padayātrā, visiting the places where Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu performed His pastimes.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays that he wishes to visit all the holy places associated with Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His devotees. You are fulfilling that wish of his by seriously performing this *saṅkīrtana padayātrā*. Caitanya Mahāprabhu visited thousands of holy places, but unfortunately only a few have been recorded. In 1985 we only visited a few, but during this present tour you have guided us to hundreds of hidden *tīrthas* that were visited by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Lord Nityānanda, and even Lord Balarāma. We cannot even begin to show our true appreciation for such a rare opportunity. Without your guidance and association, what meaning would all these hundreds of *tīrthas* have? Since you are the topmost pure devotee of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you not only saw these holy places for a few minutes, but were allowed to stay for days, or as long as you desired!

Daily, thousands of conditioned souls on the road have your *darśana*. Some are serious and others laugh, but nevertheless you bestow upon them the chance to get Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara’s most merciful blessing. While they stand in front of you in your *mūrti* form, waiting for you to speak, you captivate them all! No matter how difficult or easy the day has been, you are always waiting for them with such compassion, but unfortunately not everyone is ready to accept.

Your plan to spread *padayātrā* around the world is inconceivable. Just continuing this one is hard to believe, what to think of hundreds, thousands, and millions of *padayātrās* in every country! How is it possible? It is beyond our imagination, but certainly not beyond yours.

O Śrīla Prabhupada, please forgive us for all our stupid mistakes, and please continue to give us your *padayātrā* association in every town throughout Bhārata-varṣa. We are totally useless walking fools begging for your loving mercy to help, guide, and inspire us.

Your walking servants in India,

Lokanāth Swami / Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami / Puruṣatraya Swami / Jaya Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Śvetadvīpa dāsa / Jayadeva dāsa / Gaṅgādhāra dāsa / Upendra dāsa / Rasa-nātha dāsa / Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Janaka dāsa / Upananda dāsa / Dāruka dāsa / Vaiṣṇava dāsa / Mādhava dāsa / Ātma-nivedana dāsa / Bhakta Hymo / Bhakta Ajay / Mādhavī-devī dāsī / Rohiṇī-devī dāsī / Praśānta-devī dāsī / Som Bhai / Vashishtaji

Four-legged *padayātrīs*

Burfi / Tiger / Balarāma / Bhīma / Nṛsiṁha / Jaya Vijaya

## Padayātrā America

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is all we are made of. You walked into our hearts, and although you simply passed through this world, still you remain with us.

Now, under your direction, we are also walking everywhere, spreading the holy names of Kṛṣṇa and Nitāi-Gaurasundara. We have been deputized by Your Divine Grace for this topmost activity, the extent of which we have yet to realize. But we know for sure that you are with us every step of the way, touching the hearts of all we introduce you to. That is practical experience to which we can all testify. No one can help but be moved by this straightforward, simple, and beautiful preaching method, which is now our very life and soul.

Following in your lotus footprints, we are assured of all success. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your servants on Padayātrā America,

Viṭṭhaleśvara dāsa / Śaunaka dāsa / Kumāra dāsa / Bhīma dāsa / Rādhikā-ramaṇa dāsa / Jaya Śacī-nandana dāsa / Kṛṣṇa-vilāsinī-devī dāsī / Janaśakti-devī dāsī / Balarāma and Nakula, the oxen

## Radio Kṛṣṇa Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances to your divine lotus feet, under whose sheltering shade I always beg to remain eternally.

By your causeless mercy, on this most auspicious day of your appearance I am still able to join my voice with those of all the devotees of the Supreme Lord in glorifying you, even though you are above the reach of any amount of glorification. In fact, the totality of all glorifications cannot even approach the tip of one of the toes on your lotus feet.

To think this is an exaggeration is a work of a demonic mentality. Someone might say that this sort of adoration should only be given to God, but they ignore the fact that from His position of unchallenged supremacy, the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself worships His pure devotee.

By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa we have been able to meet you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we can safely say that it is only through your causeless mercy that we can become free from this slaughtering mentality that we belong to this material world and that our position is to be the servant of our senses. Only by your association we can reach the safest position—*kṛṣṇa-prema,* love of Godhead.

Sometimes this example is given: If we are in the desert, dying of thirst, and someone brings us water, then the water is certainly good, but the person who brings us the water is even better, and our life belongs to him. So if Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself worships His pure devotee, certainly no one can say that for us to worship His pure devotee is “too much” and will displease the Lord.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our savior and the savior of all; this fact has been realized by everyone. Nonetheless, there is no harm in repeating it. We should never forget how very strong Māyā is and how she can make us think *we* are the saviors of the fallen souls.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the spiritual master, and our most glorious position would be to be able to become like ropes in your lotus hands and, following your instructions, to remain strong and not to break under the strain while you use us to pull all other souls out of the dark hole of material existence. We should never forget, however, that it is *you* pulling the ropes by your strength.

Your movement is full of strong devotees, and if they all cooperate nicely it will be just like twisting all those ropes together into an unbreakable hawser. Then through your ISKCON you’ll pull the whole world out of the dark hole of Māyā’s kingdom and place it like a wonderfully scented flower bouquet at Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, never let me forget you, your wonderful pastimes, or your wonderful instructions. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, keep me always with you. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your worthless servants at Radio Kṛṣṇa Centrale, Terni, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍī dāsa)

## Save the Cow

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

It is stated that if a devotee even once sincerely prays, “From this day on, Kṛṣṇa, I am Yours,” Kṛṣṇa forcibly drags him back to Godhead. So maybe at some point earlier in our devotional careers we innocently prayed like this, and thus you and your dear Lord Kṛṣṇa are lovingly directing us home, even though at the time of our prayer we had even less realization of its import than we do now.

We feel fortunate and proud to be a part of your ISKCON, and in fact we are deeply afraid to do anything but try to serve you in the association of your devotees. We can humbly see that when, in spite of seeming difficulties, we follow our initiation vows, read your books, and serve within the transcendental organization you have created, we are awarded with spiritual insight and intelligence, along with a deepening desire to serve you more and more. To artificially say otherwise would be to blaspheme the very process of spiritual advancement you have given us, although we personally feel unqualified.

Our service is ridden with so many faults, yet we desire to perfect ourselves so that we may become a little bit useful to you in your preaching mission. Thus we humbly thank you for your patience with us, and we pray that we may become firmly fixed at your lotus feet by learning to take your every instruction seriously.

Your servants at Save the Cow, Alachua, Florida,

Brajendra-nandana dāsa / Kāmrā-devī dāsī

## Sussex

To our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your feet.

The Bhaktivedanta Manor temple room. The *japa* period. I sit opposite the famous full-length portrait of you. I remember watching the artist, Gopāla dāsa, paint it—your face emerging from the white canvas. Should he paint an effulgence? You consented. Gopāla dāsa worked quickly and confidently, and you soon stood in *sannyāsa* simplicity amidst a wonderland of natural opulence. Your resolute face gazes at me with both penetrating intensity and kindness, as I try to chant my rounds.

My mind focuses on your Vyāsa-pūjā day in 1973. You sit on your *vyāsāsana* before Their Lordships Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda, whom you had installed the previous day. The large painting, in place on the wall to your right, is covered by a cloth. The special moment arrives. Amidst gasps and “aahs” of appreciation, the picture is unveiled. You meekly look down at your interlocked hands. Do you feel shy, I wonder? Everyone hopes and even expects you to admire such a masterpiece. Suddenly you glance in the direction of the painting, but as quickly you look down again. Your swift glance of acceptance, however, satisfied everyone, especially the artist.

I never saw you look at your painting again.

Hoping to ever be engaged in your service and never let you down,

*Your servants in Sussex, England,*

Rohiṇīnandana dāsa / Rādhānātha dāsa / Rāmāi dāsa / Jīva dāsa / Rādhā-priyā-devī dāsī

## Vaiṣṇava Academy, Varṇāśrama Division

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious of all days, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A small boy, pushed forward by the greatest personalities, feels ecstatic yet unqualified. He prays,

*brahmādayaḥ sura-gaṇā munayo ‘tha siddhāḥ*  
*sattvaikatāna-gatayo vacasāṁ pravāhaiḥ*  
*nārādhituṁ puru-guṇair adhunāpi pipruḥ*  
*kiṁ toṣṭum arhati sa me harir ugra-jāteḥ*

**“How is it possible for me, who have been born in a family of** ***asuras*****, to offer suitable prayers to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead? Even until now, all the demigods, headed by Lord Brahmā, and all the saintly persons could not satisfy the Lord by streams of excellent words, although such persons are very qualified, being in the mode of goodness. Then what is to be said of me? I am not at all qualified” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.9.8).**

A few fortunate souls have recognized the almost inconceivable magnitude of your stature as a world preacher. They understand that you accomplished that which neither the Supreme Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu nor any of His followers before you had done. Occasionally, as a beam of sunlight momentarily breaks through a heavy bank of clouds, we begin to catch some glimpse of your real position.

It is certainly true that anyone who offers you prayers and hears your infinite glories will become purified of all material contaminations even in this most horrible of times. Therefore, although we are eminently unqualified to glorify you, for our purification we humbly offer these words of praise.

Although it is certainly a phenomenal achievement that you translated, published, and distributed millions upon millions of pieces of literature in hundreds of languages throughout the world, presenting the essence and conclusion of all Vedic wisdom, this astounding accomplishment only hints at your glories. A further indication of your incomprehensibly exalted position is the fact that this process is still going strong fourteen years after your manifest pastimes on this earth planet were concluded. But your mission is beyond even this. It is the practical establishment of the very goal of the cream of all Vedic wisdom—*tad vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavaḥ:* to create a real revolution in the impious lives of a misdirected society, showing people the practical ways, means, and examples of a God-centered way of life that can give real shelter to all.

To accomplish this you didn’t have the help of the high-class *brāhmaṇa* families from the cradle of Vedic civilization, Bhāratavarṣa, or even other advanced Vaiṣṇavas (of course, you told us that your spiritual master was always with you, but even that was due to your constant meditation on and dedication to his instructions); rather you engaged the street urchins and lost children inhabiting Māyā’s capital cities in the Western world.

The fact that so much was accomplished is indeed a glowing tribute to your unfathomable potency.

The fact that so much is still undone is a tribute to our ineptitude.

Your vision of temples everywhere, expressed to a “chance” acquaintance on a park bench in 1965, was manifested by your strength of desire during your lifetime. Due to your potency, this manifestation is still existing, even expanding, in spite of so many economic problems and repeated intense attacks by the demonic elements in society.

Your vision of a *daivī-varṇāśrama* society as a revolutionary alternative to this ugrakarmic culture, expressed in 1948 in “Reflections on the *Bhagavad-gītā*” (popularly known as “the Gītā-nāgarī prophecy”), emphasized in your famous *varṇāśrama* walks in 1975, trumpeted in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (“the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is meant for this purpose”—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.10.51), and beseechingly implored in your final days (“And I also want you to organize *varṇāśrama* society. There should a social division.… this will help the people. Don’t waste the human form of life by sinful living. I have already given the ideas in my books.… You are intelligent, all of you. Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, *para-upakāra,* do good unto others; do not exploit them. Any human being has the ability to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Give them the chanting. And also make the situation favorable!”) is now, fourteen years later, still in its *avyakta* stage.

Your transcendental educational system, the only one giving knowledge of the soul and the soul’s relation to the Supreme Soul, Śrī Kṛṣṇa; one which trains children to always think of Kṛṣṇa, become Kṛṣṇa’s devotee, while also teaching moral character and gentlemanly behavior (based on Cāṇakya Paṇḍita’s three elements of a learned man), attracting the children of high-class and pious men from society in general, has gone through a scandalous period and crumbled to a point where it is barely recognizable. Indeed, our abject failure in the area of socialization of the next generation, in terms of the educational aspect of ISKCON and the *varṇāśrama* element, is a screaming indictment of our incompetencies.

But as with your beloved Lord Govinda, your potencies are *acintya-guṇa-svarūpan;* in spite of our gross and subtle mistakes, reluctancies, and out and out obstinacy, still by your grace the creepers of hope are struggling toward the sunshine, and the buds of success are starting to open.

A beautiful mud-hut *gurukula* village, filled with bright-faced students chanting *mantras* and performing fire sacrifices morning and evening, blossoms in Māyāpur. A nineteen-year-old American boy who has known no home other than this *gurukula* since 1984 takes initiation and is thankful he has never returned to America.

A group of dedicated men and mothers, working for years to raise the money, “liberate” some land in British Columbia and begin building houses (without electricity!) and talk seriously of self-sufficiency. A French Canadian family decides to move there: “After all, this is what Prabhupāda wanted the householders with families to do.”

Small groups of mature, thoughtful men in North Carolina, California, New England, Pennsylvania, Florida, and Mississippi discuss strategies for implementing *varṇāśrama* communities; the economics, management structure, social systems, etc. You told us once that everything begins with sound vibrations.

And here, on the New Ramaṇa Reti farm in North Central Florida, a fourteen-year-old boy designs the cover page of the farm newsletter on a state-of-the art computer, while another plows and disks the fields, getting them ready for the planting of the hay our cows and oxen will need; a teenage boy helps finish building the covered wagon for American Padayātrā, while a different teenage girl each day assists the mother who cooks lunch for the seventy devotees who eat here. A group of teenage boys “sneak” into the temple for the Sunday *āratis* to play *mṛdaṅgas* and *karatālas* for the Deities, when they aren’t even required to attend … just for fun. And a group of men begin to regularly meet to talk of growing everything we need, reducing our energy consumption, and accelerating a movement toward establishing a *varṇāśrama* village. And so, in so many places, amidst the ashes of mistakes and disasters, by your potency, the phoenix of success begins to raise its head.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most important of all days, we of the Varṇāśrama College division of the Vaiṣṇava Academy pray to you that you allow us to become your instruments in fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s desires to spread this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement all over the world, to become small cogs in the great wheel of mercy that you are raining down upon all the condition souls, and thus to perfect our lives. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything!

The students, teachers, parents, and friends of the Varṇāśrama Division of the Vaiṣṇava Academy in Alachua, Florida.  
(written by Muralī-vādaka dāsa)

## Vaiṣṇava Academy, Girls’ Gurukula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

The students and teachers of the Vaiṣṇava Academy for Girls, in Alachua, Florida, want to express our great appreciation for everything you have done for us.

Some of us are quite young, but we all wanted to write an offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā. Each of us has written one stanza of this poem. Just as we worked together to do this, we are praying to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi that we will be able to work together to spread your glories and help each other to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
our very special friend.  
You’ve established gurukula  
so our rebirth will end.

How to raise your children  
so you’ll be pleased and proud?  
We scratch our heads, read your books,  
and sincerely pray out loud.

Though we are not so learned,  
we’ll work to understand  
the message of Gaurāṅga.  
Please give a helping hand.

You blessed this earthly planet—  
in September you appeared.  
You brought us love of Godhead,  
and all our doubts you cleared.

Gurudeva, you are sublime,  
giving Bhagavad-gītā.  
At your feet we lay our lives.  
You are our grand grand-pita.

Dear beloved grandfather,  
we’re all glad you came.  
We want to surrender.  
Please help us to maintain.

Hearts refreshed, mind at rest,  
we’re free to praise Śyāmasundara.  
Gurudeva, to you we pray,  
may we grow in full surrender.  
With your smile and warmth  
you drew us to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
What more could we ask for?  
Just bhaja Govinda!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
thank you for Gaura-Nitāi.  
They look so beautiful,  
like nectar to the eye.

You even risked your very life  
to save us from this world.  
All we have to do is chant,  
and our consciousness will build.

You gave your causeless mercy  
to me, a fallen soul.  
I’ll stay upon the right path,  
keeping Kṛṣṇa as my goal.

For as long as we live,  
we all promise to spread  
kṛṣṇa-prema to all jīvas  
and follow what you said.

Ever fresh and ever new,  
let us work together.  
Perfect our lives by pleasing you—  
there is no nectar better.

*Your loving servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy, Girls’ Gurukula,*

Mother Ākuti-devī dāsī / Mother Mālinī-devī dāsī / Mother Mīnā-devī dāsī / Mother Lakṣmīmaṇi-devī dāsī / Annapūrṇā-devī dāsī / Caitanya-devī dāsī / Gopālanandinī-devī dāsī / Kṛṣṇa-līlā-devī dāsī / Nitāi-devī dāsī / Raghunandinī-devī dāsī / Sevāniṣṭhā-devī dāsī / Viṣṇu-bhakti-devī dāsī / Viṣṇupriyā-devī dāsī

## Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (1)*

**The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.**

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-*  
*vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (2)*

**Chanting the holy name, dancing in ecstasy, singing, and playing musical instruments, the spiritual master is always gladdened by the saṅkīrtana movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because he is relishing the mellows of pure devotion within his mind, sometimes his bodily hairs stand on end, he feels quivering in his body, and tears flow from his eyes like waves. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāṁś ca niyuñjato ’pi*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (3)*

**The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the Deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other, similar worship of the Lord. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (4)*

**The spiritual master is always offering Kṛṣṇa four kinds of delicious food \[analyzed as that which is licked, chewed, drunk and sucked\]. When the spiritual master sees that the devotees are satisfied by eating bhagavat-prasādam, he is satisfied. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-*  
*mādhurya-līlā-guṇa-rūpa-nāmnām*  
*prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (5)*

**The spiritual master is always eager to hear and chant about the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava, and about Their qualities, names and forms. The spiritual master aspires to relish these at every moment. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (6)*

**The spiritual master is very dear, because he is expert in assisting the gopīs, who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (7)*

**The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\].**

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (8)*

**By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.**
